<SYSTEM>This is the abridged developer documentation for Caplo</SYSTEM>

# Add an agent to your website

> The short path from a published agent release to a chat widget on your site.

Once an agent has a published release, three things put it on your website: a published widget design, a website channel that serves the release, and the channel’s embed code on your pages. This page is the short version; [Install the widget](/channels/website-widget/install/) covers every option. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * A published agent release. If you do not have one yet, follow [Build your first agent in the console](/get-started/quickstart-console/) or [Build your first agent with the CLI](/get-started/quickstart-cli/). * The **Owner**, **Admin** or **Builder** role, which can configure channels and widgets. * Your website’s origin: the scheme and host, without a path, such as `https://www.example.com`. For local development you can use `http://localhost:3000`. ## Steps [Section titled “Steps”](#steps) 1. **Publish a widget design.** In the console, open **Widget designs** and select **Create design**. Name it, select **Create design**, and adjust colours, logo and welcome screen if you like. Select **Save draft**, then in **Publish and install** select **Publish revision 1** and confirm. See [Design the widget](/channels/website-widget/design/). 2. **Create a website channel.** Open **Channels**, select **Create channel**, then **Live chat widget**. Enter a **Channel name**, choose the **Agent** and the **Agent release** to serve, and select **Set up connection**. 3. **Allow your website.** Enter your site in **Allowed website origin**. To add another origin, such as a staging site, select **Add origin**. Choose the **Widget design** and select **Create channel**. The widget refuses to load on any origin you have not listed. 4. **Apply the design.** Caplo opens the new website entry point. On the **General** tab, under **Appearance**, choose the design’s published revision and select **Apply revision 1**. Applying takes effect right away. 5. **Copy the embed code.** On the **Install** tab, select **Copy embed code**. It looks like this: 
   ```html
   <script
     src="https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js"
     data-widget-id="<WIDGET_ID>"
     async
   ></script>
   ```
 A design with the inline layout adds a `<div>` that marks where the chat appears; copy both parts. 6. **Add it to your site.** Paste the code into your site’s HTML just before `</body>`, on every page that should show the widget. In a site builder or CMS, use its custom code or footer script setting. 7. **Check it.** Load a page on an allowed origin. The launcher appears, and opening it shows the agent’s greeting. ## After you go live [Section titled “After you go live”](#after-you-go-live) * **New releases do not reach the website on their own.** When you publish a new agent release, open the channel and select **Update to** the new version (or **Change release** to pick any release), or run `caplo channel deploy "<CHANNEL_NAME>"`. New conversations use the new release; conversations in progress keep the one they started with. See [Channels](/concepts/channels/). * **The embed code stays the same** when you apply a new design revision, unless you switch between the popup and inline layouts. * **Conversations** appear in the console under **Conversations**, where your team can read them and take over. See [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/). If something does not work, see [Troubleshoot the widget](/channels/website-widget/troubleshooting/).

# Build your first agent with the CLI

> Install the caplo CLI, scaffold a multi-agent project with tools, test it, and publish it from your terminal.

With the `caplo` CLI you keep agents and tool packages as files in your own repository and publish them from your terminal or CI. This quickstart scaffolds the **summit** example: an outdoor gear shop with a concierge agent that routes customers to three specialist agents, plus two tool packages with mock data. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * A Caplo account. If you have never signed in, sign in once at [app.caplo.si](https://app.caplo.si); Caplo creates a workspace for you. * The **Owner** or **Admin** role in the workspace. The project pushes tool packages and agents and publishes both, which no other role can do alone. See [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/). * Node.js 24 and npm, to install the tool packages’ dependencies. * **Fast decisions** turned on for the workspace. The summit concierge uses fast routing to pick a specialist, and an agent with fast routing cannot publish without it. An Owner or Admin turns it on in the console under **Settings** > **AI providers** > **Fast decisions**. See [Fast decisions](/providers/fast-decisions/). ## Create and publish the project [Section titled “Create and publish the project”](#create-and-publish-the-project) 1. **Install the CLI.** On macOS or Linux: 
   ```sh
   brew install getcaplo/tap/caplo
   ```
 Or install it to `~/.local/bin` without Homebrew: 
   ```sh
   curl -fsSL https://cli.caplo.si/install.sh | sh
   ```
 See [Install the CLI](/cli/install/) for Windows, version pinning and verifying signatures. 2. **Sign in.** 
   ```sh
   caplo auth login
   ```
 Your browser opens to sign in; on a machine without a browser, use `caplo auth login --device`. If you belong to one workspace, the CLI selects it. Otherwise pick one: 
   ```sh
   caplo workspace list
   caplo workspace use "<WORKSPACE_NAME>"
   ```
 `caplo auth status` shows who you are signed in as and which workspace commands act on. 3. **Scaffold the project.** 
   ```sh
   caplo init shop --template summit
   cd shop
   ```
 The project name comes from the directory and becomes part of each agent’s name. You get: | Path | Contents | | ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `caplo.yaml` | The project file. It finds agents in `agents/*.yaml` and tool packages in `tools/*`. | | `agents/concierge.yaml` | **Shop Concierge**, the entry agent. It routes to the three specialists with fast routing. | | `agents/orders.yaml` | **Shop Orders**: looks up orders and shipments and starts returns. | | `agents/gear.yaml` | **Shop Gear**: recommends products and checks store stock. | | `agents/returns.yaml` | **Shop Returns**: answers return and warranty policy questions from its instructions. | | `tools/order-desk/` | An MCP server package with mock data: `lookup_order`, `track_shipment`, `search_products` and `check_return_eligibility` (reads) and `create_return` (a write that the customer must confirm). | | `tools/store-stock/` | A function package with one read tool, `check_store_stock`. | Every agent file includes eval cases. The agents use an Anthropic model; if your workspace cannot use it, change `model.name` in each agent file. See [Instructions and model](/agents/instructions-and-model/). 4. **Install the tool dependencies.** Each package needs its dependencies and a lockfile before Caplo can build it. 
   ```sh
   (cd tools/order-desk && npm install)
   (cd tools/store-stock && npm install)
   ```
 5. **Validate the project.** This runs offline and checks the files against the schemas, every tool and sub-agent reference, and the sub-agent links. 
   ```sh
   caplo validate
   ```
 You can also run the tool packages’ test fixtures locally with `caplo test`. 6. **Push the drafts.** 
   ```sh
   caplo push
   ```
 The CLI uploads and builds both tool packages, then saves each agent as a draft, specialists before the concierge. Nothing is published yet and no customer can reach the agents. Agents and packages are matched by name, so pushing again updates them. 7. **Chat with the draft.** 
   ```sh
   caplo agent chat "Shop Concierge"
   ```
 Type one message per line, for example `Where is my order SO-1002?` or `Can you recommend a lightweight two-person tent?` Each reply shows the sub-agents and tools the turn used. When the agent proposes a write such as `create_return`, the CLI shows what it will do; type `/confirm` to approve it or `/cancel` to decline. Ordinary text never approves a write. End the chat with `/exit` or Ctrl-D. This is a private test conversation, but tools run for real. The summit tools only use mock data. 8. **Preview the publish.** 
   ```sh
   caplo publish --dry-run
   ```
 The plan lists what will be published, in order: the tool packages, then the specialists, then the concierge, pinned to the specialists’ new releases. 9. **Publish.** A project’s first publish to a workspace needs `--yes`, after you have reviewed the plan: 
   ```sh
   caplo publish --yes
   ```
 For each agent the CLI pins the exact tool versions, runs its eval cases, and creates a release. Every eval case must pass unless your workspace has made evals advisory. If a case fails, the CLI prints why and stops with exit code 3; fix the agent file or the case and run `caplo publish` again. Later publishes show the plan and ask before going ahead. Your agents now have releases in the workspace, and you can see them under **Agents** in the console. ## Put it on your website [Section titled “Put it on your website”](#put-it-on-your-website) Channels are created in the console. Follow [Add an agent to your website](/get-started/add-to-your-website/) and choose **Shop Concierge** as the channel’s agent. After that, publish new releases with `caplo publish` and move the channel to them with `caplo channel deploy`; see [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/). ## Let an AI assistant do it [Section titled “Let an AI assistant do it”](#let-an-ai-assistant-do-it) AI prompt Scaffold and publish a Caplo agent project with the CLI Creates a Caplo project from the summit template, pushes and tests it, then publishes it after asking you. editing, pushing and publishing: [CLI workflow](/cli/workflow/). * Every field of an agent file: [Agent YAML](/agents/agent-yaml/) and the [agent YAML reference](/reference/agent-yaml/). * Writing your own tool package: [Write your first tool](/tools/first-tool/). * Commands used here: [`caplo init`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-init), [`caplo push`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-push), [`caplo publish`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-publish), [`caplo agent chat`](/reference/cli/agent/#caplo-agent-chat).

# Build your first agent in the console

> Create an agent in the Caplo console, test it, publish a release, and put it on your website.

This walkthrough takes you from a new account to an agent answering on your website. It takes about 15 minutes and needs no code beyond pasting one snippet into your site. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * You need a role that can build agents (**Owner**, **Admin** or **Builder**). Publishing a release needs **Owner** or **Admin**. See [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/). * To install the widget, you need to be able to edit your website’s HTML, and to know its origin, for example `https://www.example.com`. ## Create and test the agent [Section titled “Create and test the agent”](#create-and-test-the-agent) 1. **Sign in.** Go to [app.caplo.si](https://app.caplo.si) and sign in with your email, or with **Continue with company SSO** if your company uses single sign-on. The first time you sign in, Caplo creates a workspace for you and makes you its Owner. If a colleague invited you, open the invitation link instead and you join their workspace. 2. **Create an agent.** In the sidebar, open **Agents** (under **Build**) and select **Create agent**. Enter an **Agent name**, such as `Customer support`, and optionally a **Description**, then select **Create draft**. The agent opens on its **Setup** tab. 3. **Write the instructions and greeting.** Under **Instructions**: * In **Agent instructions**, describe who the agent is, what it helps with, its tone, and what it must not do. For example: “You are the support assistant for Example Outfitters. Answer questions about our products, shipping and returns. Keep answers short. If you do not know something, say so and offer to connect the customer with our team.” * In **Greeting message**, write the first message customers see, such as “Hi, I’m the Example Outfitters assistant. How can I help?” A greeting is required before you can test or publish. Open **Advanced settings** to check the **Model**. A new agent starts with a model your workspace can use; see [Instructions and model](/agents/instructions-and-model/) to change it. 4. **Save the draft.** Select **Save draft** at the top of the page. Testing works on unsaved edits, but publishing uses the saved draft. 5. **Test it.** The **Live test** panel sits beside the editor. The first time, read the notice, select **I understand this test can use real tools and change business data.**, then **Enable live test**. Type a few questions a customer would ask and check the answers. After you change the agent, select **Apply changes** or **New chat** to test the new version. ## Add eval cases [Section titled “Add eval cases”](#add-eval-cases) By default a workspace requires every agent to pass its eval cases before it can publish, so an agent with no cases cannot publish. An eval case is a customer message plus the outcome you expect; Caplo runs it against the draft and a grader model checks the result. 1. Open the **Test** tab and select **Add case**. 2. Fill in **Case name**, **Customer message** (for example, “Do you ship to Canada?”) and **Desired customer outcome** (for example, “Says whether we ship to Canada without inventing prices or delivery times”). Optionally add **Response checks** such as **Answer must contain**. 3. Select **Save case**. Add two or three cases that cover the questions you care about most. If an Owner or Admin has set the **Eval requirement** to **Disabled** in **Settings** > **General** > **Agent publication requirements**, eval results are advisory and you can skip this section. See [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/) and [Write evals](/agents/evals/). ## Publish a release [Section titled “Publish a release”](#publish-a-release) 1. Select **Publish agent**. The dialog shows **Publish v1.0.0** and checks the saved draft. 2. Leave **Version bump** as it is for the first release, and write a short **Release note**, such as “First release”. A release note is required. 3. Under **Agent evals**, select **Run tests** and wait for the cases to finish. If a case fails, open it to see why, close the dialog, adjust the instructions or the case, save, and try again. 4. When the checks pass, select **Publish version**. The release is now frozen. Editing the agent changes only its draft until you publish again. See [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/). ## Put the agent on your website [Section titled “Put the agent on your website”](#put-the-agent-on-your-website) A website channel needs a published widget design, a channel that serves your release, and the embed code on your site. 1. **Create a widget design.** Open **Widget designs** (under **Deploy**) and select **Create design**. Enter a **Design name**, select **Create design**, adjust the look if you like, then select **Save draft**. In the **Publish and install** section, select **Publish revision 1** and confirm. Only published revisions can be applied to a website. 2. **Create the channel.** Open **Channels** (under **Deploy**), select **Create channel**, then **Live chat widget**. Enter a **Channel name**, choose your **Agent** and its **Agent release**, then select **Set up connection**. 3. **Allow your website.** In **Allowed website origin**, enter your site’s origin without a path, such as `https://www.example.com`. The widget only loads on the origins you list. Choose your **Widget design**, then select **Create channel**. 4. **Apply the design.** Caplo opens the channel’s website entry point. (To come back to it later, open the channel and select **Configure & install**.) On the **General** tab, under **Appearance**, choose the design and its revision, then select **Apply revision 1**. 5. **Copy the embed code.** On the **Install** tab, select **Copy embed code**. If the button is unavailable, the tab tells you what to do first, such as applying a design or saving your edits with **Save entry point**. Paste the code into your site’s HTML, just before the closing `</body>` tag, on every page where the widget should appear. 6. **Check it.** Open your site. The chat launcher appears; open it and the agent greets you. If the widget does not appear, see [Troubleshoot the widget](/channels/website-widget/troubleshooting/). For layouts, Content Security Policy and single-page apps, see [Install the widget](/channels/website-widget/install/). ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * Give the agent documents to answer from: [Knowledge](/agents/knowledge/). * Let it look things up and take actions: [Tools](/agents/tools/). * Watch conversations and take over when needed: [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/). * Publish a new release and move the channel to it: [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/).

# What is Caplo

> What Caplo is for, the building blocks you work with, and which tool to use for each job.

Caplo is a platform for building AI agents that talk to your customers. An agent answers from documents you approve, looks things up and takes actions through tools you connect, and serves customers on your website, in WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram, and over the phone. You build and test agents in a private draft, publish them as fixed releases, and decide which release each channel serves. ## Building blocks [Section titled “Building blocks”](#building-blocks) | Block | What it is | | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Workspace | Your team’s space in Caplo. It holds every agent, tool, knowledge base, channel and conversation, plus members, roles, provider keys and billing. | | Agent | An assistant with instructions, a greeting, a model, and optionally knowledge bases, tools and sub-agents. | | Draft | The editable version of an agent. You change and test the draft; customers never talk to it. | | Release | A frozen, numbered copy of a draft. Publishing creates a release and pins the exact tool and knowledge versions it uses. | | Tool package | Code that gives agents tools, such as looking up an order or booking an appointment. You can also connect a remote MCP server. Each published version is immutable. | | Knowledge base | Documents you upload, review and approve. Published versions are what agents search to answer questions. | | Channel | Where customers reach an agent: a website widget, a WhatsApp number, a Messenger page, an Instagram account or a phone number. A channel serves one agent release. | | Conversation | One customer’s chat or call on a channel. It keeps the release it started with, and your team can read it and take over. | ## How they fit together [Section titled “How they fit together”](#how-they-fit-together) 1. You create an agent in a workspace and write its instructions and greeting. 2. You attach published knowledge bases and tools, and link other agents as sub-agents if one agent should delegate to specialists. 3. You test the draft in a private test chat and add eval cases: example customer messages with the outcome you expect. 4. You publish. Caplo checks the draft, runs the eval cases, pins every tool and knowledge version, and creates an immutable release. 5. You point a channel at that release. New conversations on the channel use it; conversations already in progress keep the release they started with. 6. You watch conversations in the console, step in when a person is needed, and publish new releases as you improve the agent. Read [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/) for why releases are frozen, and the other [Concepts](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/) pages for each block in more depth. ## Console, CLI and Tool Studio [Section titled “Console, CLI and Tool Studio”](#console-cli-and-tool-studio) You can do most work in more than one place. Pick the one that suits the job. | Tool | Where | Use it to | | ----------- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Console | [app.caplo.si](https://app.caplo.si) | Create and test agents, upload knowledge, design and install the website widget, connect channels, read conversations and take over, invite members, manage provider keys, usage and settings. | | `caplo` CLI | Your terminal and CI | Keep agents and tool packages as files in your own repository, then validate, push, chat with, evaluate and publish them. Also deploy channels to new releases and sync knowledge from a folder. | | Tool Studio | [studio.caplo.si](https://studio.caplo.si) | Write, build, test and publish tool packages in a browser-based code editor, without installing anything. | The three share one workspace. An agent pushed from the CLI shows up in the console, and a tool published from Tool Studio can be attached to an agent in either. ## Where to go next [Section titled “Where to go next”](#where-to-go-next) * [Quickstart in the console](/get-started/quickstart-console/): create, test and publish an agent, then add it to a website. * [Quickstart with the CLI](/get-started/quickstart-cli/): scaffold a multi-agent project with tools and publish it from your terminal. * [Add an agent to your website](/get-started/add-to-your-website/): the short path from a published agent to a working widget. * [Write your first tool](/tools/first-tool/): give an agent access to your own systems.

# Agents and releases

> How an agent's editable draft becomes an immutable release, what a release pins, and how releases reach customers.

Every agent has one **draft** and any number of **releases**. You edit and test the draft. Customers only ever talk to a release. ## The draft [Section titled “The draft”](#the-draft) The draft holds the agent’s current configuration: its name, instructions, greeting, model, knowledge bases, tools, sub-agents and conversation settings. Saving it (**Save draft** in the console, or `caplo push` from a project) stores it in the workspace, where anyone who can build agents can change it. You can test a draft at any time in the console’s **Live test** panel or with `caplo agent chat`, without any channel. Editing the draft never changes a published release. The console marks an agent with **Unpublished changes** when its saved draft differs from its latest release. ## Publishing a release [Section titled “Publishing a release”](#publishing-a-release) Publishing turns the saved draft into a release. An Owner or Admin publishes from **Publish agent** in the console or with `caplo publish` or `caplo agent publish`. Caplo then: 1. Checks the configuration, such as the greeting, the model and every reference. 2. Pins the newest published version of each tool package the agent uses, and the newest published version of each knowledge base it attaches. 3. Runs the agent’s eval cases against this exact candidate and applies the workspace’s publication requirements. See [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/). 4. Checks that the agent’s instructions, tools and knowledge fit within what its model can handle. 5. Freezes the result as a numbered release with a semantic version (`1.0.0`, then a major, minor or patch bump) and your release note. If any step fails, nothing is published. ## What a release pins [Section titled “What a release pins”](#what-a-release-pins) A release is immutable. It records: * The configuration as it was when published. * The exact tool package versions. A newer tool version reaches customers only when you publish the agent again. * The exact knowledge base versions. Publishing a new knowledge version does not change live answers until the agent is published again. * The exact release of each sub-agent. See [Multi-agent](/concepts/multi-agent/). Eval cases are not part of a release. You can change them without republishing, and the next publish runs the current set. ## How releases reach customers [Section titled “How releases reach customers”](#how-releases-reach-customers) A [channel](/concepts/channels/) serves one specific release. Publishing a new release does not move any channel to it; you choose when: * In the console, open the channel and select **Update to** the new version, or **Change release** to pick any release. * With the CLI, run `caplo channel deploy` for the channel, or give the project an environment with `channels` so that `caplo publish` deploys them. New conversations on the channel start on the new release. **Conversations already in progress keep the release they started with** until they end, so a customer is never switched mid-conversation. To go back, point the channel at an earlier release with **Change release** or `caplo channel rollback`. The earlier release is served exactly as it was published. ## Revoking a release [Section titled “Revoking a release”](#revoking-a-release) Revoking permanently stops a release from running: no channel and no parent agent can run it again. Use it when a release is unsafe, for example if it exposes a tool it should not. Revoke with `caplo agent release revoke <AGENT> <RELEASE> --reason "<REASON>"`. A revocation cannot be undone, so move channels to another release first. ## Archiving and deleting agents [Section titled “Archiving and deleting agents”](#archiving-and-deleting-agents) Archiving removes an agent from the active library while keeping its drafts, releases and history. Detach it from its channels and remove links from parent agents first. You can delete an agent only while it has never been published. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/) * [Test an agent](/agents/testing/) * [Agent YAML](/agents/agent-yaml/)

# Channels

> What channels and entry points are, which kinds of channel Caplo supports, and how a channel serves, updates and rolls back an agent release.

A **channel** is how customers reach an agent. It connects one or more **entry points**, such as a widget on your website or a WhatsApp number, to one published release of one agent. You manage channels under **Channels** in the console. ## Kinds of channel [Section titled “Kinds of channel”](#kinds-of-channel) | Channel | Entry point | Conversations | | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | | **Live chat widget** | The Caplo widget, installed on your website with an embed snippet and limited to the website origins you allow. | Text, and optionally voice in the browser. | | **WhatsApp Business** | A WhatsApp Business phone number connected through Meta. | Text messages. | | **Facebook Messenger** | A Facebook Page connected through Meta. | Text messages. | | **Instagram** | An Instagram professional account connected through Meta. | Text messages. | | **Telephone** | A phone number from your Twilio or Telnyx account. | Voice calls. | For a website, you only need a published widget design; see [Add an agent to your website](/get-started/add-to-your-website/). Messaging and phone channels first need the account connected under **Integrations** > **Communication channels**. See the [channels overview](/channels/). ## A channel serves one release [Section titled “A channel serves one release”](#a-channel-serves-one-release) Each channel names an agent and a specific release of it. That agent is the channel’s **entry agent**: it greets the customer and may delegate to sub-agents (see [Multi-agent](/concepts/multi-agent/)). Because the channel names a release rather than “the latest”, publishing a new agent release never changes what customers get on its own. You decide when each channel moves. ## Deploying and rolling back [Section titled “Deploying and rolling back”](#deploying-and-rolling-back) | To | In the console | With the CLI | | ------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | | Move to the newest release | Open the channel and select **Update to** the new version | `caplo channel deploy "<CHANNEL>"` | | Choose a specific release | **Change release** | `caplo channel deploy "<CHANNEL>" --agent "<AGENT>@<VERSION>"` | | Go back to the previous release | **Change release** | `caplo channel rollback "<CHANNEL>"` | Whichever way you deploy: * **New conversations** start on the new release straight away. * **Conversations in progress** keep the release they started with until they end. A conversation ends after the channel’s inactivity timeout, which is 24 hours unless you change it. * Rolling back serves the earlier release exactly as it was published; to move forward again, deploy or publish again. A CLI project can also deploy channels as part of `caplo publish`, by listing them under an environment’s `channels`. See [Environments](/cli/environments/). ## Channel settings [Section titled “Channel settings”](#channel-settings) Besides the release, a channel’s entry points carry settings that apply to every conversation on them, such as allowed website origins, whether voice is allowed, the inactivity timeout, and conversation actions like transferring to a person or ending a call. Changes to a channel are saved as new revisions, and settings that shape a conversation apply to conversations that start after the change. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Channels overview](/channels/) * [Install the website widget](/channels/website-widget/install/) * [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/) * [`caplo channel deploy`](/reference/cli/channel/#caplo-channel-deploy)

# Conversations and handoff

> What a conversation is, how your team reads and takes over conversations in the console, and how an agent hands a customer to a person.

A **conversation** is one customer’s chat or call on a channel. It starts when the customer first writes or calls, keeps the agent release and channel settings it started with, and ends after the channel’s inactivity timeout. Test chats in the console and the CLI are private and do not appear among your channel conversations. ## The console inbox [Section titled “The console inbox”](#the-console-inbox) **Conversations** (under **Monitor**) lists calls and chats from every channel. You can filter by channel, status and date, or show only conversations that were **Handed off**. Opening one shows the transcript, the customer’s reply ratings if the agent collects them, and the tools behind each reply. Who sees what depends on the [role](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/): * Owners, Admins, Builders, Supervisors, Auditors and Viewers can read conversations. * The customer’s identity is hidden as **Participant hidden** until someone with permission selects **Reveal** and gives a reason. Every reveal is recorded in the workspace audit log. * Tool activity behind a reply is visible to Supervisors, Admins and Owners. * **Internal notes** are visible only to your team, never to the customer or the agent. ## Taking over [Section titled “Taking over”](#taking-over) Owners, Admins and Supervisors can step into an open text conversation at any time. Select **Take over**: the agent stops replying, the transcript notes “A member of our team has joined and will reply here.” (website visitors see it; on WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram it is not sent), and you reply from the console. When you are done, select **Return to agent** and the agent answers the customer’s next message. Taking over needs no setup. Voice calls cannot be taken over from the console. ## Handoff to a person [Section titled “Handoff to a person”](#handoff-to-a-person) An agent can also decide to hand a conversation to your team, for example when the customer asks for a person or the request is outside what the agent should handle. This works only when the channel’s entry point allows the **transfer** conversation action and names a destination: | Channel | Destination | What happens | | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Website widget | A Chatwoot inbox, connected under **Integrations** > **Handoff destinations** | The conversation opens in Chatwoot with a summary, and messages are relayed both ways until your team finishes. WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram conversations are mirrored into Chatwoot instead; see [Handoff to Chatwoot](/channels/handoff-to-chatwoot/). | | Telephone | A phone number or SIP address set in **Transfer calls to** | The call is transferred, either straight away (cold) or after briefing the person who answers (warm). | What the customer sees during a text handoff: 1. When the agent hands off: “I’ve asked a member of our team to join. They’ll reply here as soon as they can.” 2. If nobody accepts in time: “No one from our team is available right now, so I’ll keep helping here.” The agent carries on. 3. When your team finishes: “Our team has finished here. I’m back to help with anything else.” In the console inbox, a handed-off conversation shows **External inbox** while the destination has it. Without a handoff destination, the agent cannot hand off on its own, but your team can still take over from the console. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Handoff and end actions](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/): set up transfers and destinations. * [Channels](/concepts/channels/) * [Tools and actions](/concepts/tools-and-actions/): what happens when a write’s outcome needs a person to check.

# Evaluations

> How eval cases test an agent before it publishes, how the grader decides, and how workspace publication requirements gate releases.

**Eval cases** are example conversations that check an agent behaves as you expect. Each agent has one set of cases, its **eval suite**. Caplo runs the suite against the exact draft you are about to publish, and by default an agent publishes only when every case passes. Evals turn “it seemed fine when I tried it” into a check that runs on every release. ## What a case contains [Section titled “What a case contains”](#what-a-case-contains) A case is one customer message plus the checks it must pass. You write cases on the agent’s **Test** tab in the console, or under `evals` in the agent’s YAML file. | Check | Console field | What passes | | --------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Desired outcome | **Desired customer outcome** | A grader model judges that the reply achieved the outcome you describe. | | Answer contains | **Answer must contain** | The reply includes this text. | | Answer excludes | **Answer must not contain** | The reply does not include this text. | | Sub-agent | **Expected sub-agent**, **Must not call sub-agent** | The agent did, or did not, call that sub-agent. | | Routing | **Routed by** | For fast routing agents: the route was chosen by fast routing, by the model, or either. | | Tool | **Expected tool** | The agent called this tool. | A case passes only when every check it sets passes. Good cases target the mistakes that would hurt most: a wrong answer, a missing tool call, a promise the agent must not make, or the wrong specialist. ## How a run works [Section titled “How a run works”](#how-a-run-works) Each case runs as a single turn in an isolated test conversation, against the exact candidate being published. Read tools run for real and their results count as ground truth. Write actions never run in an eval; an agent that correctly proposes a write is treated as having taken that action. The **grader** is a model that reads the case’s desired outcome and a record of the turn: the customer’s message, every tool call with its arguments and result, any proposed actions, and the final reply. It judges only against the desired outcome, every requirement in it must hold, and a reply that states facts no tool returned fails. If the grader cannot reach a verdict, the case does not pass. A failed case shows the grader’s short rationale. You can run the suite while you work: in the console’s publish dialog with **Run tests**, or with `caplo eval` (which pushes the project first) or `caplo agent eval run` for one agent’s saved draft. ## The publication gate [Section titled “The publication gate”](#the-publication-gate) Publishing runs the suite and records the result in the release. The workspace’s **Agent publication requirements**, set by an Owner or Admin under **Settings** > **General**, decide what the result means: | Setting | Options | | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Eval requirement** | **Required**: every case must pass, and an agent with no cases cannot publish. This is the default. **Disabled**: results are shown but do not block publishing. | | **Passing routing cases per sub-agent** | For agents that use fast routing: how many passing cases with a **Routed by** check each sub-agent needs, from 1 to 10. The default is 2. | The requirements apply to every agent in the workspace; there are no per-agent exceptions. Disabling evals does not disable Caplo’s own checks: configuration, tool and knowledge eligibility, and Fast decisions for fast routing still apply. A release keeps the policy it was published under. Eval cases are not pinned in a release, so you can add or change cases at any time. The next publish runs the current suite. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Write evals](/agents/evals/) * [Test an agent](/agents/testing/) * [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/) * [Multi-agent](/concepts/multi-agent/): fast routing and its eval coverage.

# Knowledge

> How knowledge bases, documents, review, versions and audiences work, and how agents use them to answer.

A **knowledge base** is a set of documents your agents can search to answer questions, such as product guides, policies or FAQs. An agent with knowledge looks up relevant passages while it answers, instead of relying on what its model already knows. Knowledge bases belong to the workspace, not to one agent. You manage them under **Knowledge** in the console, and any number of agents can attach the same base. ## Documents and review [Section titled “Documents and review”](#documents-and-review) You add documents to a knowledge base as files: PDF, DOCX, HTML, Markdown, plain text, and PNG, JPG or WebP images, up to 25 MB each. Each file goes through the same steps before agents can use it: 1. **Extract.** Caplo extracts the text, including text in images and the page layout. 2. **Review.** You check the extracted text and the passages (chunks) agents will retrieve. Look for missing pages, broken tables, unreadable images and anything sensitive that should not be there. 3. **Approve.** An Owner or Admin approves the document. Builders can create knowledge bases and upload files, but only Owners and Admins approve documents and publish versions. This keeps a person accountable for what agents tell customers. ## Versions [Section titled “Versions”](#versions) Approved documents become available to agents only when an Owner or Admin publishes a **version** of the knowledge base. Caplo indexes the version, and once it is ready agents can search it. Versions are immutable: files you add or change later stay in the draft until you review them and publish another version. When you publish an agent, its release pins the knowledge version that was current at that moment. A new knowledge version does not change live answers until you publish the agent again. This means you can review exactly what a release knows, and an upload can never silently change what customers are told. See [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/). ## Audience [Section titled “Audience”](#audience) Each knowledge base has an **Audience**: | Audience | Who it serves | | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Public** | Anyone talking to the agent. | | **Verified customers only** | Only customers who have signed in during the conversation, on a channel that allows customer sign-in. Use it for content that should not reach anonymous visitors, such as account-specific procedures. | A change of audience applies from the next published version. ## Keeping knowledge in sync [Section titled “Keeping knowledge in sync”](#keeping-knowledge-in-sync) To keep a knowledge base in step with a folder of files in your repository, use `caplo knowledge sync`. It uploads new and changed files, removes ones that are gone, and with `--approve` and `--publish` approves them and publishes a version in one step. Approving this way skips reviewing the extracted text, so use it for content you already trust. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Give an agent knowledge](/agents/knowledge/) * [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/): add eval cases that check answers drawn from your documents. * [`caplo knowledge sync`](/reference/cli/knowledge/#caplo-knowledge-sync)

# Multi-agent

> How one agent delegates to sub-agents, the two ways a sub-agent can work, how child releases are pinned, and how routing chooses a sub-agent.

An agent can link other agents in the workspace as **sub-agents**. The agent a channel serves is the **entry agent**; it decides when to involve a sub-agent and which one. Splitting work this way keeps each agent’s instructions, tools and knowledge small and focused, for example a concierge that routes to separate orders, product and returns specialists. Each link has a **Use when** description, which tells the parent when this sub-agent is the right choice, and an optional **Do not use when** description, which rules it out for requests that belong elsewhere. Write both as you would brief a colleague. ## Two ways a sub-agent can work [Section titled “Two ways a sub-agent can work”](#two-ways-a-sub-agent-can-work) | In the console | In agent YAML | What happens | | ------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | **Answers and returns to this agent** | `mode: task_tool` | The parent asks the sub-agent in plain words, the sub-agent works with its own tools and knowledge, and returns an answer. The parent then replies to the customer. The sub-agent remembers its earlier requests in the same conversation. | | **Takes over the conversation** | `mode: active_conversation` | The entry agent hands the conversation to the sub-agent, which replies to the customer directly from the next message on until it hands the conversation back. | Use **Answers and returns** when the entry agent should stay in charge and combine answers, and **Takes over** when a specialist should run a longer exchange itself. Only the entry agent can hand the conversation over. Whichever mode you choose, a sub-agent’s write actions are shown to the customer for confirmation in the same way as the entry agent’s. See [Tools and actions](/concepts/tools-and-actions/). ## Child releases are pinned [Section titled “Child releases are pinned”](#child-releases-are-pinned) A link points at a specific published release of the child agent, and the child runs with the tools and knowledge pinned in that release, not its own current draft. A child must have a release before the parent can publish. Publishing a new child release does not change a published parent. To use it, update the link in the parent’s draft and publish the parent again. In a CLI project, `release: latest` on a link resolves to the child’s newest release whenever you push or publish, and `caplo publish` republishes the parents of a child that changed, children first. Agents can delegate further, up to a depth limit: by default at most three levels of sub-agents below an agent. ## Routing: the model or fast routing [Section titled “Routing: the model or fast routing”](#routing-the-model-or-fast-routing) The parent chooses a sub-agent in one of two ways: * **The model decides.** The parent’s own model reads the conversation and either answers or calls a sub-agent. This is the default and needs no setup. * **Fast routing.** A quick decision call picks the sub-agent before the parent’s model runs, in about 0.3 seconds instead of about 2 seconds. When the decision is below the agent’s minimum confidence, the model decides instead. Fast routing has extra requirements, because a wrong route is costly: * **Fast decisions** must be enabled for the workspace by an Owner or Admin. It sends the visitor’s recent messages and your sub-agent descriptions to OpenAI to choose a route. See [Fast decisions](/providers/fast-decisions/). * Every link needs a **Do not use when** description. * While evals are required, publishing needs passing eval cases that check the route to each sub-agent: two per sub-agent by default. See [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Sub-agents and routing](/agents/sub-agents-and-routing/): how to link agents and set up routing. * [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/) * [Build your first agent with the CLI](/get-started/quickstart-cli/): a working multi-agent example.

# Tools and actions

> How agents use tool packages and remote MCP servers, the difference between reads and writes, how customers confirm writes, and what each outcome means.

Tools let an agent look things up and take actions in your systems: check an order, find a free appointment slot, create a return. Caplo gets tools from two sources. | Source | What it is | | --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Tool package** | Code you write in TypeScript with the `@caplo/tools` SDK, either as a single function or as an MCP server with several tools. Caplo builds it, tests it, and runs it in an isolated sandbox that can reach only the hosts the package declares. Secrets are bound from workspace secrets, never stored in code. | | **Remote MCP server** | An MCP server you already run, connected by URL with its authorization. You review its tools, classify each as a read or a write, and publish the reviewed set. | Both become **immutable versions**. An agent release pins the exact version, so changing a tool’s code has no effect on customers until you publish a new tool version and then a new agent release. See [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/). ## Reads and writes [Section titled “Reads and writes”](#reads-and-writes) Every tool declares its **effect**. * A **read** only looks something up. The agent calls it whenever it needs the information and uses the result straight away. * A **write** changes something: it books, cancels, refunds or submits. By default (`confirmation: required`) a write never runs on the model’s say-so. The agent proposes the exact call, Caplo shows the customer what will happen, and the call runs only after the customer confirms. If the customer declines, nothing runs. A write can set `confirmation: none` to run without asking, but use that only for low-risk changes the customer would always expect. Model output, documents and message text never grant permission to run a write. The effect and confirmation come from your package’s `caplo.yaml`, or from your review of a remote MCP server’s tools, not from hints in the tool’s own code. ## Write outcomes [Section titled “Write outcomes”](#write-outcomes) Once a confirmed write runs, the tool reports one of four outcomes: | Outcome | Meaning | What the customer sees | | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Applied | The change happened. | Confirmation that the request succeeded. | | Rejected | Your system definitely did not make the change, for example because the slot was taken. | That the request did not go through. | | Pending | Your system accepted the request but has not finished it. | That the outcome is not confirmed yet, and they should not send it again. | | Unknown | Caplo cannot tell whether the change happened, for example after a timeout or an error. | The same as pending. | Caplo never retries a write blindly, because a retry could book or charge twice. For pending and unknown outcomes, a tool can provide a status check: Caplo asks it later about the original request and records the real outcome when the tool answers. If the outcome stays unknown, someone on your team should check your own system and follow up with the customer. While a write is unresolved, the agent cannot propose another write in the same conversation. See [Write tools](/tools/write-tools/) for how to return these outcomes and add a status check. ## Human follow-up [Section titled “Human follow-up”](#human-follow-up) Some requests should go to a person rather than a tool. An agent can hand the conversation to your team when its channel has a handoff destination, and your team can take over any text conversation from the console. See [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Tools overview](/tools/) and [Write your first tool](/tools/first-tool/) * [Connect a remote MCP server](/tools/remote-mcp/) * [Give an agent tools](/agents/tools/) * [Secrets and egress](/tools/secrets-and-egress/)

# Workspaces and roles

> How workspaces separate your work, what each of the eight member roles can do, and how to switch between workspaces.

A workspace is the boundary for everything in Caplo. Agents, tool packages, knowledge bases, channels, conversations, secrets, provider keys, usage and billing all belong to one workspace, and nothing is shared between workspaces. Many teams use one workspace per environment, for example one for staging and one for production, and publish to each with the CLI’s [environments](/cli/environments/). The first time you sign in to the console, Caplo creates a workspace for you and makes you its Owner. You can create more from the workspace menu, or with `caplo workspace create`. ## Members and roles [Section titled “Members and roles”](#members-and-roles) Each member has exactly one role in a workspace. Owners and Admins invite people from **Settings** > **Members** with **Invite member**: enter the email addresses, choose a **Role**, and select **Send invitations**. They can also limit invitations to **Allowed email domains** and change or remove members later. A workspace has exactly one Owner, and only the Owner can transfer ownership. | What you can do | Owner | Admin | Builder | Developer | Supervisor | Auditor | Viewer | Billing | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----- | ----- | ------- | --------- | ---------- | ------- | ------ | ------- | | Create, edit and test agents; upload knowledge | Yes | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | Publish agents; approve knowledge and publish its versions | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | | Build and publish tool packages | Yes | Yes | | Yes | | | | | | Manage workspace secrets and bind them to tools | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | | Configure channels and widget designs | Yes | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | Connect integrations, such as messaging accounts, phone providers and handoff destinations | Yes | Yes | | Yes | | | | | | Manage AI provider keys | Yes | Yes | | Yes | | | | | | Read conversations | Yes | Yes | Yes | | Yes | Yes | Yes | | | Take over conversations, add notes, reveal participants | Yes | Yes | | | Yes | | | | | View usage | Yes | Yes | | | | Yes | | Yes | | Manage billing and spend budgets | Yes | | | | | | | Yes | | Invite and manage members | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | | Workspace settings, SSO, customer identity, data retention | Yes | Yes | | | | | | | | Create API clients for CI and scripts | Yes | Yes | | Yes | | | | | | Read the audit log | Yes | Yes | | | | Yes | | | | Transfer ownership | Yes | | | | | | | | Every role except Auditor can also read agents, tools, channels and integration status. Auditor reads agents, channels, conversations, usage and the audit log, but not tools or integrations. A few combinations are worth knowing: * **Builders cannot publish.** A Builder prepares and tests an agent; an Owner or Admin reviews and publishes it. * **Developers cannot edit agents.** A Developer builds tool packages and integrations that Builders then attach to agents. * **A CLI project that contains both agents and tool packages** needs an Owner or Admin to push and publish it. Caplo checks permissions on every request, so the CLI, Tool Studio and the API enforce the same rules as the console. An [API client](/cli/ci-cd/) can be granted only actions that the member creating it holds. ## Switching workspaces [Section titled “Switching workspaces”](#switching-workspaces) In the console, the workspace menu in the top bar shows the current workspace’s name. Open it to switch to another workspace you belong to, or select **Create workspace**. If a workspace requires company single sign-on, you may be asked to sign in through your identity provider when you enter it. The CLI acts on one workspace at a time: * `caplo workspace use "<NAME>"` selects the workspace for later commands. * `--workspace` (or `-w`) on any command, or the `CAPLO_WORKSPACE` environment variable, overrides it for that command. * `caplo workspace list` shows the workspaces you belong to, and `caplo auth status` shows the selected one. See [Workspaces in the CLI](/cli/workspaces/) for details. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/): what Builders and Admins create and publish. * [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/): what Supervisors do in the inbox. * [Spend budgets](/usage/spend-budgets/): what the Billing role manages.

# Manage API keys

> Create API clients and keys in the console, choose scopes, rotate and revoke keys, and use a key with the caplo CLI.

An **API client** is a workspace identity for CI pipelines and scripts. It is not a member: it has no role and cannot sign in to the console. It can do only what its **scopes** allow, in its own workspace. Each client has one or more **keys**, secret strings that start with `caplo_sk_`; several active keys let you rotate without downtime. Owners, Admins and Developers manage API clients in **Settings** > **API access** (under **Developer**). For using keys from the CLI side, see [Log in and use API keys](/cli/authentication/#api-keys-for-automation). ## Create a client [Section titled “Create a client”](#create-a-client) * Console 1. Open **Settings** > **API access** and select **Create client**. 2. Enter a **Client name** (up to 80 characters) and turn on the **Permissions** it needs. Grant only what the job uses. 3. Select **Create key** and copy the key from **Copy your API key**. * CLI 
  ```sh
  caplo workspace api-client create github-deploy \
    --scope agents:write,agents:publish,tools:write,tools:publish
  ```
 The key is printed once on standard output. The key is shown only once. Caplo keeps only a hash of it and cannot show it again, so store it in your CI’s secret store straight away. A client’s name and scopes cannot be changed, and a client cannot be deleted. To change scopes, create a new client and revoke the old client’s keys. ## Scopes [Section titled “Scopes”](#scopes) A write or publish scope includes the read scope of the same resource: `agents:publish` can also read agents. | Scope | Console label | Allows | | -------------------- | -------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `agents:read` | **Read agents** | Read agents, drafts, releases and the workspace’s publication requirements; knowledge bases and their documents; eval suites and runs. | | `agents:write` | **Edit agents and knowledge** | Create agents and save drafts; create and change knowledge bases, upload and delete documents; create and save eval suites. | | `agents:publish` | **Publish agents and knowledge** | Run evals; publish and revoke releases and pin their tool versions; archive and delete agents; approve documents and publish knowledge versions; change the workspace’s [agent publication requirements](/admin/workspace-settings/#agent-publication-requirements). | | `tools:read` | **Read tools** | Read tool packages, their files, builds and build logs, versions, test runs and invocation logs; remote MCP servers; the names of package and workspace secrets and where they are bound. | | `tools:write` | **Edit tools** | Create, change and delete packages and their files; install dependencies; start and cancel builds; run remote fixture tests; review the tools of remote MCP servers. | | `tools:publish` | **Publish tools** | Publish and revoke package versions; publish remote MCP server tools; set and remove a package’s own secret values. | | `channels:read` | **Read channels** | Read channels, the release each serves, and widget designs. | | `channels:write` | **Deploy to channels** | Create, change, pause, resume and delete channels; deploy and roll back releases; create, edit and publish widget designs and upload their assets. | | `conversations:read` | **Read conversations** | Accepted, but no Caplo API accepts API keys for conversations yet. | | `usage:read` | **Read usage** | Accepted, but no Caplo API accepts API keys for usage yet. | | `endpoints:read` | **Read endpoints** | Accepted, but no Caplo API uses it. | | `endpoints:write` | **Manage endpoints** | Accepted, but no Caplo API uses it. | agents:publish is broad `agents:publish` lets a key publish agents, and it can also delete agents and turn off the workspace’s eval requirement. Give it only to pipelines that publish, and keep that key in a protected CI environment. No scope covers members, roles, SSO, billing, workspace secrets, integrations or other API clients. Those always need a person. See [What works with an API key](/cli/authentication/#what-works-with-an-api-key) for the CLI commands each scope enables. ### What you can grant [Section titled “What you can grant”](#what-you-can-grant) You can create a client, or add a key to one, only if your role holds every action its scopes grant. For example: * A **Developer** can grant `agents:read`, the tool scopes and `channels:read`, but not `agents:write`, `agents:publish`, `channels:write`, `conversations:read` or `usage:read`. * An **Admin** or **Owner** can grant any scope. This also applies to rotation: a Developer cannot add a key to a client that an Admin created with `agents:publish`. ## Rotate a key [Section titled “Rotate a key”](#rotate-a-key) 1. Add a new key: **Add rotation key** on the client in the console, or `caplo workspace api-client rotate github-deploy`. 2. Replace the old key in your CI’s secret store and check that jobs pass. 3. Revoke the old key. The client’s other keys keep working until you revoke them, so nothing breaks during the switch. ## Revoke keys [Section titled “Revoke keys”](#revoke-keys) In the console, select **Revoke** next to a key. With the CLI: 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client revoke github-deploy --key <KEY_ID_OR_PREFIX>
caplo workspace api-client revoke github-deploy --except <NEW_KEY_ID_OR_PREFIX>
caplo workspace api-client revoke github-deploy
```
 The first revokes one key, the second every key but the new one after a rotation, and the third every active key. Anything using a revoked key stops working at once, and a revoked key cannot be restored. Each key in the console shows its prefix, when it was created and when it was last used, so you can find keys nobody uses any more. `caplo workspace api-client list` shows each client’s scopes and active key prefixes. ## Use a key [Section titled “Use a key”](#use-a-key) Set the key and the workspace ID in the environment, and `caplo` commands run as the client: 
```sh
export CAPLO_API_KEY=<API_KEY>
export CAPLO_WORKSPACE=<WORKSPACE_ID>
caplo auth status
```
 Name the workspace by its ID, shown in [General settings](/admin/workspace-settings/#general), because a key cannot list workspaces. For pipelines, see [CI/CD](/cli/ci-cd/). Every key creation and revocation is recorded in the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Log in and use API keys](/cli/authentication/) * [CI/CD](/cli/ci-cd/) * [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/)

# Manage members and roles

> Invite people to a workspace, choose and change their roles, restrict invitations to your domains, transfer ownership, and remove members.

Open **Settings**, then **Members**. Every member can see the list; Owners and Admins invite, change roles and remove people. Each member has one role, and what each role can do is in the [roles table](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/#members-and-roles). **Role permissions** on the same page shows the full permission matrix. A workspace has exactly one Owner. The Owner role cannot be given by invitation or role change, only by [transferring ownership](#transfer-ownership). ## Invite members [Section titled “Invite members”](#invite-members) 1. Select **Invite member**. 2. Under **Email addresses**, paste up to 50 addresses, separated by commas or one per line, or select **Upload CSV** to read them from the first column of a CSV file. 3. Choose a **Role**: Admin, Builder, Developer, Supervisor, Auditor, Viewer or Billing. 4. Select **Send invitations**. Each person gets an email with a link to sign in and join. An invitation: * Expires after 7 days. **Resend** sends a new link and restarts the 7 days; **Revoke** cancels it. * Must be accepted by someone signed in with that exact email address, verified by their sign-in provider. * Is refused for someone who is already a member or already has a pending invitation. If the workspace [requires SSO](/admin/sso-and-scim/#require-sso), the invitee must sign in through your identity provider to join. With SSO onboarding or SCIM provisioning, people can also join without an invitation; see [SSO and SCIM](/admin/sso-and-scim/). ### Limit invitations to your domains [Section titled “Limit invitations to your domains”](#limit-invitations-to-your-domains) Under **Invitation policy**, enter up to 20 **Allowed email domains**, such as `company.com, company.co.uk`, and save. Invitations to any other domain are then refused. Leave it empty to allow any domain. The policy applies to new invitations, not to members who have already joined. ## Change a member’s role [Section titled “Change a member’s role”](#change-a-members-role) Choose a new role in the member’s **Role** menu and confirm **Change role**. It takes effect on their next action; if you change your own role, your access changes at once. The Owner’s role cannot be changed here. ## Transfer ownership [Section titled “Transfer ownership”](#transfer-ownership) Only the Owner can transfer ownership, and only to an active member. 1. On your own row, select **Transfer ownership**. 2. Choose the **New Owner**. 3. Select **Transfer ownership**. The new Owner gets the Owner role and you become an Admin immediately. The transfer is recorded in the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/). Only the new Owner can transfer it back. ## Remove a member [Section titled “Remove a member”](#remove-a-member) Select **Remove** on the member’s row and confirm **Remove member**. They lose access to this workspace; their other workspace memberships are not affected. To remove the Owner, transfer ownership first. Removing a member does not revoke their API keys API clients belong to the workspace, not to the member who created them. Keys a removed member created keep working until you revoke them in **Settings** > **API access**. See [API keys](/admin/api-keys/#revoke-keys). Members provisioned by SCIM are suspended rather than removed when your identity provider deactivates them. See [SCIM provisioning](/admin/sso-and-scim/#scim-provisioning). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/) * [SSO and SCIM](/admin/sso-and-scim/) * [Audit log](/operate/audit-log/)

# Manage workspace secrets

> Create, rotate and delete the workspace secrets that tool packages use, see where each is bound, and know who can manage them.

A **workspace secret** is a credential, such as an API key for your store, that you save once and bind to every tool package that needs it. Rotating it updates every bound package. This page covers managing the secrets themselves; to declare secrets in a package and bind them, see [Secrets and network access](/tools/secrets-and-egress/). Values are write-only. Once saved, neither the console nor the CLI shows a value again, and the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/) records changes without values. ## Who can do what [Section titled “Who can do what”](#who-can-do-what) | Action | Roles | | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | | See the list of workspace secrets and where they are bound | Owner, Admin, Builder, Developer, Supervisor, Viewer, Billing | | Create, rotate and delete workspace secrets; bind and unbind them to packages | Owner, Admin | API keys can list secrets with `tools:read` but cannot create, rotate, delete or bind them. ## Create a secret [Section titled “Create a secret”](#create-a-secret) * Console 1. Open **Settings** > **Secrets** (under **Developer**) and select **Create secret**. 2. Enter a **Name**: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter, 2 to 64 characters, such as `shop-prod`. 3. Optionally add a **Description** (up to 500 characters) of what it is for. 4. Enter the **Value** (up to 8,192 bytes) and select **Create secret**. * CLI The value is read from a hidden prompt, or from standard input when it is not a terminal, so it never appears in your shell history: 
  ```sh
  caplo secret set shop-prod --description "Shop API production key"
  ```
 In a script, pipe it from your secret store: `printf '%s' "$SHOP_KEY" | caplo secret set shop-prod`. A new secret is **Not bound** until you bind it to a package from **Tools**, in the package’s **Secrets** dialog, or with `caplo tool secret bind`. See [Provide the values](/tools/secrets-and-egress/#provide-the-values). ## Rotate a secret [Section titled “Rotate a secret”](#rotate-a-secret) * Console Select **Rotate** next to the secret, enter the **New value**, and select **Rotate value**. * CLI Run `caplo secret set` with the existing name. Without `--description`, it keeps the current description. 
  ```sh
  caplo secret set shop-prod
  ```
 Every bound package uses the new value in live calls within a minute. There is no overlap period, so if your provider supports two active credentials, create the new one there, rotate the secret in Caplo, wait a minute, then revoke the old credential at the provider. ## See where a secret is used [Section titled “See where a secret is used”](#see-where-a-secret-is-used) The list shows each secret with **Bound** and the number of package secrets that use it, or **Not bound**, and when it was last updated. For the packages and names behind the count: 
```sh
caplo secret list
caplo secret usage shop-prod
```
 ## Delete a secret [Section titled “Delete a secret”](#delete-a-secret) You can delete a secret only when no package secret is bound to it. In the console, select **Delete**: if it is in use, Caplo lists the package secrets to unbind first; otherwise confirm **Delete secret**. With the CLI, `caplo secret delete shop-prod` refuses a bound secret and lists its bindings. Deleting cannot be undone The value is removed for good. Unbinding a package secret first means live calls of tools that need it fail until you set or bind a value again. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Secrets and network access](/tools/secrets-and-egress/) * [`caplo secret` reference](/reference/cli/secret/) * [Manage API keys](/admin/api-keys/)

# Set up SSO and SCIM

> Let your team sign in through your identity provider with OIDC or SAML, verify your domains, require SSO, and provision members with SCIM 2.0.

Workspace SSO lets members sign in to a workspace through your company identity provider instead of Google or an email and password. Everything here is under **Settings** > **Identity & SSO**, and only Owners and Admins can see or change it. SSO settings belong to one workspace; other workspaces your members belong to keep their own. The usual order is: verify a domain, add and test a connection, activate it, then choose a sign-in policy. ## Verify a domain [Section titled “Verify a domain”](#verify-a-domain) Caplo uses verified domains to route sign-ins and to admit new members through SSO. 1. Under **Organization domains**, enter the exact domain, such as `company.com`, and select **Add domain**. Wildcards are not accepted. 2. Caplo shows a **DNS TXT verification record**. In your DNS provider, add a TXT record at the **Host** shown, `_caplo-verify.company.com`, with the **Value** shown, which starts with `caplo-verification=`. 3. Select **Check verification**. If the record is not visible yet, wait for DNS to update and check again. A domain can belong to only one workspace. Verifying it never grants anyone Admin access. To remove a domain, first take it out of any connection’s **Routing domain**; the last verified domain cannot be removed while SSO is required or SSO onboarding is on. ## Add an identity connection [Section titled “Add an identity connection”](#add-an-identity-connection) A connection is saved as a draft, tested with a real sign-in, then activated. 1. Under **Identity connections**, select **Add connection**, enter a **Connection name** and choose the **Protocol**, **OIDC** or **SAML**. 2. Enter your provider’s details: * **OIDC**: the **Issuer URL** (HTTPS; Caplo reads the provider’s discovery document), the **Client ID** and the **Client secret**. * **SAML**: choose how to give the provider’s metadata: a **Metadata URL**, an uploaded XML file, or **Enter manually** with the **IdP entity ID**, **IdP sign-in URL** and **IdP signing certificate**. 3. Check **Email attribute or claim** and **Display-name attribute or claim** (`email` and `name` by default) and select **Save draft**. 4. Select **Prepare Redirect URI** (OIDC) or **Prepare provider URLs** (SAML), and register what Caplo shows in your provider: the **Redirect URI** for OIDC; the **Assertion consumer service URL**, **Service provider entity ID** or **Service provider metadata URL** for SAML. 5. Select **Test login**, then **Continue to provider**, and sign in through your provider. A successful sign-in marks the connection **Test passed**. 6. Select **Activate**. Caplo stores the client secret encrypted and never shows it again. Your provider must confirm the user’s email address. Editing an active connection creates a replacement draft; the active configuration keeps working until you test and activate the draft, or **Discard draft**. The protocol cannot be changed; create a new connection instead. You can add several connections, for example one per subsidiary. ### Routing options [Section titled “Routing options”](#routing-options) | Option | What it does | | ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | **Routing domain** | **Any verified organization domain**, or one verified domain this connection serves. | | **Redirect when email domain matches** | Sends people whose email domain matches straight to this provider when they sign in to the workspace. It is discovery, not an access rule. | | **Excluded routing domains** | Domains to skip for redirection, such as `contractors.company.com` or `*.partners.company.com`. | | **Hide from the provider choice** | Members reach the provider only through redirection. | | **Show to members linked to another provider** | Whether members who already sign in through another connection see this one. | The **Workspace login link** in [General settings](/admin/workspace-settings/#general) opens this workspace’s sign-in page, with its SSO options, directly. ### Map groups to roles [Section titled “Map groups to roles”](#map-groups-to-roles) **Group-to-role mapping** sets the role of members who join through SSO onboarding from their groups in your provider. Enter pairs such as `support=Supervisor, builders=Builder`. The provider must send the groups in a `groups` claim (OIDC) or attribute (SAML). * Roles are Viewer, Builder, Developer, Supervisor, Auditor and Billing. Owner and Admin are always assigned by hand. * The role is checked again at every sign-in, so a group change in your provider changes the role. * When several groups match, the first role in this order wins: Supervisor, Developer, Builder, Auditor, Billing, Viewer. With no match, the member gets the **New member role**. * Members who joined by invitation or SCIM keep the role you set in **Members**. ## Choose the sign-in policy [Section titled “Choose the sign-in policy”](#choose-the-sign-in-policy) Under **Sign-in policy**, set **Workspace SSO policy**: | Policy | Effect | | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | **Disabled** | Members use Google or email and password. Connections cannot be used to enter this workspace. This is the default. | | **Optional** | Members can use company SSO or Google and email and password. | | **Required** | Members can enter the workspace only through an active connection. | **New member onboarding** decides how people join: * **Invitation only** (default): only people you [invite](/admin/members-and-roles/#invite-members) can join. * **Approved SSO onboarding on verified domains**: anyone who signs in through an active connection with a verified email on one of your verified domains joins with the **New member role** (Viewer, Builder or Supervisor) or the role their groups map to. It needs a verified domain, an active connection, and SSO set to **Optional** or **Required**. ### Require SSO [Section titled “Require SSO”](#require-sso) Requiring SSO needs a verified domain, an active, tested connection and **Recovery path confirmed** turned on, and you must check **I understand the sign-in and session impact**. When you save it: * Members signed in with Google or email and password lose access to this workspace at once, Owner included, until they sign in again through your provider. * Existing members keep their roles. * API keys keep working; they do not sign in. Plan for an identity provider outage If your provider is down or misconfigured while SSO is required, nobody can enter the workspace through it. The Owner can lift the requirement: open `https://app.caplo.si/login?workspace=<WORKSPACE_ID>&recovery=1` and sign in with the email and password of their Caplo account, not through SSO. Caplo sets the policy back to **Optional** with **Invitation only** onboarding and records an owner recovery in the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/). This works only while **Recovery path confirmed** is on. ## SCIM provisioning [Section titled “SCIM provisioning”](#scim-provisioning) SCIM lets your identity provider add, update and deactivate workspace members automatically. 1. Under **SCIM provisioning**, select **Create token** and copy the **Bearer token**. It is shown once. 2. Turn on **Enable SCIM**. 3. In your identity provider, set the SCIM base URL to the **SCIM 2.0 endpoint** shown, `https://api.caplo.si/v1/identity/workspaces/<WORKSPACE_ID>/scim/v2`, and authenticate with the token as a bearer token. **Rotate token** issues a new token and revokes the old one at once. Turning SCIM off makes the token stop working. ### What Caplo supports [Section titled “What Caplo supports”](#what-caplo-supports) * The **Users** resource, with `POST`, `GET`, `PUT`, `PATCH` (`add`, `replace`, `remove`) and `DELETE`. Groups, bulk operations, sorting and password changes are not supported. * Filters on `userName`, `emails.value` or `externalId` with `eq`, up to 100 results per page. | Attribute | Use | | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | | `userName` | Required. If `emails` is absent, it must be an email address. | | `emails` | The primary address, or the first one, is the member’s email. | | `name.givenName`, `name.familyName`, `name.formatted`, `displayName` | The member’s display name. | | `externalId` | Your provider’s ID for the user. | | `active` | `false` suspends the member; `true` restores them. | SCIM does not set roles. Provisioned members get the **New member role** when they join; change it later in [Members](/admin/members-and-roles/#change-a-members-role). ### How provisioned users join [Section titled “How provisioned users join”](#how-provisioned-users-join) * A provisioned user becomes a member the first time they sign in through an active connection with that email address. Provisioning alone sends no email. * Provisioned members can enter the workspace only through SSO, even when the policy is **Optional**. Keep the policy at **Optional** or **Required** and at least one connection active. * Deactivating or deleting a user in your provider suspends their membership. Changing their email suspends the old membership. * While SCIM is on, SSO onboarding admits only provisioned users. Invitations still work. ### Existing members [Section titled “Existing members”](#existing-members) When your provider provisions an email that already belongs to a member, that member becomes SCIM-managed: from then on your provider can suspend them.
