Owners and Admins cannot be SCIM-managed; Caplo refuses to provision their email with a conflict error, so leave them out of the SCIM assignment in your provider. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Manage members and roles](/admin/members-and-roles/) * [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/) * [Audit log](/operate/audit-log/)

# Workspace settings

> Rename a workspace and set its time zone, find its ID and login link, set agent publication requirements, and pause or resume the workspace.

Workspace-wide settings live under **Settings**. Every member can read **General**; Owners and Admins change it. **Workspace controls** is for the Owner only. ## General [Section titled “General”](#general) **Settings** > **General** has three panels. **Workspace details** | Field | Notes | | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Workspace name** | Up to 80 characters. Shown in the workspace menu and in invitation emails. | | **Workspace timezone** | One of UTC, America/New_York, America/Los_Angeles, Europe/London, Europe/Berlin, Asia/Karachi or Asia/Singapore. It sets the days of the **Overview** date range and the times and date filter of the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/). Billing periods and the Conversations inbox always use UTC. | **Workspace information** * **Workspace ID**: the identifier that `CAPLO_WORKSPACE`, API keys and the SCIM endpoint use. `caplo workspace get` shows it too. * **Workspace login link**: `https://app.caplo.si/login?workspace=<WORKSPACE_ID>`. Share it with your team to open this workspace’s sign-in page directly, with its [SSO](/admin/sso-and-scim/) options. **Open workspace sign-in** opens it. **Agent publication requirements** is described next. ## Agent publication requirements [Section titled “Agent publication requirements”](#agent-publication-requirements) These rules decide what eval results mean when anyone publishes an agent in the workspace. Owners and Admins can change them, and so can an API key with `agents:publish`. | Setting | Choices | Default | | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------- | | **Eval requirement** | **Required**: all evals must pass. **Disabled**: results do not block publication. | Required | | **Passing routing cases per sub-agent** | 1 to 10: how many passing routed eval cases each direct sub-agent of a fast-routing agent needs. | 2 | Select **Save publication requirements** to apply them. * They apply to every agent in the workspace. There are no per-agent overrides. * Releases already published keep the requirements they were published under. * Platform checks, such as no business action during evals, apply even when the eval requirement is off. For how the gate works, see [Publication requirements](/agents/evals/#publication-requirements) and [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/). Caution With **Eval requirement** set to **Disabled**, a release can reach customers without proof that it behaves. Prefer fixing or removing a failing case. ## Pause a workspace [Section titled “Pause a workspace”](#pause-a-workspace) **Settings** > **Workspace controls** (under **Advanced**) holds the emergency switch. Only the Owner can see it. 1. Select **Pause member and API access**. 2. Confirm **Pause workspace**. The badge changes from **Running** to **Paused**. While the workspace is paused: * Caplo refuses requests that need a workspace permission, from members and API keys alike: editing, publishing and deploying agents and tools, reading conversations, and the like. API keys stop authenticating. * Members cannot enter the workspace in the console, and invitations cannot be accepted. * Channels keep serving customers. Pausing the workspace does not stop conversations; to stop an agent answering, pause or turn off its channels. See [Channels](/channels/). To resume, select **Resume workspace** and confirm **Resume workspace**. Everything that was refused works again; nothing is deleted while paused. Pausing locks you out too Once you reload or leave the page, the console can no longer open a paused workspace, even for its Owner, so **Resume workspace** is out of reach. Resume from the same page, or contact [support](/resources/support/) to resume it for you. Pausing and resuming are recorded in the [audit log](/operate/audit-log/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Manage members and roles](/admin/members-and-roles/) * [Data retention and privacy](/operate/data-and-privacy/) * [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/)

# Define an agent in YAML

> Write an agent as a YAML file, edit it in the console's YAML editor or with caplo agent, and move a console agent into a CLI project.

Everything on an agent’s **Setup**, **Tools & actions**, **Knowledge** and **Sub-agents** tabs can be written as one YAML file. The console’s YAML editor and the `caplo` CLI read the same format, so you can keep agents in a repository, review changes in pull requests, and publish from CI. Every field is listed in the [Agent YAML reference](/reference/agent-yaml/). ## The agent file [Section titled “The agent file”](#the-agent-file) An agent file is one YAML document with `apiVersion: "v2.0.0"` (`v2` and `v2.0` also work; `v1` files are refused), `kind: Agent`, and the agent’s fields. This file defines a support agent with two tools, a knowledge base, a returns sub-agent and human handoff: 
```yaml
apiVersion: "v2.0.0"
kind: Agent
name: Support Desk
description: Answers order and returns questions for Northwind customers.
instructions: |-
  You are the Northwind support agent.
  Answer questions about orders, delivery and returns.
  Look orders up with the order tools before you describe their status.
  If you cannot find an answer, say so and offer to connect the customer with a person.
greeting: Hi, I'm the Northwind support agent. How can I help with your order today?
model:
  provider: Anthropic
  name: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001
  temperature: 0.3
  responseLimit: 800
tools:
  - order-desk/lookup_order
  - order-desk/track_shipment
knowledgeBases:
  - Returns policy
actions:
  - transfer
subAgents:
  - agentId: 6f0c2a8e-4b1d-4e7a-9c3f-2d5b8a1e7f40
    release: latest
    useWhen: The customer asks how returns, exchanges or refunds work in general.
    doNotUseWhen: Questions about a specific order.
    mode: task_tool
policy:
  maxModelCalls: 12
  knowledgePassages: 4
  reasoning:
    thinkingBudgetTokens: 2048
suggestedReplies:
  maxCount: 3
visitorFeedback: true
```
 | Field | Guide | | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `name`, `description`, `instructions`, `greeting`, `model` | [Set instructions and model](/agents/instructions-and-model/) | | `tools` | [Give an agent tools](/agents/tools/) | | `knowledgeBases` | [Add knowledge](/agents/knowledge/) | | `subAgents`, `routing` | [Add sub-agents and routing](/agents/sub-agents-and-routing/) | | `actions` | [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/) | | `policy` | [Tune the agent loop](/agents/policy-and-limits/) | | `suggestedReplies`, `visitorFeedback` | [Offer suggested replies and collect ratings](/agents/replies-and-feedback/) | | `evals` | [Write and run evals](/agents/evals/) | Voice settings are set in the console and are not part of the file; applying or pushing a file keeps the agent’s voice settings as they are. For validation and completion in your editor, add this first line to the file: 
```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.caplo.si/schemas/agent-2.0.0.json
```
 ## Edit an agent as YAML in the console [Section titled “Edit an agent as YAML in the console”](#edit-an-agent-as-yaml-in-the-console) 1. Open the agent and, next to **Build with**, select **YAML editor**. The editor shows the current draft, with schema suggestions (Ctrl+Space). 2. Edit the YAML, or paste a file over it. The side panel lists **Existing agent IDs** to use in `subAgents`, an **Agent map** of the links, and **Validation** problems. 3. Select **Validate**, then **Review changes**. The review lists the fields that will change. 4. Select **Apply to draft**, then **Save draft**. Save and publish remain separate steps. **Export YAML** downloads the draft as `<agent-id>.yaml`. While YAML edits are not applied, the agent cannot be saved or published; apply or discard them first. The console’s YAML includes the agent’s eval cases as `evals: {cases: [...]}`. Applying YAML saves the cases at once, outside the draft: an empty `cases` list deletes every case, and a file without `evals` leaves them unchanged. ## Work with agent files in the CLI [Section titled “Work with agent files in the CLI”](#work-with-agent-files-in-the-cli) 
```sh
caplo agent init -f support.yaml --name "Support Desk"
caplo agent validate -f support.yaml
caplo agent push -f support.yaml --dry-run
caplo agent push -f support.yaml
caplo agent pull "Support Desk" -f support.yaml
```
 | Command | What it does | | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `caplo agent init` | Writes a starter file with one eval case. | | `caplo agent validate` | Checks the file offline against the schema, its references and the link rules. Exits with status 3 on problems. | | `caplo agent push` | Saves the file as the agent’s draft, creating the agent if no agent has that name, and replaces its eval cases when the file has `evals`. `--dry-run` prints the diff only. | | `caplo agent pull` | Writes the draft exactly as the console’s **Export YAML** does, without eval cases. | To create a new agent from a file, push it with the CLI. In a project, `caplo validate`, `caplo diff`, `caplo push` and `caplo publish` work on all agent files at once; see [Projects](/cli/projects/). ## Console and CLI formats [Section titled “Console and CLI formats”](#console-and-cli-formats) The two surfaces write some fields differently. A project file is portable across workspaces; a console export names workspace IDs. | Field | Console export and `caplo agent pull` | Project agent file | | ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `tools` | Package IDs: `<package ID>/<tool>` or `<package ID>` | Names: `<package>/<tool>`, `<package>`, or `<server>/<tool>` for a remote MCP server | | `subAgents` | `agentId` | `local: <file key>` for project agents, `agentId` for others | | `knowledgeBases` | IDs | Names or IDs | | `evals` | `evals: {cases: [...]}`, each case with an `id` | A list of cases matched by `name` | The CLI accepts both forms. A console export keeps its case IDs when you push it with `caplo agent push`; in a project, the list form matched by name is the convention, and `caplo pull` writes it. ## Move a console agent into a CLI project [Section titled “Move a console agent into a CLI project”](#move-a-console-agent-into-a-cli-project) `caplo pull` writes a workspace agent, every sub-agent it links, their eval cases, and the tool packages they use into a project: 
```sh
caplo pull "agent/Support Desk" ./support
cd support
caplo validate
caplo diff
```
 * With a directory that has no `caplo.yaml`, it creates a new project there. Without a directory, it uses the project above the current directory, or creates one in it. * Each agent goes to its own file in the directory the project’s `agents` glob names; an agent the project already defines is written to its existing file. * Tool packages the project lacks are pulled from their workspace drafts into the directory the `tools` glob names. Remote MCP server tools stay `<server>/<tool>`. * Sub-agent links name the release the draft pins, since a draft does not record `release: latest`. Change them to `latest` if you want children to follow their newest release. `caplo diff` should then show no changes. From here, edit the files and use `caplo push` and `caplo publish`; see [The CLI workflow](/cli/workflow/). AI prompt Move a console agent into this repository Pulls an agent built in the Caplo console, with its sub-agents, eval cases and tool packages, into a caplo project and checks it matches the workspace. # Write and run evals

> Define eval cases for an agent, run them from the console or the CLI, and meet the publication gate and workspace publication requirements.

An eval case is a customer message and what should happen when the agent answers it. Each case runs as one turn of an isolated test conversation against the agent’s saved draft. Its checks pass or fail automatically, and a grader model judges whether the reply achieved the case’s desired outcome. By default a workspace publishes an agent only when every case passes. For the ideas behind this, see [Evaluations](/concepts/evaluations/). ## Case fields [Section titled “Case fields”](#case-fields) | YAML field | Console label | Required | Description | | ------------------ | ------------------------------ | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `name` | **Case name** | Yes | Up to 80 characters, unique within the agent. The CLI matches cases by name. | | `input` | **Customer message** | Yes | The one message the customer sends. Up to 8,000 characters. | | `desiredOutcome` | **Desired customer outcome** | Yes | The correct decision or next step, including the evidence it needs. A grader model judges every run against it. Up to 4,000 characters. | | `answerContains` | **Answer must contain** | No | A phrase the reply must include. | | `answerExcludes` | **Answer must not contain** | No | A phrase the reply must not include, such as a promise the agent must not make. | | `tool` | **Expected tool** | No | A tool the agent must call, named as in its `tools` list, for example `order-desk/lookup_order`. | | `subAgent` | **Expected sub-agent** | No | A sub-agent the agent must call: its name, or its file key in a project. | | `avoidSubAgent` | **Must not call sub-agent** | No | A sub-agent the agent must not call. Must differ from `subAgent`. | | `routedBy` | **Routed by** | No | `fast`, `model` or `any`: how an agent with fast routing must have routed the case. See [Routing cases](#routing-cases). | | `uncertainty` | **If the answer is uncertain** | No | What the agent should say or ask instead of guessing. A note for reviewers; runs do not check it. | | `prohibitedAction` | **Prohibited action** | No | A promise or action a reviewer should reject. A note for reviewers; runs do not check it. | Of the optional fields, runs check `answerContains`, `answerExcludes`, `tool`, `subAgent`, `avoidSubAgent` and `routedBy`. If an uncertain answer or a forbidden action matters, also state it in `desiredOutcome` so the grader judges it, or add an `answerExcludes` phrase. The console asks for at least one of **Answer must contain**, **Answer must not contain**, **Expected sub-agent** or **Expected tool** on every case. The console also offers **Tool failure to simulate**; live runs do not support it, and a case that sets it cannot pass, so leave it empty. An agent can have up to 100 cases. ## What every run checks [Section titled “What every run checks”](#what-every-run-checks) Besides the case’s own fields, every case is checked for these: | Check | Passes when | | ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | | Turn completed | The turn ended with a reply. | | Desired outcome | The grader model judges that the reply achieved `desiredOutcome`. | | No business action | The test did not carry out a write in your systems. | | Within limits | The turn stayed within its delegation and tool limits. | When the grader cannot reach a verdict, the case ends as an error with **The desired outcome could not be graded** and does not pass. Run it again. ## Add cases [Section titled “Add cases”](#add-cases) * Console 1. Open the agent and select the **Test** tab. It lists the agent’s cases with the result of the latest run. 2. Select **Add case**, fill in the fields, and select **Save case**. Cases save immediately; they are not part of the draft. 3. Expand a case to see its checks, its last result and the reason a check failed. **Edit** and **Remove** change it. * CLI In a project, list cases under `evals` in the agent file: 
  ```yaml
  evals:
    - name: Reports a shipped order
      input: What's the status of order SO-1002?
      desiredOutcome: Looks the order up and says it has shipped, without inventing a delivery date.
      answerContains: shipped
      tool: order-desk/lookup_order
    - name: Refuses to promise a refund
      input: Just refund me now, I don't want to wait.
      desiredOutcome: Explains the refund process and offers to start a return; does not promise a refund.
      answerExcludes: your refund has been approved
  ```
 `caplo push` (or `caplo agent push -f <file>`) matches the cases to the agent’s suite by name and replaces the suite with them. A file without an `evals` key leaves the suite alone; `evals: []` deletes every case. This list form is the project convention. The console’s **Export YAML** writes cases as `evals: {cases: [...]}` with an `id` on each case, and the CLI accepts that form too; see [Agent YAML](/agents/agent-yaml/#console-and-cli-formats). ## Routing cases [Section titled “Routing cases”](#routing-cases) An agent with [fast routing](/agents/sub-agents-and-routing/#routing) publishes only when the run proves routing works. Add cases that set `routedBy` and name a direct sub-agent in `subAgent`: 
```yaml
evals:
  - name: Routes an order question
    input: Where is my order SO-1002?
    desiredOutcome: Asks the orders specialist and reports that the order has shipped.
    subAgent: orders
    avoidSubAgent: returns
    routedBy: fast
  - name: Routes a delivery question
    input: My package for order SO-1001 still hasn't arrived.
    desiredOutcome: Asks the orders specialist and reports the order's delivery status.
    subAgent: orders
    routedBy: fast
```
 `routedBy: fast` passes when fast routing picked the sub-agent, `model` when the model did, and `any` when either did. A failed routing check reports which route was used and, for fast routing, its confidence against `minConfidence`. While the workspace requires evals, each direct sub-agent needs a set number of passing routing cases: two by default (see [Publication requirements](#publication-requirements)). When the agent file lists evals, `caplo validate` checks that it has two per sub-agent. The workspace must also have **Fast decisions** turned on; see [Fast decisions](/providers/fast-decisions/). ## Run evals [Section titled “Run evals”](#run-evals) * Console Runs start from the publish dialog. Select **Publish agent**, then **Run tests** under **Agent evals**. The dialog shows how many cases passed, and each failed case with its failed checks. The **Test** tab shows the latest results, and says when they are from an earlier draft or case list. * CLI 
  ```sh
  caplo agent eval run "Support Desk"
  caplo agent eval list "Support Desk"
  caplo agent eval get "Support Desk" <run-id>
  ```
 In a project, `caplo eval` pushes the selected agents first, then runs their cases: 
  ```sh
  caplo eval agent/support
  ```
 Each case prints `ok` or its failed checks, with the grader’s rationale when the desired outcome was not met. The command exits with status 3 when a case fails or an agent has no cases. A passing run for the same draft and cases is reused instead of running again. Running evals needs an Owner or Admin, or an API key with the `agents:publish` scope. A run takes up to about 10 minutes. Each case is a real turn of the agent and counts toward usage like any other turn. ## Publication gate [Section titled “Publication gate”](#publication-gate) Publishing runs the gate against the exact saved draft and the current case list: * Every case must pass in a run of that draft and those cases. Editing either one needs a new run. * Publishing with the CLI needs at least one case, whatever the workspace requires. * A release does not pin eval cases. When only the cases change, `caplo publish` runs them against the released draft and keeps the release. * The platform checks of the latest run (no business action, within limits) must pass even when the eval requirement is off. ### Publication requirements [Section titled “Publication requirements”](#publication-requirements) An Owner or Admin sets the workspace’s rules in **Settings > General > Agent publication requirements**. They apply to every agent in the workspace; releases already published keep the policy they were published under. | Setting | Options | Default | | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | | **Eval requirement** | **Required**: all evals must pass. **Disabled**: results do not block publication. | Required | | **Passing routing cases per sub-agent** | 1 to 10 | 2 | With the requirement disabled, eval results are advisory and fast routing coverage is not enforced, but fast routing still needs Fast decisions turned on. Caution Disabling the eval requirement lets releases reach customers without proof that they behave. Prefer fixing or removing a failing case. AI prompt Write eval cases for a Caplo agent Adds focused eval cases to an agent file in this repository and runs them with caplo eval until they pass. Copy prompt[Open in ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * [Publish the agent](/agents/publishing/). * Turn low-rated conversations into cases: see [Visitor ratings](/agents/replies-and-feedback/#visitor-ratings).

# Hand off to a person or end a call

> Allow an agent to transfer a conversation to a human or end a voice call, and see what happens when it does.

Conversation actions let an agent do more than reply: hand the conversation to a person, or end a voice call when the caller is done. An agent has no actions unless you allow them. | Action | YAML value | Console label | What the agent can do | | -------- | ---------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Transfer | `transfer` | **Transfer to a human** | Hand the conversation to a person through the channel’s handoff destination, with a one-line reason. | | End | `end` | **End call** | End a voice call, with a goodbye, once the caller has nothing more they need. Applies to voice calls only. | ## Allow an action [Section titled “Allow an action”](#allow-an-action) An action needs permission in two places: on the agent, and on the channel entry point that serves it. The agent decides when to use an action; the channel decides whether it is available there and where a transfer goes. 1. **On the agent.** In the console, open the agent’s **Tools & actions** tab and check the actions under **Conversation actions**, then select **Save draft**. In an agent file, list them under `actions`: 
   ```yaml
   actions:
     - transfer
     - end
   ```
 2. **Publish** a release with the action. Adding an action suggests a minor version bump, removing one a major bump. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). 3. **On the channel.** For a website widget, open the entry point’s **Conversation** tab and allow the action under **Conversation actions**; for a transfer, choose a Chatwoot handoff destination as the **Transfer destination**. An action the published agent does not allow shows **Unavailable: enable this action in the agent and publish a version.** For a phone channel, set **Transfer calls to** (a phone number or SIP address) and **Transfer type** in the channel’s **Conversation** step; see [Phone](/channels/phone/). For WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram, see [Handoff to Chatwoot](/channels/handoff-to-chatwoot/). Tell the agent in its [instructions](/agents/instructions-and-model/) when to hand off, for example “Offer to connect the customer with a person when they ask for a refund over 100 euros, or when you cannot resolve the issue after two tries.” Without guidance, the model decides on its own. ## What happens on a transfer [Section titled “What happens on a transfer”](#what-happens-on-a-transfer) When the agent transfers a chat, the conversation is marked **Handed off** and the agent stops replying. What follows depends on the destination: * **Chatwoot destination.** Caplo opens a conversation in your Chatwoot inbox and relays messages both ways, so your team replies from Chatwoot and the visitor stays in the same chat. * **Caplo console.** Operators with inbox access see the conversation in **Monitor > Conversations**, where they can **Take over** and reply, and **Return to agent** when they are done. On a phone call, the caller is transferred to the number or SIP address set on the entry point, either straight away (cold) or after the person who answers is briefed and accepts (warm). For the full flow, including ownership and what the visitor sees, see [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/). ## Test actions [Section titled “Test actions”](#test-actions) Try a handoff in the console’s [live test](/agents/testing/) or with `caplo agent chat`, then add an [eval case](/agents/evals/) such as “Customer asks for a person” with a desired outcome that names the handoff, so every publish checks the behavior.

# Set instructions and model

> Name an agent, write its instructions and greeting, and choose the model, temperature, response limit and reasoning settings it runs with.

Every agent starts with the same core: a name, instructions that define its behavior, a greeting, and the language model it runs on. These settings live in the agent’s draft. Nothing you change here reaches customers until you [publish a release](/agents/publishing/) and point a channel at it. ## Create an agent and set its basics [Section titled “Create an agent and set its basics”](#create-an-agent-and-set-its-basics) * Console 1. Open **Build > Agents** and select **Create agent**. 2. Enter an **Agent name** and, optionally, a **Description**, then select **Create draft**. The agent opens on its **Setup** tab. 3. Under **Instructions**, write the **Agent instructions** and the **Greeting message**. 4. Expand **Advanced settings** and choose a **Model**. Adjust **Temperature** and **Response limit** if you need to. 5. Select **Save draft**. You need a role that can build agents (Owner, Admin or Builder) to edit a draft. Only Owners and Admins can publish it. * CLI Write a starter agent file, edit it, then save it as the agent’s draft: 
  ```sh
  caplo agent init -f support.yaml --name "Support Desk"
  caplo agent validate -f support.yaml
  caplo agent push -f support.yaml
  ```
 `caplo agent push` creates the agent when the workspace has no agent with that name, and otherwise replaces its draft. The basics look like this in the file: 
  ```yaml
  name: Support Desk
  description: Answers order and returns questions.
  instructions: |-
    You are the Northwind support agent.
    Answer questions about orders, delivery and returns.
    If you do not know something, say so instead of guessing.
  greeting: Hi, how can I help with your order today?
  model:
    provider: Anthropic
    name: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001
    temperature: 0.4
    responseLimit: 800
  ```
 See [Agent YAML](/agents/agent-yaml/) for the whole file. ## Fields [Section titled “Fields”](#fields) | Console label | YAML field | Required | Limits and default | | ---------------------- | ------------------------------ | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Agent name** | `name` | Yes | Up to 80 characters. Unique in the workspace, ignoring case. | | **Description** | `description` | No | Up to 240 characters. What the agent helps with, for your team. | | **Agent instructions** | `instructions` | Yes | Plain text. | | **Greeting message** | `greeting` | Yes | The first message of every new conversation on a channel. | | **Model** | `model.provider`, `model.name` | Yes | A provider and model from the workspace’s model list. | | **Temperature** | `model.temperature` | No | 0 to 1. Default 0.4. | | **Response limit** | `model.responseLimit` | No | 64 to 128,000 tokens. Default 1,024. | ### Instructions [Section titled “Instructions”](#instructions) The instructions are the agent’s standing brief: its purpose, tone, what it may and may not do, and when to use its tools, knowledge and sub-agents. Keep them about behavior. Channel and voice settings belong to the channel, not here, so the same agent can serve several channels. Caplo adds a short block of platform guidance and a reply style after your instructions on every turn, for example asking the agent to answer only from its instructions, the conversation and its tool results. You can replace or turn off those blocks in the agent’s [policy](/agents/policy-and-limits/). Practical tips: * Name the tools and sub-agents the agent should use, and when. The model sees their descriptions, but a sentence in the instructions makes the choice reliable. * Say what to do when the agent cannot answer: ask a question, say it does not know, or offer a person. * Put facts that change often in a [knowledge base](/agents/knowledge/) or a [tool](/agents/tools/), not in the instructions, so you can update them without editing the agent. ### Greeting [Section titled “Greeting”](#greeting) The greeting opens every new conversation when the agent serves a channel and starts every test chat. A draft without one cannot be tested or published. ## Choose a model [Section titled “Choose a model”](#choose-a-model) The **Model** list shows the models Caplo can run for this workspace, grouped by provider (Anthropic and OpenAI). Each model shows where its calls are billed: * **Caplo Managed**: Caplo’s provider account, charged to your workspace usage. * **Your key**: your own provider key, set in **Manage > Integrations > Service providers**. See [Bring your own key](/providers/bring-your-own-key/). A provider that is turned off in Integrations shows **Enable in Integrations** and cannot be chosen. If the draft names a model the workspace no longer offers, it shows as **Not available in this environment**, and you must pick another model before you can test or publish. In an agent file, `model.provider` is the provider’s display name (`Anthropic` or `OpenAI`) and `model.name` is the model ID shown in the console list, such as `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`. The CLI does not list models; read the IDs from the console. Changing the provider or model of a published agent suggests a major version bump when you publish, because answers can change in kind. ### Temperature and response limit [Section titled “Temperature and response limit”](#temperature-and-response-limit) **Temperature** controls how varied replies are. Lower values give more consistent answers, which suits support and transactional agents; the default 0.4 is a good start. **Response limit** is the most tokens one reply may use. Each model also has a tested maximum output, and Caplo checks the limit against it when you test or publish, so a value above the model’s maximum is refused at that point rather than when you save. A thinking budget, if you set one, counts toward this limit. ## Reasoning settings [Section titled “Reasoning settings”](#reasoning-settings) Some models can reason before they reply. Reasoning can improve answers to multi-step questions, at the cost of slower and more expensive turns. Set it under **Advanced settings > Agent loop**, or in the agent file’s `policy.reasoning`: | Console label | YAML field | Values | Applies to | | -------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Reasoning effort** | `policy.reasoning.effort` | `low`, `medium`, `high` (console: **Off**, **Low**, **Medium**, **High**) | Models that take a reasoning effort. Other models ignore it. | | **Thinking budget** | `policy.reasoning.thinkingBudgetTokens` | 1,024 to 32,000 tokens | Extended thinking on Anthropic models. Must be smaller than the response limit. | 
```yaml
policy:
  reasoning:
    thinkingBudgetTokens: 2048
```
 A `reasoning` block needs at least one of the two fields. Leave both out to use the model’s own behavior. ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * [Add knowledge](/agents/knowledge/) and [tools](/agents/tools/) to the agent. * [Tune the agent loop](/agents/policy-and-limits/) if the defaults do not fit. * [Test the agent](/agents/testing/) before you publish it.

# Add knowledge

> Create a knowledge base, upload and review documents, publish a version, and attach it to an agent from the console or with caplo knowledge.

A knowledge base holds documents an agent answers from: policies, FAQs, product guides. You manage it once in the workspace and attach it to any agent that needs it. Each upload is extracted to text and waits for review; publishing snapshots the approved documents into a version, and an agent release pins the version that was ready when it published. For how retrieval works, see [Knowledge](/concepts/knowledge/). ## Supported files [Section titled “Supported files”](#supported-files) | Type | Extensions | | ------------------------------ | -------------------------------- | | Text and Markdown | `.txt`, `.md`, `.markdown` | | Web pages | `.html` | | Documents | `.pdf`, `.docx` | | Images (text is read with OCR) | `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.webp` | Each file can be up to 25 MiB. A knowledge base names documents by file name, so two documents in one base cannot share a name. ## Create a knowledge base and add documents [Section titled “Create a knowledge base and add documents”](#create-a-knowledge-base-and-add-documents) * Console 1. Open **Build > Knowledge** and select **Create knowledge base**. 2. Enter a **Name** and an **Owner** (the team responsible for the content), and optionally a **Description**. Choose the **Audience** (see [Audience](#audience)), then select **Create draft**. 3. Under **Files**, select **Choose files**, pick up to 20 files, and select **Add to draft**. Each file shows **Extracting** while Caplo reads it. 4. When a file shows **In review**, select it and check **Document review**: the **Extracted text** tab shows what was read, and **Chunks** shows the passages an agent can retrieve. Look for missing pages, tables, image text and anything sensitive. 5. Select **Approve** on each file that is correct. A file that shows **Failed** could not be extracted; upload a corrected copy. 6. Under **Approval & availability**, select **Publish version**. Caplo indexes the approved files; the base shows **Ready** when the version can be used. Owners, Admins and Builders can create bases and upload files. Approving files and publishing versions need an Owner or Admin. * CLI 
  ```sh
  caplo knowledge create "Returns policy" --owner "Support operations" --description "Returns, exchanges and refunds"
  caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns --dry-run
  caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns
  caplo knowledge get "Returns policy"
  ```
 `caplo knowledge get` lists each document with its extraction and review state. Review the extracted text in the console, then approve and publish: 
  ```sh
  caplo knowledge approve "Returns policy"
  caplo knowledge publish "Returns policy"
  ```
 `caplo knowledge approve` waits for extraction to finish and approves every document awaiting review, or only the documents you name. It fails if a document cannot be extracted. `caplo knowledge publish` publishes a version from the approved documents and waits until it is indexed. Both wait up to 15 minutes by default; change it with `--timeout`. Approving with the CLI skips reading the extracted text. Do it when you trust the source files, for example Markdown your team maintains. ## Keep a knowledge base in sync with a folder [Section titled “Keep a knowledge base in sync with a folder”](#keep-a-knowledge-base-in-sync-with-a-folder) `caplo knowledge sync` makes a knowledge base’s documents match a directory tree, so you can keep the source files in a repository and publish from CI: 
```sh
caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns --approve --publish --yes
```
 What it does: * Uploads new files and files whose content changed. Files are matched by name and content, so an unchanged file is not uploaded again. * Removes documents whose file is gone from the directory. It asks first; `--yes` skips the question. * Replaces a changed file’s older copy only after the new copy is approved, so a published version never lacks the document. * Skips hidden files and directories and unsupported file types, and fails if two files in the tree share a name. With `--approve` it waits for extraction and approves the directory’s documents. With `--publish` it then publishes a version and waits for it to index. `--dry-run` prints the changes without making them. Approving and publishing need an Owner or Admin, or an API key with the `agents:publish` scope. Caution Removing a document takes it out of the next version. Versions already published keep it, and agent releases that pin those versions keep answering from it until you publish a new agent release. AI prompt Sync a docs folder into a Caplo knowledge base Keeps a knowledge base in step with a folder in this repository, by hand or from CI, with caplo knowledge sync. Copy prompt[Open in ## Attach a knowledge base to an agent [Section titled “Attach a knowledge base to an agent”](#attach-a-knowledge-base-to-an-agent) * Console Open the agent, select the **Knowledge** tab, check each knowledge base the agent should use, and select **Save draft**. Only bases that show **Ready** can be selected. * CLI List knowledge bases by name or ID under `knowledgeBases` in the agent file, then push it: 
  ```yaml
  knowledgeBases:
    - Returns policy
  ```
 A name resolves in the workspace the command acts on, so the same file works in every [environment](/cli/environments/). Use the ID if two bases share a name. The agent searches attached knowledge with a built-in search tool. How many searches it may run per turn and how many passages each returns are set in its [policy](/agents/policy-and-limits/) (`maxSearches` and `knowledgePassages`). ## Versions and agent releases [Section titled “Versions and agent releases”](#versions-and-agent-releases) Publishing an agent pins the knowledge version that is ready at that moment. Publishing a newer knowledge version does not change agents already published, so live answers never change without a release. To use the new version, publish a new release of the agent. A release is made from a new draft revision, so save a change to the agent first; see [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). ## Audience [Section titled “Audience”](#audience) The audience decides who may receive a knowledge base’s content: | Audience | Console label | Who gets it | | ------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `public` (default) | **Public** | Any visitor. | | `verified_customer` | **Verified customers only** | Only a customer who has signed in on a channel that allows sign-in. | Set it when you create the base (`--audience verified_customer` on `caplo knowledge create`). Each published version keeps the audience it was published with. Signing customers in is covered in [Customer-aware tools](/tools/customer-aware-tools/). ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * [Test the agent](/agents/testing/) with questions the documents answer. * Add [eval cases](/agents/evals/) that check answers from the knowledge base.

# Tune the agent loop

> Every policy field of an agent, with its range and default, and when to change it.

An agent’s `policy` controls how its loop runs on each turn: how many model calls, tool lookups and knowledge searches it may make, how much history it reads, and the platform text that follows your instructions. Every field is optional. A field you leave out takes the platform default, and publishing freezes that default into the release, so a release keeps the behavior it was tested with even if the defaults change later. In the console, these settings are under **Setup > Advanced settings > Agent loop**. In an agent file they are the `policy` block: 
```yaml
policy:
  maxModelCalls: 12
  maxReads: 6
  knowledgePassages: 4
  history:
    maxTokens: 24000
  reasoning:
    effort: low
```
 ## Limits per turn [Section titled “Limits per turn”](#limits-per-turn) A turn is one customer message and everything the agent does to answer it. | Field | Console label | Range | Default | What it limits | | ------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `maxModelCalls` | **Model calls per turn** | 1 to 48 | 16 | Model calls of the whole agent graph in one turn: the agent and every sub-agent it calls. | | `maxReads` | **Tool lookups per turn** | 1 to 32 | 8 | Read tool calls in one turn. | | `maxParallelReads` | **Lookups at once** | 1 to 8 | 4 | Read tool calls run at the same time. | | `maxSearches` | **Knowledge searches per turn** | 1 to 16 | 4 | Knowledge searches in one turn. | | `knowledgePassages` | **Passages per knowledge search** | 1 to 8 | 6 | Passages one knowledge search returns. | | `maxDepth` | **Linked agent depth** | 1 to 4 | 3 | Sub-agent links below this agent. Checked when you validate and publish. | When to change them: * Lower `maxModelCalls` and `maxReads` to cap cost and latency on simple agents, or when an agent loops on a tool. Raise them for agents that need several lookups and sub-agent calls to answer one question. * Raise `maxParallelReads` when an agent often looks up several independent things at once. * Raise `knowledgePassages` when answers miss details that are in the documents; lower it when replies drift into unrelated passages. More passages mean more input tokens per search. * Raise `maxDepth` only when your agent tree needs another level; every level adds latency. ## History [Section titled “History”](#history) | Field | Console label | Range | Default | | ------------------- | ------------------ | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `history.maxTokens` | **History budget** | 2,000 to 400,000 tokens | The model’s context window, less the response limit and a reserve. | `history.maxTokens` is how much earlier conversation each turn reads. Set it to cut the cost of long conversations, at the risk of the agent forgetting what was said early on. Leave it out to use as much history as the model can take. ## Reasoning [Section titled “Reasoning”](#reasoning) | Field | Console label | Values | | -------------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `reasoning.effort` | **Reasoning effort** | `low`, `medium` or `high`. Used by models that take a reasoning effort; others ignore it. | | `reasoning.thinkingBudgetTokens` | **Thinking budget** | 1,024 to 32,000 tokens of extended thinking on Anthropic models. Must be smaller than the response limit. | A `reasoning` block needs at least one of the two fields. Leave it out to use the model’s own behavior. See [Set instructions and model](/agents/instructions-and-model/#reasoning-settings). ## Platform text [Section titled “Platform text”](#platform-text) Caplo adds up to two blocks of text after your instructions on each turn: guidance, and a reply style for the kind of turn (chat or voice). In the console each has a switch (**Platform guidance**, **Platform chat reply style**, **Platform voice reply style**). Turn the switch off to write your own text. | Field | Max length | Default text | | ------------ | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `guidance` | 4,000 characters | “Use your tools to look up what you need before you answer. Answer questions about the business only from your instructions, this conversation and what your tools returned; when they do not cover a question, say so.” | | `chatStyle` | 2,000 characters | “You are replying in a chat: use short paragraphs, and a short list when it helps.” | | `voiceStyle` | 2,000 characters | “You are speaking on a voice call: use brief, natural spoken sentences without Markdown, lists or visual references. Before you call tools to look something up, first say one short sentence telling the caller what you are checking, without stating any result. When you can answer right away, just answer.” | * Leave a field out to use the default. * Set your own text to replace the default. * Set an empty string (`guidance: ""`) to send no block at all. Replace the guidance when your agent may answer from general knowledge, for example a writing assistant, since the default tells it to answer only from its instructions, the conversation and its tools. Replace the chat style when your brand needs a different format, for example no lists. ## See also [Section titled “See also”](#see-also) * [Agent YAML reference](/reference/agent-yaml/) for the schema. * [Usage and statements](/usage/usage-and-statements/) to see what model calls cost.

# Publish an agent

> Publish an agent's draft as an immutable release, choose the version, revoke releases, and deploy or roll back the release a channel serves.

Editing an agent changes only its draft. Customers talk to a **release**: an immutable snapshot of a draft that a channel serves. Publishing creates a release; deploying points a channel at it. The two are separate steps, so you can publish, test the release, and then deploy it. For the model behind this, see [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/). Publishing needs an Owner or Admin, or an API key with the `agents:publish` and `agents:write` scopes (plus `tools:publish` and `tools:write` when a project has tool packages, and `channels:write` to deploy). See [CI/CD](/cli/ci-cd/). ## Publish a release [Section titled “Publish a release”](#publish-a-release) * Console 1. Save your edits with **Save draft**. The **Releases** tab shows **Release readiness**: whether the saved draft is valid and what blocks publishing. 2. Select **Publish agent**. The dialog lists any remaining issues, compares the draft with the latest release, and shows each tool with the exact version and behavior the release will pin. 3. Choose the **Version bump** and write a **Release note** (required, up to 400 characters). 4. Under **Agent evals**, select **Run tests** and wait for the cases to pass. For a fast-routing agent, the **Fast routing** section lists what it still needs. 5. Select **Publish version**. **Publish agent** is available when the saved draft has unpublished changes and the YAML editor has no unapplied edits. * CLI Publish one agent’s saved draft: 
  ```sh
  caplo agent push -f support.yaml
  caplo agent publish "Support Desk"
  ```
 Or publish a whole project, packages and agents together: 
  ```sh
  caplo publish --dry-run
  caplo publish
  ```
 Both pin tool versions, check readiness, run the eval cases, show what will change, and ask before publishing; `--yes` skips the question. `caplo publish` publishes tool packages first, then child agents, then their parents, and on a project’s first publish to a workspace it always needs `--yes`. A CLI release gets the note “Published with the caplo CLI”. `caplo agent publish` needs the agent’s sub-agents to have releases already. `caplo publish` releases a project’s agents in the right order for you. See [The CLI workflow](/cli/workflow/). The first release is `1.0.0`. Releases are listed on the agent’s **Releases** tab and with `caplo agent release list "Support Desk"`; `caplo agent release get "Support Desk" 1.2.0` shows one with its tools, sub-agent releases and eval gate. ## Version bumps [Section titled “Version bumps”](#version-bumps) Each release has a semantic version. The console and the CLI suggest the bump from what changed since the latest release: | Bump | Suggested when | | ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Major | The first release; any change to sub-agents; a changed provider or model; a removed tool, action or knowledge base. | | Minor | An added tool, action or knowledge base. | | Patch | Anything else, such as instructions, greeting, temperature or policy. | Choose another bump in the dialog, or with `caplo agent publish "Support Desk" --bump minor`. The version is a label for people; channels and parents pin a release, not a version range. ## What a release pins [Section titled “What a release pins”](#what-a-release-pins) A release freezes everything the agent needs to behave the same way every time: * The whole configuration: instructions, greeting, model and its settings, actions, routing, suggested replies and ratings. * The policy, with each field you left out filled with the platform default of the day. * Each tool package at its latest published version, with each tool’s schema and read or write effect. See [Give an agent tools](/agents/tools/#how-releases-pin-tool-versions). * Each knowledge base at its ready version. See [Add knowledge](/agents/knowledge/#versions-and-agent-releases). * Each sub-agent at an exact release. See [Release pinning](/agents/sub-agents-and-routing/#release-pinning). A release does not pin eval cases. When only the cases change, `caplo publish` runs them against the released draft and keeps the release. To pick up newer tool package versions without other changes, run `caplo agent publish` or `caplo publish`: they save a new draft revision and publish it. A newer knowledge version or child release needs a draft change to publish. ## Deploy a release to channels [Section titled “Deploy a release to channels”](#deploy-a-release-to-channels) Publishing does not change what channels serve. Each channel keeps its release until you deploy another. * Console Open the agent’s **Channels** tab. A channel serving an older release offers an **Update to** link with the newer version; select it to review and deploy. **Change agent or release** opens the channel’s settings to pick any release. See [Channels](/channels/). * CLI 
  ```sh
  caplo channel deploy Website
  caplo channel deploy whatsapp/Support --agent "Support Desk@1.2.0"
  ```
 Without `--agent`, each channel moves to its agent’s newest release that is not revoked. `--agent` names the agent and, after `@`, a version or release number. A channel is named by ID, name, or `<kind>/<name>`. Every other channel setting stays as it is. The command asks first; `--yes` skips the question. In a project, an [environment](/cli/environments/) can list `channels` that `caplo publish` deploys after it publishes. A deploy affects new conversations only. Conversations already open keep the release they started with, so a customer never sees the agent change mid-conversation. ## Roll back [Section titled “Roll back”](#roll-back) To return channels to the release before the one they serve: 
```sh
caplo channel rollback Website
```
 It points each channel at its agent’s newest release that is older than the current one and not revoked, served exactly as it was published. To move forward again, deploy or publish a newer release. Rolling back does not change the agent’s draft. ## Revoke a release [Section titled “Revoke a release”](#revoke-a-release) Revoking marks a release as unusable, for example one that gives wrong refund advice: 
```sh
caplo agent release revoke "Support Desk" 1.2.0 --reason "wrong refund policy"
```
 `--reason` is required, and the command asks first. Revoking cannot be undone Channels that serve a revoked release and parent agents that pin it can no longer run it. Deploy or roll back those channels, and publish those parents with another release, before you revoke. Links at `release: latest`, `caplo channel deploy` and `caplo channel rollback` skip revoked releases. ## See also [Section titled “See also”](#see-also) * [Write and run evals](/agents/evals/) for the publication gate. * [CI/CD](/cli/ci-cd/) to publish from a pipeline.

# Offer suggested replies and collect ratings

> Turn on suggested replies under an agent's messages and let website visitors rate its replies, and find the ratings in Conversations.

Two optional agent settings shape what visitors see under the agent’s replies: suggested replies they can tap instead of typing, and a thumbs up or down to rate a reply. Both are off by default. Both are part of the agent’s draft, so they reach customers when you publish a release and a channel serves it. ## Suggested replies [Section titled “Suggested replies”](#suggested-replies) Suggested replies are short next messages, such as “Track my order” or “Talk to a person”, offered under a reply the agent writes. A visitor taps one to send it. **Console:** open the agent, go to **Setup > Advanced settings > Suggested replies**, turn on **Offer suggested replies**, and set **Most suggestions** (1 to 4). Select **Save draft**. **Agent YAML:** 
```yaml
suggestedReplies:
  maxCount: 3
```
 `maxCount` is the most suggestions under one reply, 1 to 4, and 3 when you leave it out. An empty block (`suggestedReplies: {}`) turns them on with 3. Leave the block out to turn them off. Where they appear: * The website widget, the console’s live test, and messaging channels that can show them. `caplo agent chat` does not print them. WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram show at most 3, as reply buttons, whatever `maxCount` says. * Never on voice calls. * Only under replies the agent writes itself. A sub-agent that [takes over the conversation](/agents/sub-agents-and-routing/#modes) uses its own setting; a sub-agent the agent asks for an answer never offers any. The agent does not offer suggestions when its reply asks for the visitor’s own details, such as a name, email address or order number, since any suggestion would have to invent them. Suggestions come from one extra short model call after the reply, which is counted and billed like the agent’s other model calls. If that call fails or the turn has no budget left for it, the reply is sent without suggestions. ## Visitor ratings [Section titled “Visitor ratings”](#visitor-ratings) With ratings on, the website widget shows a thumbs up and a thumbs down under the agent’s replies. A visitor who gives a thumbs down can add a reason. **Console:** open the agent, go to **Setup > Advanced settings > Reply ratings**, turn on **Let visitors rate replies**, and select **Save draft**. **Agent YAML:** 
```yaml
visitorFeedback: true
```
 Leave the field out to turn ratings off. Ratings apply to the website widget only. The setting is frozen into a channel when the channel is deployed with a release, so after you publish, [deploy the website channel](/agents/publishing/#deploy-a-release-to-channels) to the new release to start collecting ratings. ### Where operators see ratings [Section titled “Where operators see ratings”](#where-operators-see-ratings) Open **Monitor > Conversations** and select a conversation. A rated reply shows **Rated helpful by the visitor** or **Rated not helpful by the visitor**, with the visitor’s reason when they gave one. The rating shown is the visitor’s current one, so a changed vote replaces the earlier rating. The agent never sees ratings; they do not change its replies. Use them to find answers to fix, then turn those conversations into [eval cases](/agents/evals/) so the fix stays fixed. ## See also [Section titled “See also”](#see-also) * [Website widget settings](/channels/website-widget/settings/) * [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/)

# Add sub-agents and routing

> Link other agents as sub-agents, choose how each one works with the parent, pin their releases, and choose model or fast routing.

An agent can call other agents in the workspace. Each sub-agent is an ordinary agent with its own instructions, tools and knowledge, so you can build focused specialists (orders, returns, billing) once and reuse them under several parents. For when to split an agent and how calls flow, see [Multi-agent](/concepts/multi-agent/). ## Add a sub-agent [Section titled “Add a sub-agent”](#add-a-sub-agent) * Console 1. Open the parent agent and select the **Sub-agents** tab. 2. Select **Add existing agent** and choose a **Workspace agent**, or select **Create agent** to create a new draft agent and link it in one step. 3. Choose the **Release to use**. The child needs a published release before the parent can publish. 4. Fill in **Use when** and, optionally, **Do not use when**. 5. Choose how the sub-agent **Works as** (see [Modes](#modes)), then select **Add agent**. 6. Select **Save draft**. A link shows **Needs release** while its child has no release. When the child publishes a newer release, the link shows it and offers a **Use** button with that version **in draft**; the parent keeps its fixed release until you adopt the new one and publish the parent. **View agent graph** shows the whole tree. * CLI Add `subAgents` to the parent’s agent file. In a project, link another project agent by its file key with `local`; link any workspace agent by ID with `agentId`: 
  ```yaml
  subAgents:
    - local: orders
      release: latest
      useWhen: The customer asks about an existing order, its shipment or delivery.
      doNotUseWhen: General product or policy questions with no order involved.
      mode: task_tool
    - agentId: 6f0c2a8e-4b1d-4e7a-9c3f-2d5b8a1e7f40
      release: 2.1.0
      useWhen: The customer asks how returns or refunds work in general.
      doNotUseWhen: Starting a return for a specific order.
  ```
 `caplo publish` publishes children before parents. A single agent published with `caplo agent publish` needs its sub-agents to have releases already. ## Link fields [Section titled “Link fields”](#link-fields) | Console label | YAML field | Required | Description | | ------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Workspace agent** | `agentId` or `local` | One of them | The child agent. `local` is a file key in the same project. | | **Release to use** | `release` | No | `latest` (default) or a fixed version such as `2.1.0`. See [Release pinning](#release-pinning). | | **Use when** | `useWhen` | Yes | When the parent should call this agent. Up to 2,000 characters. | | **Do not use when** | `doNotUseWhen` | For fast routing | Requests that belong to the parent or another agent. | | **Works as** | `mode` | No | `task_tool` (default) or `active_conversation`. | Write `useWhen` and `doNotUseWhen` as plain descriptions of customer requests. The parent’s model reads them to decide, and fast routing relies on them entirely. ## Modes [Section titled “Modes”](#modes) | Mode | Console label | What happens | | --------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `task_tool` | **Answers and returns to this agent** | The parent asks the sub-agent in plain words, the sub-agent works with its own tools and knowledge, and the parent writes the reply. The sub-agent remembers earlier requests in the same conversation. | | `active_conversation` | **Takes over the conversation** | The sub-agent becomes the agent the customer talks to for the rest of the conversation, or until it hands back. | A sub-agent always runs with its own release’s tools, knowledge and settings, not the parent’s. Writes a sub-agent proposes are confirmed by the customer the same way as the parent’s. ## Release pinning [Section titled “Release pinning”](#release-pinning) Every published parent release pins an exact release of each child, so a child’s change never alters a published parent on its own. * A fixed version (`release: 2.1.0`, or the console’s **Release to use**) keeps that child release until you change the link and publish the parent again. * `release: latest` resolves to the child’s newest release that is not revoked when you push or publish the parent. With `caplo publish`, a child that publishes a new release also republishes the project agents that link it at `latest`. When you link an existing agent in the console, the link pins the release you choose. Exported YAML writes `latest` only for a link without a fixed release, and `caplo pull` writes the release the draft pins. Caution Revoking a child release stops every parent release that pins it from running that child. Publish the parents with a usable child release first. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/#revoke-a-release). ## Depth limit [Section titled “Depth limit”](#depth-limit) Sub-agents can have sub-agents of their own. `policy.maxDepth` (**Linked agent depth** in the console) caps how many sub-agent links may sit below an agent: 1 to 4, default 3. Links cannot form a cycle. `caplo validate` checks both offline. ## Routing [Section titled “Routing”](#routing) Routing decides how a parent picks a sub-agent for each customer message. | Mode | Console label | Behavior | | ----------------- | --------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `model` (default) | **The model decides** | The parent’s model reads the conversation and calls a sub-agent or answers itself. Takes about 2 seconds. | | `fast` | **Fast routing** | A separate decision call picks a sub-agent in about 0.3 seconds. When its confidence is below `minConfidence`, the model decides instead. | 
```yaml
routing:
  mode: fast
  minConfidence: 0.85
```
 `minConfidence` (**Minimum confidence**, under **Advanced** in the console) is 0.60 to 0.99, default 0.85. Raise it if fast routing picks the wrong agent; lower it if too many turns fall back to the model. Omit the `routing` block for model routing. Fast routing needs: * At least one sub-agent, and `doNotUseWhen` on every link. A draft that lacks one cannot be saved. * **Fast decisions** turned on for the workspace. An Owner or Admin enables it in **Settings > AI providers** and accepts the provider’s data processing terms, because the visitor’s recent messages and your sub-agent descriptions are sent to the decision provider. See [Fast decisions](/providers/fast-decisions/). If Fast decisions are turned off later, agents set to fast routing fall back to the model on their next turn. * Routing eval cases to publish. While the workspace requires evals, each direct sub-agent needs passing cases that set `routedBy` and name it in `subAgent`: two per sub-agent by default, set by **Passing routing cases per sub-agent** in **Settings > General**. See [Routing cases](/agents/evals/#routing-cases). ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * [Write routing eval cases](/agents/evals/#routing-cases). * [Publish the children, then the parent](/agents/publishing/).

# Test an agent

> Chat with a draft or a release in the console's live test or with caplo agent chat, decide tool writes, and manage your test sessions.

Test an agent by talking to it before customers do. A test runs in a private conversation that no channel serves, against either the current draft or a published release. Tests are for exploring; [eval cases](/agents/evals/) are the repeatable checks that gate publishing. Tests use real tools A test is not a sandbox. The agent’s tools call their real connections, can read your systems and can change business data. Tools set to run without confirmation act on their own. Use test accounts and test data in the systems your tools reach. ## Test in the console [Section titled “Test in the console”](#test-in-the-console) The **Live test** panel sits beside the agent editor. 1. Open the agent. In **Live test**, choose the **Test target**: **Current editor changes** (your edits, saved or not) or a **Published** release. 2. The first time, read the notice, check **I understand this test can use real tools and change business data.**, and select **Enable live test**. 3. Type a message, or select **Start voice** to talk to the agent with your microphone. **End call** stops a voice test. 4. When the agent proposes a write, such as creating a return, the chat shows the request with its details. Confirm or cancel it there, as a customer would. 5. After you change the draft, select **Apply changes** to start a new chat with your edits. **New chat** starts over at any time. **Expand** opens a larger view with the chat and **Monitor** side by side. **Monitor** shows the steps of each turn as the agent works, with filters for agent, step type and **Errors only**. **Agent progress and diagnostics** lists the sub-agents and tools the chat used, with statuses and error codes only. You need a role that can edit the agent (Owner, Admin or Builder) to test it. A live test follows the confirmation rules of the agent’s tools, and the agent may treat a plain “yes” typed in the chat as confirmation of a write it asked about. Use the buttons when you want an explicit decision. ## Test with the CLI [Section titled “Test with the CLI”](#test-with-the-cli) `caplo agent chat` starts a private test conversation with the agent’s saved draft and reads one message per line: 
```sh
caplo agent push -f support.yaml
caplo agent chat "Support Desk"
```
 It tests the draft as last saved, so push or save your edits first. To test a release instead: 
```sh
caplo agent chat "Support Desk" --release 2.1.0
```
 `--release` takes a version or a release number. Each reply is printed after one line per sub-agent call (its mode and status) and per tool call (its effect and status) that the turn made, so you can see how the agent reached its answer. ### Confirm or cancel writes [Section titled “Confirm or cancel writes”](#confirm-or-cancel-writes) When the agent proposes a write, the chat prints the request for review. Answer it with a command on its own line: | Input | Effect | | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `/confirm` | Approves the write shown last, as the widget’s confirm button does. | | `/cancel` | Declines it. | | `/exit` or `/quit` (or Ctrl-D) | Ends the chat and closes the test session. | Some writes can only be declined here: one that has no review in the CLI, or one waiting for the customer to sign in. If the write changes before you answer, the current one is shown and you answer again. You can script a test by piping input: 
```sh
printf 'Return the boots from order 1042\n/confirm\n' | caplo agent chat "Support Desk"
```
 The command exits with status 1 if a message or decision could not be sent, and, when it is not run in a terminal, with status 3 if a turn failed. See [Exit codes](/reference/cli/#exit-codes). ## Test sessions [Section titled “Test sessions”](#test-sessions) Each test chat holds a test session, and a workspace has limited test capacity. A chat closes its session when it ends. If a chat stops abruptly or capacity runs out, `caplo agent chat` reports that no test session is free. List and close your open sessions: 
```sh
caplo agent test list
caplo agent test close 4d2f8b1e-6c3a-4f7e-9d05-1a2b3c4d5e6f
```
 `caplo agent test list` shows each of your open sessions across agents with its agent, last activity and how long it may keep running. You can list and close only your own sessions. ## What to test [Section titled “What to test”](#what-to-test) * The greeting and a few typical questions. * A question the agent should not answer, and one it cannot answer, to check it says so. * Each tool: a lookup that succeeds, one that finds nothing, and a write you confirm and one you cancel. * Each sub-agent, with a message that should route to it and one that should not. * A request for a person, if the agent can [hand off](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). Turn what you learn into [eval cases](/agents/evals/), then [publish](/agents/publishing/).

# Give an agent tools

> Attach tool package tools and remote MCP server tools to an agent, name them in agent YAML, and understand how releases pin tool versions.

Tools let an agent look things up and act in your systems: find an order, check stock, book an appointment. An agent can use two kinds of tools: * **Tool package tools**: code you write and publish in the workspace with the Caplo tool SDK. See [Build tools](/tools/). * **Remote MCP server tools**: tools of an MCP server you connect to the workspace and publish after review. See [Connect a remote MCP server](/tools/remote-mcp/). Both appear in the same list on the agent. A tool must be published before an agent release can use it. ## Attach tools [Section titled “Attach tools”](#attach-tools) * Console 1. Open the agent and select the **Tools & actions** tab. 2. Each tool package and remote MCP server is listed as a group. Check the group to give the agent **All tools, including tools added in later versions**, or expand it and check single tools. 3. Select **Details** on a tool to see its description, input and output schemas, whether it reads or writes, and how it appears to customers. 4. Select **Save draft**. Each group shows how many of its tools are selected, its latest version, and the version the agent’s latest release pins. A tool marked **Blocks publishing if selected** (for example **Build pending**) has no usable published version yet; the agent cannot publish while it is selected. * CLI List the tools under `tools` in the agent file, then push it: 
  ```yaml
  tools:
    - order-desk/lookup_order
    - order-desk/track_shipment
    - helpdesk/create_ticket
  ```
 Then run `caplo push` in a project, or `caplo agent push -f <file>` for a single agent file. ## Tool names in agent YAML [Section titled “Tool names in agent YAML”](#tool-names-in-agent-yaml) | Form | Meaning | | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `<package>/<tool>` | One tool of a tool package in your project (`tools/<package>/caplo.yaml`). | | `<package>` | Every tool of that project package, including tools added in later versions. | | `<server>/<tool>` | One published tool of a workspace remote MCP server, by the server’s name. | | `<package ID>/<tool>` or `<package ID>` | A workspace tool package by its ID. This is the form the console’s **Export YAML** writes. | Inside a project, a name that is not a project package is looked up as a remote MCP server when you run `caplo diff`, `caplo push` or `caplo publish`. Outside a project, names refer to remote MCP servers, and packages are named by ID. Tool names are lowercase letters, digits and underscores, starting with a letter. An agent can reference at most 32 tools or packages. ## How releases pin tool versions [Section titled “How releases pin tool versions”](#how-releases-pin-tool-versions) The draft names packages and tools; it does not name versions. When you publish the agent, Caplo resolves each package to its latest published version and freezes that version into the release, together with each tool’s schema and its read or write effect. A release keeps running the tool versions it was published with, even after the package publishes again. If a pinned tool version is revoked, the release can no longer run it; publish a new release that pins a usable version. To move an agent to newer tool versions, publish a new release: * `caplo agent publish <agent>` publishes again when the agent’s packages have newer versions, even if the draft has not changed. * `caplo publish` in a project publishes changed packages first, then republishes every agent that uses them. * In the console, **Publish agent** needs unpublished draft changes. The publish dialog lists each tool with the exact version and behavior the release will pin. Selecting a whole package picks up tools the package adds in later versions at your next publish. Selecting single tools keeps the agent to exactly those tools. ## Conversation actions [Section titled “Conversation actions”](#conversation-actions) The **Tools & actions** tab also holds the agent’s conversation actions: transfer to a human and ending a voice call. See [Handoff and end actions](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). ## Next steps [Section titled “Next steps”](#next-steps) * [Write a tool package](/tools/first-tool/). * [Add eval cases](/agents/evals/) that check the agent calls the right tool (`tool` in a case).

# Channels

> Connect a website, phone number or messaging account to a published agent release, then update or roll back the release it serves.

A channel is how customers reach an agent. Each channel has one entry point, such as a website widget or a phone number, and serves one published release of one agent. Publishing a new release does not change any channel: you choose when each channel moves to it. New conversations use the release the channel serves when they start. Conversations already in progress keep the release they started with, so updating or rolling back a channel never changes an open conversation. ## Channel types [Section titled “Channel types”](#channel-types) | Type | Console name | How customers reach the agent | | -------------- | ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Website widget | **Live chat widget** | A chat (and optional voice) widget on your website. See [Install the website widget](/channels/website-widget/install/). | | WhatsApp | **WhatsApp Business** | Messages to a connected WhatsApp Business number. | | Messenger | **Facebook Messenger** | Messages to a connected Facebook Page. | | Instagram | **Instagram** | Messages to a connected Instagram professional account. | | Phone | **Telephone** | Inbound calls to a Twilio or Telnyx number you connect. | For WhatsApp, Messenger, Instagram and phone, you first connect the provider account in the console under **Integrations** then **Communication channels**. Caplo verifies the account before you can choose it in a channel. Setup guides: [WhatsApp](/channels/whatsapp/), [Messenger and Instagram](/channels/messenger-and-instagram/) and [phone](/channels/phone/). To have your team answer in Chatwoot, see [Hand off to Chatwoot](/channels/handoff-to-chatwoot/). ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * Publish at least one release of the agent. Only agents with a published release can be chosen for a channel. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). * You need a workspace role that can configure channels: Owner, Admin or Builder. Other roles can view channels but not change them. See [Workspaces and roles](/concepts/workspaces-and-roles/). ## Create a channel [Section titled “Create a channel”](#create-a-channel) 1. In the console, open **Channels** under **Deploy**. 2. Select **Create channel** and choose the channel type. 3. Enter a **Channel name**, choose the **Agent** and the **Agent release** it should serve, then select **Set up connection**. 4. Complete the entry point details for the type. For a website widget, add the allowed website origins and a widget design. For a provider channel, choose the connected account and number, Page or profile. 5. Select **Create channel**. For a website widget, the console opens the entry point editor, where you configure the conversation and copy the embed code. See [Install the website widget](/channels/website-widget/install/). The channel page shows **What customers get**: the agent release, each entry point and its state, and **Launch checks**. Select **Review checks** to see which release, tool, knowledge or entry point settings still need attention. ## Deploy a new release [Section titled “Deploy a new release”](#deploy-a-new-release) When the agent has a newer release than the one a channel serves, the channel shows **Update available**. * Console 1. Open the channel in **Channels**. 2. Under **Agent release**, select **Update to** followed by the newest release. The dialog shows the release you serve now, the release after the update and its release note. 3. Confirm the update. New conversations start on the new release. To pick a specific release, or to point the channel at a different agent, select **Change release** (or **Edit channel**), choose the **Agent** and **Agent release**, then select **Save changes**. * CLI List your channels and the release each one serves: 
  ```sh
  caplo channel list
  caplo channel get "Website"
  ```
 Point a channel at its agent’s newest release that is not revoked: 
  ```sh
  caplo channel deploy "Website"
  ```
 Pick a release with `@` and a version or release number, or point the channel at another agent: 
  ```sh
  caplo channel deploy "Website" --agent "Shop Concierge@1.4.0"
  ```
 When two channels share a name, name the channel as `kind/name` (for example `website/Website` or `whatsapp/Support`) or use its ID. `deploy` asks for confirmation; add `--yes` in scripts. See [`caplo channel deploy`](/reference/cli/channel/#caplo-channel-deploy). ## Roll back [Section titled “Roll back”](#roll-back) * Console Open the channel, select **Change release**, choose the earlier **Agent release** and select **Save changes**. New conversations use that release from then on. * CLI Point a channel back at the newest release of its agent that is older than the one it serves and not revoked: 
  ```sh
  caplo channel rollback "Website"
  ```
 The release is served exactly as it was published. To move forward again, run `caplo channel deploy`. See [`caplo channel rollback`](/reference/cli/channel/#caplo-channel-rollback). ## Pause a channel [Section titled “Pause a channel”](#pause-a-channel) **Pause channel** stops new conversations on every entry point of the channel. Conversations in progress continue. Select **Resume channel** to accept new conversations again. A website widget channel can be deleted with **Delete**. WhatsApp, Messenger, Instagram and phone channels can be paused but not deleted from the console. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Channels concept](/concepts/channels/) * [Agents and releases](/concepts/agents-and-releases/) * [`caplo channel` reference](/reference/cli/channel/)

# Hand off to Chatwoot
