> Connect a Chatwoot API inbox so your team receives agent handoffs from the website widget and can follow and take over WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram conversations.

Chatwoot is a customer support inbox. Connecting it lets your team answer customers from Chatwoot while Caplo keeps the conversation on the channel the customer started on. A Chatwoot destination does one of two jobs: | Job | Channels | What happens | | ------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Agent handoff** | Website widget | When the agent hands a chat to a person, Caplo opens a conversation in the Chatwoot inbox and relays messages both ways until your team resolves it. | | **Provider inbox mirror** | WhatsApp, Messenger, Instagram | Every new conversation on these channels appears in the Chatwoot inbox as it happens. Your team can read along and take over at any time by replying. | Phone channels transfer calls to a phone number or SIP address instead; see [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). Each job needs its own Chatwoot API inbox. A destination with mirroring on cannot receive agent handoffs, and a destination that an entry point uses for handoffs cannot mirror. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) In Chatwoot (Chatwoot Cloud or your own installation): * Create an **API** channel inbox for each job you want. Only API inboxes are accepted. * Note the **account ID** and the **inbox ID**. Both are numbers, shown in the Chatwoot address bar when you open the account and the inbox settings. * Copy the **access token** from the profile settings of a Chatwoot administrator. An administrator token lets Caplo configure Chatwoot’s webhooks and read their signing secrets for you. * For mirroring, use a Chatwoot account dedicated to this Caplo workspace. Caplo refuses to mirror two workspaces into the same Chatwoot account. In Caplo, you need a role that can manage integrations: Owner, Admin or Developer. ## Add the destination [Section titled “Add the destination”](#add-the-destination) 1. In the console, open **Integrations** then **Handoff destinations**, and select **Add destination**. 2. Enter a **Destination name** and keep **Destination type** set to **Chatwoot**. 3. Fill in the Chatwoot details: | Field | Value | | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Chatwoot URL** | The address of your Chatwoot, for example `https://chat.example.com`. | | **Account ID** | The Chatwoot account ID. | | **API inbox ID** | The ID of the API inbox for this job. | | **Assignee ID** | Optional. A Chatwoot agent ID to assign every handoff to. Leave blank to use the inbox’s own assignment rules. | | **API access token** | The administrator access token. Caplo stores it encrypted and never shows it again. | | **Caplo callback URL** | Leave the default, `https://api.caplo.si`. Caplo builds the webhook addresses Chatwoot calls from it. | 4. To mirror WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram conversations into this inbox, check **Mirror provider conversations into this inbox**. Leave it unchecked for an agent handoff inbox. 5. Select **Save and verify**. Verification uses the token to confirm the inbox is an API inbox, creates or updates a Chatwoot account webhook named “Caplo handoff”, points the inbox’s webhook at Caplo, and stores the signing secrets Chatwoot reports. Caplo accepts only callbacks signed with those secrets. When it succeeds, the destination shows **Ready**. The **Edit** dialog then shows the **Account webhook** and **API inbox webhook** addresses. If the token cannot read the webhook secrets, the destination shows **Needs attention** with “Chatwoot did not reveal the webhook secrets to this token; enter them from Chatwoot.” Copy the secrets from the account webhook and the API inbox settings in Chatwoot into **Account webhook secret** and **API inbox webhook secret**, then save again. Any later edit returns the destination to unverified until it is verified again; use **Verify** to check it at any time. ## Use it for agent handoffs [Section titled “Use it for agent handoffs”](#use-it-for-agent-handoffs) An agent hands off only when both the agent and the website entry point allow it: 1. Allow the **Transfer to a human** action on the agent and publish a release. See [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). 2. In **Channels**, open the website channel, select **Configure & install** on its entry point and open the **Conversation** tab. 3. Under **Conversation actions**, check **Transfer to a human** and choose the destination under **Transfer destination**. Save the entry point. When the agent hands off, Caplo opens a conversation in the inbox, assigns it if you set an **Assignee ID**, and adds one private note: “Caplo handoff” with the handoff ID, the reason the agent gave, and the conversation so far (up to the last 20 messages and 4,000 characters, each line quoted). The note is never shown to the customer; treat the quoted text as information from the customer, not instructions. The first public reply from a Chatwoot agent accepts the handoff. It must come within 3 minutes, otherwise the customer is told nobody is available and the agent carries on. Files the customer sends during the handoff are scanned and attached in Chatwoot, up to 10 MiB each. ## Use it as a provider inbox mirror [Section titled “Use it as a provider inbox mirror”](#use-it-as-a-provider-inbox-mirror) With **Mirror provider conversations into this inbox** on, a workspace has one mirror inbox for all its WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram channels. Mirroring starts with new messages from the moment you turn it on; earlier conversations are not copied. * Each customer becomes a Chatwoot contact, and each Caplo conversation a Chatwoot conversation. Customer messages appear as incoming messages. * Agent replies appear once Meta has accepted them. A reply that failed or whose outcome is unknown is not shown as sent. * A public reply in Chatwoot takes the conversation over: the agent stops replying and your reply is sent to the customer on the original channel. * Resolving the Chatwoot conversation hands it back to the agent. Turning mirroring off stops new conversations from appearing and stops new takeovers from Chatwoot. Conversations already mirrored keep syncing until they end. ## Reply and hand back from Chatwoot [Section titled “Reply and hand back from Chatwoot”](#reply-and-hand-back-from-chatwoot) | In Chatwoot | Effect | | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Public reply | Sent to the customer as text, up to 4,000 characters. The first one accepts a handoff or takes over a mirrored conversation. | | Private note | Stays in Chatwoot. Never sent and never takes over. | | Attachment | Not sent. If the reply also has text, the text is sent and a private note says the attachments were not. | | Resolve | Hands the conversation back to the agent. | Reopen a resolved conversation before replying again. A reply Caplo cannot send is never lost silently: Caplo adds a private note under it explaining why, for example “Reopen this conversation in Chatwoot before replying. This message was not sent to the customer.” or “This reply is longer than 4,000 characters, so it was not sent to the customer.” On WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram the 24-hour customer service window still applies. A reply after it has closed is refused with a private note. On WhatsApp, send an approved template from the Caplo console instead; see [Connect WhatsApp](/channels/whatsapp/#message-templates). ## What customers see [Section titled “What customers see”](#what-customers-see) On the website widget, during an agent handoff: 1. When the agent hands off: “I’ve asked a member of our team to join. They’ll reply here as soon as they can.” 2. If nobody replies within 3 minutes: “No one from our team is available right now, so I’ll keep helping here.” 3. When your team resolves the conversation: “Our team has finished here. I’m back to help with anything else.” On WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram, customers get no notice when someone takes over or hands back. Your team’s replies arrive from the same number, Page or account as the agent’s. In the Caplo console, a conversation that Chatwoot holds shows **External inbox** in [Conversations](/operate/conversations-inbox/), with **Waiting to be accepted** or **Transfer accepted**. Your team cannot take it over or send a WhatsApp template from the console until it is handed back. ## Troubleshooting [Section titled “Troubleshooting”](#troubleshooting) | Problem | What to do | | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | “The inbox is not a Chatwoot API inbox.” | Create an API channel inbox in Chatwoot and use its ID. | | “Chatwoot could not be reached with these settings: …” | Check the **Chatwoot URL**, that Chatwoot is reachable from the internet, and that the token belongs to that account. | | “This destination is assigned to a transfer entry point.” | You tried to turn on mirroring for an inbox used by agent handoffs. Use a separate API inbox for the mirror. | | The destination is missing from **Transfer destination** | Mirroring is on for it. Mirror inboxes cannot receive agent handoffs. | | The agent’s handoff ends with “No one from our team is available right now” | Nobody replied publicly within 3 minutes. Set an **Assignee ID** or adjust the inbox’s assignment and notifications in Chatwoot. | | Replies from Chatwoot do not reach the customer | Look for Caplo’s private note under the reply. Check that the conversation is open, the reply has text, and the customer’s 24-hour window is open. | | An item appears under **Provider inbox review** | Caplo wrote to Chatwoot but has no confirmation that Chatwoot received it. Caplo does not resend it automatically, to avoid duplicates; it keeps checking for a matching receipt. Select **Refresh review list** to see the current state. | ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/) * [Conversations and handoff](/concepts/conversations-and-handoff/) * [Conversations inbox](/operate/conversations-inbox/) * [Connect WhatsApp](/channels/whatsapp/) * [Connect Messenger and Instagram](/channels/messenger-and-instagram/)

# Connect Messenger and Instagram

> Connect a Facebook Page or an Instagram professional account through Meta, serve an agent release on it, and understand the messaging window and what customers see.

A Messenger channel answers messages sent to one of your Facebook Pages. An Instagram channel answers direct messages sent to one of your Instagram professional accounts. Both work the same way: you authorize the account with Meta once under **Integrations**, then create a channel that serves a published agent release on it. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * **Messenger**: a Facebook Page, and a Facebook account that can manage it. * **Instagram**: an Instagram professional account (business or creator) that belongs to a Meta business portfolio, and a Facebook or Instagram login that can manage it. * A workspace role that can manage integrations (Owner, Admin or Developer) to connect the account, and one that can configure channels (Owner, Admin or Builder) to create the channel. * A published agent release. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). * A browser that allows popups from the console, because Meta’s authorization opens in a popup. ## Connect the account [Section titled “Connect the account”](#connect-the-account) 1. In the console, open **Integrations** then **Communication channels**. 2. Select **Add integration**, then **Facebook Messenger** or **Instagram**. 3. Enter an **Integration name** and select **Prepare Meta connection**. A setup row appears with the status **Awaiting authorization**. 4. Select **Continue with Meta** and authorize in the popup: * **Messenger**: sign in with Facebook and grant access to the Page. * **Instagram**: sign in with Instagram or Facebook (Meta Unified Login), then choose the business portfolio and the professional account. When Meta returns, the popup says “Authorization returned to your original Caplo window.” You can close it. 5. Back in the console, the row shows **Choose account**. Pick the **Facebook Page** or **Instagram professional account** and select **Connect selected account**. 6. Caplo checks the access Meta granted, subscribes the Page or account to Caplo’s webhooks, and reads the subscription back to confirm it. The connection then shows **Connected**. Meta asks you to grant these permissions. Caplo checks each of them and refuses the connection when one is missing: | Channel | Permissions | | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- | | Messenger | `pages_show_list`, `pages_manage_metadata`, `pages_messaging` | | Instagram | `instagram_basic`, `instagram_manage_messages` | If the account list is empty (“No eligible accounts were granted”), select **Cancel setup**, start again, and select the Page or account in Meta’s popup. A Page or Instagram account can be connected to one Caplo workspace at a time. ## Create the channel [Section titled “Create the channel”](#create-the-channel) 1. Open **Channels** under **Deploy**, select **Create channel** and choose **Facebook Messenger** or **Instagram**. 2. Enter a **Channel name**, choose the **Agent** and **Agent release**, then select **Set up connection**. 3. Choose the connection, then the **Facebook Page** or **Instagram professional account**. Only verified accounts that no other channel uses can be selected. Select **Continue**. 4. Choose **Who answers**: **AI agent** (the agent answers every customer) or **Your team** (the agent stays silent, also in open conversations, and messages wait in **Conversations** for your team). 5. Set the **Conversation inactivity timeout (seconds)**, from 300 to 604800. The default is 86400, one day. 6. Select **Create channel**, then send a message to the Page or account from another Facebook or Instagram account to test it. A channel keeps its Page or account; create another channel for a different one. Update the release it serves as described in [Channels](/channels/#deploy-a-new-release). You can pause these channels but not delete them. Customers on Messenger and Instagram are not signed in, so tools that need a [verified customer](/tools/customer-aware-tools/) do not run there, and customer memory is off. A conversation lasts at most seven days, whatever the inactivity timeout. ## The 24-hour messaging window [Section titled “The 24-hour messaging window”](#the-24-hour-messaging-window) Meta allows a business to reply for 24 hours after the customer’s last message. Caplo follows that rule for the agent and for your team: * The agent replies only within 24 hours of the customer’s message. A message that reaches Caplo late, for example after a Meta outage, is recorded without a reply. * Your team’s replies from the console or from Chatwoot are refused once the window has closed. The customer must write again to reopen it. * Confirmation buttons work only while the window is open. Caplo does not send message tags or other messages outside the window on Messenger or Instagram. If you need to reach a customer later, ask them to write to you. ## What is supported [Section titled “What is supported”](#what-is-supported) | | Messenger and Instagram | | -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Customer messages | Text, taps on Caplo’s buttons and carousels, and your Page’s Get Started button, persistent menu, ice breakers and quick replies (the agent receives their visible text). | | Attachments from customers | Not supported. A message with only an image or file reaches the agent as a placeholder such as “\[image, file]”; text sent with an attachment is kept. | | Long messages | A customer message over 4,000 characters reaches the agent shortened and marked “\[message shortened]”. Long agent replies are split into several messages: over 2,000 characters on Messenger and 1,000 bytes on Instagram. | | Suggested replies | Up to three reply buttons of up to 20 characters, when the reply is 640 characters or shorter. See [Replies and feedback](/agents/replies-and-feedback/). | | Tool results | Cards become a carousel. Choices become reply buttons when three or fewer fit, otherwise a carousel. A detail with reply actions becomes a one-card carousel. Other results are sent as short numbered text. See [Present results](/tools/present-results/). | | Confirmations | A message with the details, “Confirm to approve these details, or Cancel.”, and **Confirm** and **Cancel** buttons. A confirmation that does not fit in 640 characters, or that needs editing, is not offered; the customer is told it cannot be confirmed here. See [Write tools](/tools/write-tools/). | | Handoff | No transfer destination: the agent cannot hand off on its own. Your team takes over from **Conversations**, or from Chatwoot when you mirror these channels there. See [Hand off to Chatwoot](/channels/handoff-to-chatwoot/). | | Sending rate | One message a second per Page or account. Replies queue behind each other in order. | ## Check the connection [Section titled “Check the connection”](#check-the-connection) Select **Check connection** on the account under **Integrations**. The **Deliveries** tab lists outgoing messages with their status and Meta’s reason for a failure. **Check delivery evidence** looks for Meta’s receipt; **Retry delivery** is offered only when the earlier attempt is proven not to have been accepted. Caplo never resends a message whose outcome is unknown. **Disconnect** stops new messages and replies for every channel that uses the account and releases the Page or account, so it can be connected again later or to another workspace. Existing conversations are kept. **Reconnect** restores it. ## Troubleshooting [Section titled “Troubleshooting”](#troubleshooting) | Problem | What to do | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | “Allow popups for this console, then continue with Meta again.” | Allow popups for `app.caplo.si`, then select **Continue with Meta**. | | “This authorization window is no longer connected to Caplo.” | The console tab that started setup was closed or reloaded. Close the popup and select **Continue with Meta** in the original tab. | | “Meta did not grant the account access required for messaging.” | A permission in the table above was not granted. Start again and accept all requested permissions. | | Connection shows **Reconnect required** or **Authorization expired** | Meta access was revoked or expired, or a permission was removed. Select **Reconnect**. | | Connection shows **Needs attention** | Caplo could not complete a check, often because Meta was briefly unavailable. It keeps sending and checks again. If it stays, select **Check connection**. | | Instagram messages never arrive | In the Instagram app, allow connected tools to access messages (in the account’s message settings), and check that the account is a professional account. | | Delivery failed with a reason ending in `2018278`, `2018065` or `2534022` | The 24-hour window had closed. Wait for the customer to write again. | | Delivery failed with a reason ending in `2018108` or `1545041` | The person no longer receives messages from the Page or is unavailable. | | The agent does not answer | Check that the channel is not paused, **Who answers** is **AI agent**, and the customer wrote within the last 24 hours. | ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Channels](/channels/) * [Connect WhatsApp](/channels/whatsapp/) * [Hand off to Chatwoot](/channels/handoff-to-chatwoot/) * [Conversations inbox](/operate/conversations-inbox/)

# Phone calls

> Answer calls to your own Twilio or Telnyx number with an agent, transfer callers to your team, and know what callers hear, what it costs and how to fix common problems.

A phone channel lets customers call an agent. You bring a phone number from your own Twilio or Telnyx account and point it at Caplo. When someone calls, your carrier sends the call to Caplo over SIP, and the agent release the channel serves answers it. Phone channels take **inbound calls only**. Caplo does not buy numbers, place outbound calls to customers, or change your carrier account beyond what you configure yourself. ## How a call reaches your agent [Section titled “How a call reaches your agent”](#how-a-call-reaches-your-agent) 1. A caller dials your Twilio or Telnyx number. 2. Your carrier sends the call over SIP to the address Caplo shows for your account (a Twilio Elastic SIP trunk or a Telnyx FQDN connection). 3. Caplo checks that the number is connected to an active phone channel in your workspace, then starts a conversation on the release that channel serves. 4. The agent speaks its greeting, listens, and answers by voice until the call ends or is transferred. Each call is one conversation. It appears under **Monitor** then **Conversations** with the channel’s name, so you can read the transcript. See [Conversations inbox](/operate/conversations-inbox/). ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * **Phone channels are enabled per workspace.** If the **Twilio** or **Telnyx** tab under **Integrations** then **Communication channels** shows an error, or connecting an account fails, ask [Caplo support](/resources/support/) to enable phone numbers for your workspace. * A Twilio or Telnyx account with at least one voice-capable phone number. Your carrier bills you for the number and the call minutes. * A published agent release. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). * A role that can manage integrations (Owner, Admin or Developer) to connect the carrier account, and one that can configure channels (Owner, Admin or Builder) to create the channel. ## Choose Twilio or Telnyx [Section titled “Choose Twilio or Telnyx”](#choose-twilio-or-telnyx) Both carriers work the same way once connected. Pick the one where your numbers already are. | | Twilio | Telnyx | | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | What you connect | Account SID, an API key and the account’s Auth Token | An API v2 key and the account’s public key | | Where calls are routed | An Elastic SIP trunk with Caplo’s origination URI | An FQDN SIP connection to Caplo’s SIP host, with credentials Caplo gives you | | Account limits | US1 region accounts only | None specific to Caplo | | Number checks | When you connect, rotate credentials or select **Refresh account and numbers** | Automatically about once an hour, and when you refresh | | Extra setup for warm transfers | A termination SIP URI and a credential list on the trunk | The connection must be allowed to place outbound calls | Setup guides: [Connect a Twilio number](/channels/phone/twilio/) and [Connect a Telnyx number](/channels/phone/telnyx/). ## Set up a phone channel [Section titled “Set up a phone channel”](#set-up-a-phone-channel) 1. **Connect your carrier account** in **Integrations** then **Communication channels**, and route your number to Caplo as the carrier guide describes. When **Connection details** lists the number as **Eligible for binding**, it is ready. 2. **Create the channel.** In **Channels** (under **Deploy**), select **Create channel** and choose **Telephone**. Enter a **Channel name**, choose the **Agent** and **Agent release**, then select **Set up connection**. 3. On **Phone connection**, choose the **Voice provider** (Twilio or Telnyx), the connected account, and the **Phone number**. Only verified numbers that no other channel uses can be chosen. Select **Continue**. 4. On **Conversation**, optionally set **Transfer calls to** and **Transfer type** (see [Transfer calls to a person](#transfer-calls-to-a-person)), then select **Create channel**. 5. Caplo creates the number’s route. Under **Number routes** in the SIP connection panel, the number shows **Provisioning** and then **Ready**, usually within a minute. 6. **Call the number.** You should hear the agent’s greeting. Check the call in **Monitor** then **Conversations**. Discovering a number does not prove that audio works both ways, so always make a real test call before you give the number to customers. The provider and the number are fixed once the channel exists. To have a different agent answer the same number, change the channel’s release or agent instead. ### Change the release a number serves [Section titled “Change the release a number serves”](#change-the-release-a-number-serves) * Console Open the channel in **Channels** and select **Update to** the newest release, or **Change release** to pick any release or another agent. New calls use the new release; calls in progress keep theirs. See [Channels](/channels/). * CLI Phone channels are named `twilio/<name>` or `telnyx/<name>`: 
  ```sh
  caplo channel list
  caplo channel deploy "twilio/Support line"
  caplo channel deploy "telnyx/Support line" --agent "Support Desk@1.4.0"
  caplo channel rollback "twilio/Support line"
  ```
 See [`caplo channel deploy`](/reference/cli/channel/#caplo-channel-deploy). To change transfer settings later, open the channel and select **Configure** on its phone entry point. ## Voice settings for phone calls [Section titled “Voice settings for phone calls”](#voice-settings-for-phone-calls) How the agent listens and speaks is set on the agent, under **Advanced settings** then **Voice**, so it is the same on the phone and in the website widget. [Voice and speech](/providers/voice-and-speech/) explains every setting. On the phone, these matter most: * **Voice language** and **Speech recognition**: callers are understood only in the agent’s voice language. Test with real phone audio, which is lower quality than a browser microphone. * **Greeting**: the caller hears the agent’s greeting as soon as the call connects. Keep it short. * **Fixed lines**: the transfer, transfer failed, time limit and silence lines are what callers hear at those moments. The **Continue in chat line** never plays on a phone call. * **Silence check after** and **End call after**: with the defaults, a silent caller hears “Are you still there?” after 15 seconds and the call ends 10 seconds later. * **Typing sound while working**: plays a soft typing sound while the agent looks something up, so the caller does not hear dead air. * **Start replies early** and a **Realtime model** make replies start sooner. Callers can interrupt the agent at any time. The agent stops speaking, and its next reply knows how much of the interrupted answer the caller actually heard. To let the agent hang up when the caller is done, allow the **End call** action on the agent. See [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). Voice changes reach callers after you publish a release and update the channel to it. ## Transfer calls to a person [Section titled “Transfer calls to a person”](#transfer-calls-to-a-person) A phone channel can send callers to your team when the agent hands off. It needs both: * The agent allows the **Transfer to a human** action in a published release, and its instructions say when to use it. See [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/). * The channel’s **Transfer calls to** field holds a phone number in international format (for example `+15550100142`) or a `sip:` or `sips:` URI, up to 256 characters. Without a **Transfer calls to** target, the call stays with the agent. Phone calls cannot be taken over from the console. Choose the **Transfer type**: | | Cold | Warm | | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Target | Phone number or SIP URI | Phone number only | | What the caller hears | The transfer line, then ringing at your team’s number | The transfer line, then hold music | | What your team hears | A normal incoming call | A briefing with the agent’s reason for the transfer and the caller’s number, then “Press 1 to take the call” | | When the caller is connected | As soon as your team answers | When the person presses 1 | | How it works | Your carrier transfers the call (SIP REFER) | Caplo calls the person through your carrier, then joins them to the caller | The destination rings for up to 30 seconds. For a warm transfer, the briefing plays at most twice, with 10 seconds after each to press 1, and the whole transfer must connect within 150 seconds. Edit the briefing with the **Warm transfer briefing** line in the agent’s voice settings; `{reason}` and `{caller}` are filled in for you. If nobody answers, the person declines, or the carrier refuses the transfer, the caller hears the **Transfer failed line** and the agent carries on. A call can attempt at most two warm transfers. Your carrier must allow transfers on the number. See the carrier guide for the settings: [Twilio](/channels/phone/twilio/#transfers) or [Telnyx](/channels/phone/telnyx/#transfers). ## Maximum call duration [Section titled “Maximum call duration”](#maximum-call-duration) Every call has a time limit, set for the whole workspace in **Settings** then **Data & privacy** under **Voice calls**. It is 60 minutes unless you change it, and you can set 5 to 240 minutes. Owners and Admins can change it. See [Data and privacy](/operate/data-and-privacy/). * One minute before the limit, the caller hears the **Time limit warning line**. * At the limit, the caller hears the **Time limit goodbye line** and the call ends. A transfer already in progress finishes first. * A change applies to calls that start after it. After a warm transfer connects, the conversation between the caller and your team is no longer timed by this setting, but no phone call can last longer than about four hours. ## What callers hear when something goes wrong [Section titled “What callers hear when something goes wrong”](#what-callers-hear-when-something-goes-wrong) | Situation | What the caller hears | | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | The number has no phone channel, or its route is not **Ready** | Caplo does not answer; the caller gets your carrier’s failed-call treatment. | | The channel is paused, the carrier integration is disabled, the release was revoked, or Caplo is at its call capacity | The call is disconnected without a message. | | A Twilio number has not been checked for 24 hours | The call is disconnected without a message. Select **Refresh account and numbers**. | | A reply cannot be produced right now | The **Unavailable line**, then the agent keeps listening. | | A reply fails | The **Error line**, then the agent keeps listening. | | A speech provider refuses your own key | The call ends. Check the key under **Integrations** then **Service providers**. | | The caller stays silent | The **Silence check line**, then the **Silence goodbye line**, and the call ends. | Fixed lines left empty use the platform’s wording, shown as placeholders in the agent’s voice settings. ## Costs [Section titled “Costs”](#costs) A phone call is billed in two places: * **Caplo** charges **Voice minutes** for connected call time, billed by the second, plus speech recognition, spoken characters and model tokens for providers set to **Caplo Managed**. See [How Caplo charges](/usage/) and [what affects the cost of a call](/providers/voice-and-speech/#what-affects-cost). * **Your carrier** bills your Twilio or Telnyx account directly for the number, the inbound call minutes, and any transferred calls. A warm transfer places an outbound call from your account, and the caller’s inbound call stays open while they talk to your team. Shorter silence timings, a lower maximum call duration and short spoken replies all reduce the cost of a call. ## Limits [Section titled “Limits”](#limits) | Limit | Value | | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Call direction | Inbound only. Caplo never calls customers. | | Numbers per channel | One. A number can serve only one channel, and the provider and number cannot be changed after the channel is created. | | Simultaneous calls | Currently one call at a time per workspace, counting phone and website voice calls together; platform capacity is also limited. A call that arrives when no capacity is free is disconnected. Contact [support](/resources/support/) if you need more. | | Call length | 5 to 240 minutes, set per workspace; 60 by default. | | Write tools | Do not run on phone calls. Read tools work as usual. | | Caller identity | Callers are anonymous. The caller’s number is not treated as a verified customer. | | Transfer target | One per channel. Warm transfers need a phone number; at most two warm transfer attempts per call. | | Deleting | Phone channels can be paused but not deleted. | Because write tools do not run on calls, tell the agent in its instructions how to help callers who want to change something, for example by transferring them or pointing them to your website. See [Write tools](/tools/write-tools/). ## Pause or stop answering a number [Section titled “Pause or stop answering a number”](#pause-or-stop-answering-a-number) Select **Pause channel** on the channel. Caplo then disconnects new calls to the number; calls in progress continue. **Resume channel** takes calls again. To send the number’s calls somewhere else entirely, route it away from Caplo in your carrier account. See [Remove a Twilio number](/channels/phone/twilio/#remove-a-number) or [Remove a Telnyx number](/channels/phone/telnyx/#remove-a-number). ## Troubleshooting [Section titled “Troubleshooting”](#troubleshooting) **The call does not connect, or the caller hears a carrier error.** * In **Integrations**, open the account’s **Connection details**. The number should be **Eligible for binding**, and under **Number routes** it should be **Ready**. A blocker next to the number says what to fix. * Check that the account is enabled and the channel is not paused. * Check the SIP settings in your carrier account against **Connection details**. Copy the values again rather than typing them. * For Twilio, select **Refresh account and numbers**: a Twilio number not checked for 24 hours stops taking calls. **The call connects and then hangs up straight away.** The channel may be paused, the release revoked, or another call may be using your workspace’s call capacity. Try again once other calls have ended. If your speech providers use your own keys, check that the keys still work. **The caller hears nothing, or the agent does not hear the caller.** This is usually a media setting at the carrier. For Telnyx, enable SRTP and the PCMU (G.711 μ-law) codec on the connection. For Twilio, check the trunk has not been changed since you connected it, then select **Refresh account and numbers**. If the greeting plays but the agent never answers, check that the caller speaks the agent’s **Voice language**. **Transfers fail.** The caller hears the transfer failed line when the target does not answer within 30 seconds, the person does not press 1, or the carrier refuses the transfer. Check: * The agent’s published release allows **Transfer to a human**, and the channel has **Transfer calls to** set. * The target number is in international format and can take calls from your carrier. * Your carrier allows transfers on this number. For Twilio, the channel’s **Configure** dialog warns you when the trunk blocks the transfer you chose. **The number is not in the list when creating the channel.** It must be voice-capable, routed to Caplo (an Elastic SIP trunk for Twilio, an FQDN SIP connection for Telnyx), and not already used by another channel. Fix the routing, then select **Refresh account and numbers**. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) [Connect a Twilio number](/channels/phone/twilio/) [Connect a Telnyx number](/channels/phone/telnyx/) [Voice and speech](/providers/voice-and-speech/) [Hand off to a person or end a call](/agents/handoff-and-end-actions/)

# Connect a Telnyx number

> Connect your Telnyx account, route a number to Caplo with an FQDN SIP connection, set up transfers, test a call, and remove the number again.

Caplo answers calls to your Telnyx numbers through a Telnyx FQDN SIP connection. You create the connection in the Telnyx portal and point it at Caplo; Caplo never buys numbers or changes your Telnyx configuration. For how phone channels work as a whole, see [Phone calls](/channels/phone/). ## What you need [Section titled “What you need”](#what-you-need) | Value | Where to find it in Telnyx | What Caplo does with it | | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | | **Telnyx API v2 key** | The portal’s account settings, under **API Keys**. Create a key for Caplo. | Reads the account’s phone numbers and SIP connections. | | **Ed25519 public key** | The portal’s account settings, next to the API keys, as the account’s public key. A Base64 value of 44 characters ending in `=`. | Required by the connect form and stored with the account. | Copy the **public** key. Never paste a private signing key. Caplo only reads through the API key. It stores the key encrypted and never shows it again. You also need a role that can manage integrations (Owner, Admin or Developer), and phone numbers enabled for your workspace. See [Before you start](/channels/phone/#before-you-start). ## Connect the account [Section titled “Connect the account”](#connect-the-account) 1. In the console, open **Integrations** then **Communication channels**, select the **Telnyx** tab, then **Add integration**. 2. Enter an **Integration name**, the **Telnyx API v2 key** and the **Ed25519 public key**, then select **Verify and connect**. Caplo checks the key with Telnyx and lists the account’s voice numbers. 3. Select **Connection details** on the new account. The **SIP connection** panel shows what your Telnyx connection needs: | Field | Use | | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **SIP host** | The address Telnyx sends calls to. | | **Port and transport** | The port and protocol for that address, for example TLS. | | **SIP URI** | The same address as a single SIP URI, for settings that ask for one. | | **Username** and **Password** | Credentials Caplo created for this Telnyx account. Select **Reveal** to see the password. | **Verification key** shows a fingerprint of the public key you entered, so you can tell which key is stored. Connecting changes nothing in Telnyx. Your numbers keep their current routing until you assign them to the connection. ## Route a number to Caplo [Section titled “Route a number to Caplo”](#route-a-number-to-caplo) 1. In the Telnyx portal, create a SIP connection of type **FQDN** that points to Caplo’s **SIP host**, on the port and transport shown in **Connection details**. Copy the values rather than typing them. 2. Use credential authentication with the **Username** and **Password** from **Connection details**. 3. Enable SRTP media encryption and the PCMU (G.711 μ-law) codec on the connection. 4. Assign the phone number to this connection. 5. Back in Caplo, select **Refresh account and numbers** in **Connection details**. The number should now show **Eligible for binding**. 6. Create a phone channel for the number. See [Set up a phone channel](/channels/phone/#set-up-a-phone-channel). Saving the channel creates the number’s route in Caplo. It shows **Provisioning** and then **Ready** under **Number routes**. Caution Assigning a number to the connection replaces how Telnyx handled its calls before, such as a Call Control application. Numbers on a Call Control application or any connection type other than FQDN cannot be connected to Caplo. You can use one FQDN connection for every number you send to Caplo from the same Telnyx account. The username and password belong to the Telnyx account you connected in Caplo, not to a single number. ## Transfers [Section titled “Transfers”](#transfers) **Cold transfers** ask Telnyx to transfer the call (SIP REFER) to the target number or SIP URI. **Warm transfers** make a new call from your Telnyx account to the person, authenticated with the same username and password. Make sure the connection is allowed to place outbound calls to the target, which in Telnyx means an outbound voice profile assigned to the connection that permits the destination. The transferred call is billed to your Telnyx account. ## Test a call [Section titled “Test a call”](#test-a-call) 1. Check that the number shows **Ready** under **Number routes** in **Connection details**. 2. Call the number from a phone. You should hear the agent’s greeting. 3. Talk to the agent, try interrupting it, and hang up. 4. Open **Monitor** then **Conversations** and find the call on the channel to read the transcript. Number discovery does not prove that audio works both ways, so make a real call after every change to the connection. ## Keep the account current [Section titled “Keep the account current”](#keep-the-account-current) Caplo checks a Telnyx account’s numbers and connections automatically about once an hour, and whenever you select **Refresh account and numbers**. After you change something in Telnyx, refresh to apply it straight away. If Caplo cannot check the account for 24 hours, for example because the API key was deleted in Telnyx, its numbers stop taking calls until a check succeeds. Rotate the credentials to fix it. To replace the API key or public key, select **Rotate credentials** on the account, enter the new values and select **Verify and rotate**. The SIP username and password stay the same, so you do not need to change the connection in Telnyx. ## What not to change in Telnyx [Section titled “What not to change in Telnyx”](#what-not-to-change-in-telnyx) While a number serves a phone channel, leave these as they are: * The connection’s host, port and transport. Without them, calls never reach Caplo. * The connection’s username and password. * SRTP and the PCMU codec on the connection. Without them, callers may hear nothing. * The number’s assignment to the connection, and the connection’s enabled state. A disabled connection stops calls. If you change any of them, select **Refresh account and numbers** in Caplo and make a test call. ## Remove a number [Section titled “Remove a number”](#remove-a-number) 1. In Caplo, open the number’s channel and select **Pause channel**. New calls are disconnected; calls in progress finish. 2. In Telnyx, assign the number to another connection or application. 3. In Caplo, select **Refresh account and numbers**, or wait for the next automatic check. The number shows **Needs setup** and its route **Disconnected**. Phone channels cannot be deleted, and a number stays tied to the channel it was first connected to. To have another agent answer the number, change the channel’s agent instead. To stop all numbers of a Telnyx account at once, select **Disable** on the account in **Integrations**. Its numbers stop taking calls until you select **Enable**. ## Troubleshooting [Section titled “Troubleshooting”](#troubleshooting) Blockers next to a number in **Connection details**: | Message | Fix | | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | The number is no longer available in this account. | The number was released or moved. Check the account in Telnyx, then refresh. | | Enable this account connection. | The account is disabled in Caplo. Select **Enable**. | | Refresh the account and numbers. | The last check is more than 24 hours old. Select **Refresh account and numbers**. | | Enable the number’s SIP connection in Telnyx. | The connection is disabled in Telnyx. Enable it, then refresh. | | Assign the number to an FQDN SIP connection configured with the SIP connection settings. | The number is on another connection type or none. Assign it to the FQDN connection, then refresh. | **No voice-capable numbers found.** Assign an owned number to an FQDN SIP connection in Telnyx, then refresh. **Verify and connect fails.** Check that the API key is active and that the public key is the 44-character Base64 value from your Telnyx account, not a private key. **The call connects but there is no audio.** Check that SRTP and the PCMU (G.711 μ-law) codec are enabled on the connection. **Calls are not answered.** Check the connection’s host, port, transport and credentials against **Connection details**, that the route is **Ready**, and that the channel is not paused. See [Troubleshooting](/channels/phone/#troubleshooting) for more.

# Connect a Twilio number

> Connect your Twilio account, route a number to Caplo with an Elastic SIP trunk, set up transfers, test a call, and remove the number again.

Caplo answers calls to your Twilio numbers through Twilio Elastic SIP Trunking. You create the trunk in your Twilio account and point it at Caplo; Caplo never creates trunks, buys numbers or changes your Twilio configuration. For how phone channels work as a whole, see [Phone calls](/channels/phone/). ## What you need [Section titled “What you need”](#what-you-need) | Value | Where to find it in Twilio | What Caplo does with it | | -------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Twilio Account SID** | The account dashboard. Starts with `AC`. | Selects the account or subaccount whose numbers you connect. | | **API Key SID** and **API Key Secret** | **API keys & tokens**. Create a key in the same account as the Account SID. The SID starts with `SK`. | Reads the account’s phone numbers, the trunks they are assigned to, and call records after calls end. | | **Account Auth Token** | **API keys & tokens**, under the account’s auth tokens. | Required by the connect form. Caplo stores it encrypted. | The account must be in Twilio’s **US1** region. The API Key Secret and the Account Auth Token are different values; the form needs both. Caplo only reads through the API key: incoming phone numbers, calls, and Elastic SIP trunks. A Standard key works. If you use a Restricted key, give it read access to those three resources. Caplo stores the secrets encrypted and never shows them again. You also need a role that can manage integrations (Owner, Admin or Developer), and phone numbers enabled for your workspace. See [Before you start](/channels/phone/#before-you-start). ## Connect the account [Section titled “Connect the account”](#connect-the-account) 1. In the console, open **Integrations** then **Communication channels**, select the **Twilio** tab, then **Add integration**. 2. Enter an **Integration name**, the **Twilio Account SID**, **API Key SID**, **API Key Secret** and **Account Auth Token**, then select **Verify and connect**. Caplo checks the credentials with Twilio and lists the account’s voice numbers. 3. Select **Connection details** on the new account. The **SIP connection** panel shows the values you need in Twilio: | Field | Use | | ----------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Origination URI** | Where the trunk sends calls. Always copy it from here. | | **Termination username** and **Termination password** | Only for warm transfers. Select **Reveal** to see the password. | Connecting changes nothing in Twilio. Your numbers keep their current routing until you assign them to a trunk. ## Route a number to Caplo [Section titled “Route a number to Caplo”](#route-a-number-to-caplo) 1. In the Twilio console, go to **Elastic SIP Trunking** and create a trunk. Use one trunk for all the numbers you send to Caplo. 2. On the trunk’s **Origination** settings, add the **Origination URI** from Caplo’s **Connection details**. No credential list is needed for origination. 3. On the trunk’s **Numbers** settings, add the phone number. Caution Assigning a number to a trunk replaces how Twilio handled its calls before, such as a webhook URL or TwiML app. Calls to that number reach Caplo only from then on. 4. Back in Caplo, select **Refresh account and numbers** in **Connection details**. The number should now show **Eligible for binding**. 5. Create a phone channel for the number. See [Set up a phone channel](/channels/phone/#set-up-a-phone-channel). Saving the channel creates the number’s route in Caplo. It shows **Provisioning** and then **Ready** under **Number routes**. Secure Trunking (encrypted calls) can be on or off. Caplo accepts calls either way. ## Transfers [Section titled “Transfers”](#transfers) If the channel transfers callers to your team, the trunk must allow it. Change these on the trunk in Twilio, then select **Refresh account and numbers** in Caplo, because Caplo reads the trunk’s settings only when the account is checked. **Cold transfers** use SIP REFER: * In the trunk’s general settings, turn on **Call Transfer (SIP REFER)**. * To transfer to a phone number, also allow transfers to the PSTN. With SIP-only transfers, use a `sip:` URI as the target. **Warm transfers** make a new call from your Twilio account to the person: * Set a **Termination SIP URI** on the trunk. * Create a credential list with the **Termination username** and **Termination password** from Caplo’s **Connection details**, and add it to the trunk’s termination authentication. If you change Secure Trunking later, refresh the account so warm transfers use the matching encryption. When the trunk blocks the transfer type you chose, the channel’s **Configure** dialog shows a warning such as “Call transfer is turned off on this number’s Twilio trunk”. The transferred call is billed to your Twilio account. ## Test a call [Section titled “Test a call”](#test-a-call) 1. Check that the number shows **Ready** under **Number routes** in **Connection details**. 2. Call the number from a phone. You should hear the agent’s greeting. 3. Talk to the agent, try interrupting it, and hang up. 4. Open **Monitor** then **Conversations** and find the call on the channel to read the transcript. Number discovery does not prove that audio works both ways, so make a real call after every change to the trunk. ## Keep the account current [Section titled “Keep the account current”](#keep-the-account-current) Caplo checks a Twilio account’s numbers and trunks when you connect it, rotate its credentials, or select **Refresh account and numbers**. It does not check them on its own. Refresh at least every 24 hours A Twilio number whose last check is more than 24 hours old stops taking calls: callers are disconnected without a message. Select **Refresh account and numbers** to bring it back. Select **Refresh account and numbers** whenever you change something in Twilio, such as trunk settings or the numbers on a trunk. To replace the API key or Auth Token, select **Rotate credentials** on the account, enter the new values and select **Verify and rotate**. The name and Account SID stay the same. ## What not to change in Twilio [Section titled “What not to change in Twilio”](#what-not-to-change-in-twilio) While a number serves a phone channel, leave these as they are: * The Origination URI on the trunk. Without it, calls never reach Caplo. * The number’s assignment to the trunk. Moving it off the trunk stops its calls reaching Caplo. * The termination SIP URI and credential list, if you use warm transfers. * **Call Transfer (SIP REFER)** and PSTN transfer settings, if you use cold transfers. If you change any of them, select **Refresh account and numbers** in Caplo and make a test call. ## Remove a number [Section titled “Remove a number”](#remove-a-number) 1. In Caplo, open the number’s channel and select **Pause channel**. New calls are disconnected; calls in progress finish. 2. In Twilio, remove the number from the Elastic SIP trunk and set up its voice handling as you want it. 3. In Caplo, select **Refresh account and numbers**. The number shows **Needs setup** and its route **Disconnected**. Phone channels cannot be deleted, and a number stays tied to the channel it was first connected to. To have another agent answer the number, change the channel’s agent instead. To stop all numbers of a Twilio account at once, select **Disable** on the account in **Integrations**. Its numbers stop taking calls until you select **Enable**. ## Troubleshooting [Section titled “Troubleshooting”](#troubleshooting) Blockers next to a number in **Connection details**: | Message | Fix | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | The number is no longer available in this account. | The number was released or moved. Check the account in Twilio, then refresh. | | Enable this account connection. | The account is disabled in Caplo. Select **Enable**. | | Refresh the account and numbers. | The last check is more than 24 hours old. Select **Refresh account and numbers**. | | Assign the number to an Elastic SIP trunk configured with the origination URI. | Add the number to the trunk, then refresh. | **No voice-capable numbers found.** Assign an owned voice number to an Elastic SIP trunk in Twilio, then refresh. **Verify and connect fails.** Check that the API key belongs to the account in **Twilio Account SID**, that the key is active, and that the account is in the US1 region. **Calls are not answered.** Check the Origination URI matches **Connection details** exactly, the route is **Ready**, the channel is not paused, and the account was refreshed in the last 24 hours. See [Troubleshooting](/channels/phone/#troubleshooting) for more.

# Design the website widget

> Create a reusable widget design, set its brand, welcome screen, launcher and language, then publish a revision and apply it to your website channels.

A widget design controls how the website widget looks: colours, logo, fonts, welcome screen, launcher and layout. Designs are reusable. One design can serve several website channels, and each channel shows the design revision you apply to it. A design has a draft you edit and a history of published revisions. Websites never show a draft. They change only when you apply a published revision to a channel, so you can work on a design while the live widget stays the same. Who answers, which modes are offered and how visitors verify are not part of the design. Those are [entry point settings](/channels/website-widget/settings/). ## Create a design [Section titled “Create a design”](#create-a-design) 1. In the console, open **Widget designs** under **Deploy** and select **Create design**. 2. Enter a **Design name**. It is internal and only shown in the console; visitors see the widget title instead. 3. Choose a template to start from: **Standard**, **Concierge**, **Evergreen**, **Orbit**, **Help center** (an inline layout) or **Midnight** (dark). A template only seeds the first draft. 4. Select **Create design**. The design editor opens on the draft. You can also create a design while creating a website channel, with **Create a design here**. ## Design sections [Section titled “Design sections”](#design-sections) The editor groups settings into sections. The preview beside them updates as you edit. Select **Save draft** to keep your changes; **Discard** returns to the last saved draft. ### Brand and colours [Section titled “Brand and colours”](#brand-and-colours) * **Header**: the **Widget title** and **Subtitle** visitors see at the top of the widget. * **Colours**: pick from the **Brand palette** or set **Brand colour** and **Soft surface** yourself. * **Style**: **Colour mode** (Light or Dark) and **Corner radius (px)**. ### Logo and fonts [Section titled “Logo and fonts”](#logo-and-fonts) * **Logo**: PNG or WebP, at most 256 KiB and 16 to 1024 px per side. Shown in the header and welcome area. * **Assistant avatar**: PNG or WebP, at most 256 KiB. Shown beside assistant replies. * **Body font** and **Heading font**: **DM Sans**, **Manrope**, **Device system font** or **Uploaded font**. An uploaded font is a WOFF2 file of at most 512 KiB. Visitors see the device font if it cannot load. Each upload is kept as its own version, and published revisions keep the version they used. Open **Reuse an uploaded asset** to pick a logo, avatar or font you uploaded before. Withdrawing an asset version keeps it on revisions that already use it, but stops new drafts, publications and applies from using it. ### Welcome screen [Section titled “Welcome screen”](#welcome-screen) What visitors see before their first message: * **Welcome heading** and **Welcome description**. * **Suggested questions**: up to 4 shortcuts. Each has a **Suggestion label**, a **Suggestion description** and the **Message to send** when a visitor taps it. * **Message placeholder**: the hint text in the message box. The agent’s own greeting is set on the agent, not in the design. ### Launcher and layout [Section titled “Launcher and layout”](#launcher-and-layout) * **Display mode**: **Floating popup** opens from a launcher button in a corner of the page. **Inline container** renders inside an element on your page, with no launcher button. * **Width (px)** and **Height (px)** of the widget. * For a popup: **Corner** (Bottom right or Bottom left) and **Edge spacing (px)**. * **Launcher button**: **Launcher label** and **Launcher icon** (Message or Sparkles). * **Open automatically on desktop**, with an **Opening delay (seconds)** from 0 to 60. Caution Popup and inline need different embed code. If you change **Display mode** and apply the revision to a live channel, update the markup on your site at the same time. See [Install the website widget](/channels/website-widget/install/). ### Language [Section titled “Language”](#language) * **Locale**: for example `en-US`. This sets the language of the widget’s own labels and buttons. Translated labels need a supported language pack. * **Text direction**: **Left to right** or **Right to left**. The language the agent answers in is set on the agent, not here. ## Publish a revision [Section titled “Publish a revision”](#publish-a-revision) 1. Save your draft. Only the saved draft is published. 2. Open **Publish and install** and select **Publish revision** followed by the next revision number. 3. Review the comparison with the previous published revision, then select **Publish revision**. A published revision is immutable. Publishing does not change any website. ## Apply a revision to a website [Section titled “Apply a revision to a website”](#apply-a-revision-to-a-website) You can apply from the design or from the channel. Either way, the change takes effect right away; you do not need to save anything else. From the design: 1. Open **Publish and install**. **Live on websites** lists the channels that use this design and the revision each shows. 2. Choose the **Revision to apply** and select **Review and apply**. 3. Review the before and after, then confirm. From the channel: open the website entry point (**Configure & install**), and on the **General** tab under **Appearance**, choose the **Widget design** and **Revision**, then select **Apply revision**. Widgets that are already open keep their look until the visitor reloads the page. The widget ID and the embed code stay the same, except when the display mode changes between popup and inline. To roll a website back, apply an earlier revision. Select **Load older revisions** if the one you want is not listed. ## Archive a design [Section titled “Archive a design”](#archive-a-design) In **Design settings**, **Archive design** hides a design from the library and stops it being published or applied. It is available once no website uses the design. **Restore design** makes it available again. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Install the website widget](/channels/website-widget/install/) * [Website widget settings](/channels/website-widget/settings/) * [Present tool results](/tools/present-results/) for how rich results such as cards and tables appear in the widget

# Install the website widget

> Copy the widget embed code from the console and add it to a plain HTML site, a Next.js app or a React single page app.

The website widget is one script tag. It loads the widget from Caplo, shows the published design you applied to the channel, and connects visitors to the agent release the channel serves. ## Before you start [Section titled “Before you start”](#before-you-start) * Create a website channel (**Live chat widget**) for a published agent. See [Channels](/channels/). * Publish a widget design and apply a revision to the channel. The console only gives you embed code once a design is applied, because the code depends on whether the design is a popup or an inline container. See [Design the widget](/channels/website-widget/design/). * Know the exact origin of every site that will show the widget, for example `https://www.example.com`. ## Get the embed code [Section titled “Get the embed code”](#get-the-embed-code) 1. In **Channels**, open the website channel and select **Configure & install** on its website entry point. 2. On the **General** tab, check **Allowed website origin**. Add every origin that will show the widget, then select **Save entry point**. 3. Open the **Install** tab and select **Copy embed code**. If **Copy embed code** is disabled, the hint next to it says why: no design is applied, there are unsaved edits, the entry point is disabled, or the channel is paused. The **Widget ID** on the Install tab is a public identifier. The snippet contains no credentials, and you never need to add any. ## The embed code [Section titled “The embed code”](#the-embed-code) A popup design (a launcher button in a corner of the page): 
```html
<script
  src="https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js"
  data-widget-id="<WIDGET_ID>"
  async
></script>
```
 An inline design (the conversation sits inside an element on your page) adds a container and points the script at it: 
```html
<div id="caplo-<WIDGET_ID>"></div>
<script
  src="https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js"
  data-widget-id="<WIDGET_ID>"
  data-container="#caplo-<WIDGET_ID>"
  async
></script>
```
 Always copy the code from the console rather than typing it: the console escapes the container selector when the widget ID contains a dot. | Attribute | Required | What it does | | ------------------ | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `src` | Yes | The widget loader. Load it from Caplo; a self-hosted copy cannot reach the widget service. | | `data-widget-id` | Yes | The widget ID from the Install tab. | | `data-container` | Inline designs | A CSS selector for the element the widget renders into. The element must exist on the page when the loader runs. | | `data-persistence` | No | By default the widget restores the visitor’s conversation after a reload in the same browser tab. Set `data-persistence="none"` to start fresh on every page load. | | `nonce` | When your CSP uses nonces | Add your page’s nonce. The widget applies it to the styles it adds. | | `async` | Recommended | Loads the widget without blocking your page. | The widget ID stays the same when you apply a new design revision, so you do not need to change your site when the appearance changes. Switching a design between popup and inline is the exception: inline needs the container element and `data-container`. ## Allowed origins [Section titled “Allowed origins”](#allowed-origins) The widget only works on the origins listed on the entry point. * List exact origins: scheme and host, plus the port if it is not the default. No paths and no wildcards. * `https://example.com` and `https://www.example.com` are different origins. List both if you use both. * Use HTTPS. HTTP is accepted only for `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `.localhost` hosts, which is useful for local development. * You can list up to 10 origins per entry point. On any other site, including a staging host you have not listed, the widget shows a **Chat unavailable · Try again** button instead of the chat. Removing an origin closes open conversations on that site. ## Add it to your site [Section titled “Add it to your site”](#add-it-to-your-site) * HTML Paste the code just before the closing `</body>` tag on every page that should show the widget. For an inline design, place the `<div>` where the conversation should appear and keep the script after it. 
  ```html
      <!-- your page content -->
      <script
        src="https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js"
        data-widget-id="<WIDGET_ID>"
        async
      ></script>
    </body>
  </html>
  ```
 * Next.js (App Router) For a popup design, load the script once in the root layout with `next/script`. It stays on the page across client-side navigation. app/layout.tsx 
  ```tsx
  import Script from 'next/script';


  export default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
    return (
      <html lang="en">
        <body>
          {children}
          <Script
            src="https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js"
            data-widget-id="<WIDGET_ID>"
            strategy="afterInteractive"
          />
        </body>
      </html>
    );
  }
  ```
 For an inline design shown on one route, use the client component from the React tab instead of `next/script`. `next/script` loads a given script only once per page session, so the widget would not come back after a visitor navigates away and returns. If your site sets a Content Security Policy with a nonce, pass it to `<Script nonce={nonce} />`. * React SPA For a popup design, add the embed code to your `index.html`, before `</body>`. The widget stays mounted while your router changes pages. For an inline design, render the container and add the loader from an effect: 
  ```tsx
  import { useEffect } from 'react';


  const LOADER = 'https://api.caplo.si/v1/messaging/widget/loader.js';


  export function CaploInlineWidget({ widgetId }: { widgetId: string }) {
    const containerId = `caplo-${widgetId}`;
    useEffect(() => {
      const script = document.createElement('script');
      script.src = LOADER;
      script.async = true;
      script.dataset.widgetId = widgetId;
      script.dataset.container = `#${CSS.escape(containerId)}`;
      document.body.append(script);
      return () => script.remove();
    }, [widgetId, containerId]);
    return <div id={containerId} />;
  }
  ```
 When the container leaves the page, the widget unmounts itself. Mounting the component again adds a new script, and the widget renders into the new container. Loading the same widget ID twice on one page is harmless: the second script does nothing. ## Content Security Policy [Section titled “Content Security Policy”](#content-security-policy) If your site sends a `Content-Security-Policy` header, add these sources to the matching directives and keep your existing sources: 
```text
script-src https://api.caplo.si;
connect-src https://api.caplo.si;
font-src https://api.caplo.si;
img-src blob:;
worker-src blob:;
```
 The console shows the same list under **Content Security Policy additions** on the Install tab. Brand logos and avatars render from `blob:` URLs, and map results start a worker from a `blob:` URL. If the channel allows voice, the browser also connects to Caplo’s media server for the call. Add it to `connect-src`: 
```text
connect-src https://api.caplo.si wss://livekit.caplo.si;
```
 Caplo never changes your site’s policy. See [Troubleshoot the website widget](/channels/website-widget/troubleshooting/) for how CSP errors show up. ## Single page apps [Section titled “Single page apps”](#single-page-apps) * The loader runs once per script element. Calling it again with the same widget ID on the same page does nothing until the widget is unmounted. * A popup widget stays mounted across client-side route changes. To remove it on some routes, call `unmount()` from the [JavaScript API](/channels/website-widget/javascript-api/); add the script again to bring it back. * An inline widget unmounts itself when its container is removed from the page. * On a full page unload the widget closes its connection. With the default persistence, the conversation resumes when the visitor returns in the same tab. ## Check the installation [Section titled “Check the installation”](#check-the-installation) 1. Open a page on an allowed origin. The launcher appears in the corner you chose, or the conversation appears in its container. 2. Send a message and check that the agent replies. The conversation appears in **Conversations** in the console. 3. If you see **Chat unavailable · Try again**, or nothing, see [Troubleshoot the website widget](/channels/website-widget/troubleshooting/). AI prompt Add the Caplo widget to my website Adds the website widget embed code to a plain HTML site, updates the Content Security Policy if there is one, and checks that it loads.
