ChatGPT](https://chatgpt.com/?q=Goal%3A%20give%20the%20Caplo%20tool%20package%20in%20%60%3CPACKAGE_DIR%3E%60%20a%20useful%20set%20of%20fixtures%2C%20one%20JSON%20file%20per%20call%2C%20so%20%60caplo%20tool%20test%60%20catches%20regressions%20before%20a%20version%20is%20published.%0A%0ARead%20these%20pages%20first%3A%0A%0A-%20https%3A%2F%2Fdocs.caplo.si%2Ftools%2Ftesting.md%0A-%20https%3A%2F%2Fdocs.caplo.si%2Freference%2Ffixtures.md%0A-%20https%3A%2F%2Fdocs.caplo.si%2Ftools%2Fdefine-tool-and-context.md%0A%0ASteps%3A%0A%0A1.%20Read%20%60caplo.yaml%60%20\(its%20%60tools%60%20list%20or%2C%20for%20a%20function%20package%2C%20its%20top-level%20policy%2C%20and%20its%20%60tests%60%20glob%2C%20default%20%60tests%2F*.json%60\)%20and%20the%20source%20of%20each%20tool.%20Note%20each%20tool's%20%60effect%60%2C%20its%20input%20schema%20and%20what%20it%20returns%20in%20%60structuredContent%60%20and%20text.%0A2.%20For%20each%20tool%2C%20plan%20fixtures%20for%3A%0A%20%20%20-%20a%20normal%20call%20with%20realistic%20arguments%3B%0A%20%20%20-%20an%20edge%20case%2C%20such%20as%20an%20empty%20result%20or%20the%20largest%20allowed%20input%3B%0A%20%20%20-%20an%20expected%20error%2C%20such%20as%20an%20unknown%20ID%2C%20with%20%60%22expect%22%3A%20%7B%20%22isError%22%3A%20true%20%7D%60.%0A%20%20%20Show%20me%20the%20plan%20before%20writing%20files.%0A3.%20Write%20each%20fixture%20as%20its%20own%20file%20in%20the%20tests%20folder%2C%20named%20after%20the%20tool%20and%20case%20\(for%20example%20%60tests%2Fget_order-shipped.json%60\)%3A%0A%0A%20%20%20%60%60%60json%0A%20%20%20%7B%0A%20%20%20%20%20%22name%22%3A%20%22%3CWHAT_THIS_CASE_CHECKS%3E%22%2C%0A%20%20%20%20%20%22tool%22%3A%20%22%3CTOOL_NAME%3E%22%2C%0A%20%20%20%20%20%22arguments%22%3A%20%7B%20%7D%2C%0A%20%20%20%20%20%22expect%22%3A%20%7B%20%22structuredContent%22%3A%20%7B%20%7D%2C%20%22textIncludes%22%3A%20%22%3CTEXT%3E%22%20%7D%0A%20%20%20%7D%0A%20%20%20%60%60%60%0A%0A%20%20%20%60tool%60%20is%20the%20tool%20name%20the%20package%20lists%2C%20not%20the%20%60%3Cpackage%3E__%3Ctool%3E%60%20model%20name.%20In%20%60structuredContent%60%2C%20list%20only%20the%20properties%20that%20matter%3B%20listed%20values%20must%20match%20exactly%2C%20unlisted%20ones%20are%20ignored.%20Every%20fixture%20needs%20at%20least%20one%20expectation.%0A4.%20If%20a%20tool%20reads%20secrets%2C%20the%20fixture%20needs%20%60secrets%60%20with%20test%20values.%20Use%20only%20values%20I%20give%20you%20for%20a%20test%20or%20sandbox%20environment%3B%20if%20I%20have%20none%2C%20ask%20me%20how%20to%20proceed.%0A5.%20If%20the%20package%20has%20write%20tools%2C%20warn%20me%20that%20%60caplo%20tool%20test%60%20runs%20write%20fixtures%20for%20real%20on%20my%20machine.%20Write%20them%20only%20against%20a%20test%20environment%20I%20confirm%3B%20%60caplo%20tool%20test%20--remote%60%20skips%20them.%0A6.%20Run%20the%20fixtures%20and%20fix%20the%20fixtures%20\(not%20the%20tool\)%20until%20they%20reflect%20the%20tool's%20real%2C%20correct%20behaviour.%20If%20a%20fixture%20reveals%20a%20bug%20in%20the%20tool%2C%20report%20it%20instead%20of%20weakening%20the%20expectation.%0A%0A%20%20%20%60%60%60sh%0A%20%20%20cd%20%3CPACKAGE_DIR%3E%0A%20%20%20caplo%20tool%20test%20-v%0A%20%20%20%60%60%60%0A%0A7.%20Ask%20me%20before%20running%20%60caplo%20tool%20test%20--remote%60%2C%20which%20pushes%20the%20package%20as%20the%20workspace%20draft%2C%20builds%20it%20and%20runs%20the%20read%20fixtures%20on%20Caplo%20with%20the%20egress%20list%20enforced.%0A%0AVerify%3A%20%60caplo%20tool%20test%60%20prints%20%60ok%60%20for%20every%20fixture%20and%20exits%200%3B%20with%20%60--remote%60%2C%20there%20are%20no%20failures%20and%20no%20%60egress%20denied%60%20lines.%0A%0ARules%3A%0A%0A-%20Never%20put%20production%20credentials%2C%20real%20customer%20data%20or%20secret%20values%20in%20fixtures%20or%20in%20this%20chat.%0A-%20Do%20not%20run%20%60caplo%20tool%20push%60%2C%20%60caplo%20tool%20publish%60%20or%20%60caplo%20publish%60%20without%20my%20approval.%0A-%20Use%20Node%2024%3B%20check%20with%20%60node%20--version%60%20and%20run%20%60npm%20install%60%20first%20if%20%60node_modules%60%20is%20missing.)

# Manage versions and read logs

> Follow builds, publish and revoke versions, archive or delete packages, pull a version's exact source, and read recent invocation logs.

A tool package moves through three stages: 1. **Draft.** The files you push or edit in Tool Studio. There is one draft per package, and each change raises its revision (`r1`, `r2`…). 2. **Build.** A snapshot of the draft, installed, bundled, checked and introspected. A build is `queued`, `running`, `succeeded`, `failed` or `cancelled`. 3. **Version.** A succeeded build you published, numbered `v1`, `v2`… A version never changes. Agents pin versions. ## Builds [Section titled “Builds”](#builds) `caplo tool push` starts a build and streams its log; so does **Build** in Tool Studio. To look at builds afterwards: 
```sh
caplo tool build list orders --status failed
caplo tool build get orders <BUILD_ID>
caplo tool build logs orders <BUILD_ID>
caplo tool build cancel orders <BUILD_ID>
```
 A build installs dependencies from your lockfile with install scripts disabled, checks them against known malicious packages, bundles the code, rejects unsafe code, and starts the bundle with no network access to read its tool list. Common failures: | Code | Cause | | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `lockfile_required`, `lockfile_mismatch` | No `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock` or `pnpm-lock.yaml`, or it does not match `package.json`. Run `npm install` and push again. | | `install_failed`, `install_timeout` | Installing failed, or took over 90 seconds. | | `malicious_package` | A dependency is a known malicious package. | | `native_addon` | A dependency needs a native `.node` addon. | | `forbidden_import` | The code imports `child_process`, `cluster`, `worker_threads` or `inspector`. | | `dynamic_require` | The code requires a path the bundler cannot resolve, or reads package files at run time. | | `bundle_too_large` | The bundle is over 10 MiB. | | `config_invalid` | `caplo.yaml` fails its schema, or its `name` or `type` differs from the package. | | `entry_invalid`, `compile_failed` | The entry module is missing, exports the wrong thing, or does not compile. | | `introspection_failed` | Loading the bundle or listing its tools failed. Code that calls the network while loading fails here. | | `tool_policy_missing`, `tool_policy_unknown_tool` | A listed tool has no policy in `caplo.yaml`, or a policy names a tool the code does not list. | | `tool_name_invalid`, `schema_invalid` | A tool name or model name breaks the naming rules, or a schema or presentation is invalid. | | `build_timeout` | The build took over 120 seconds. | Run `caplo tool test` locally first: it applies the same bundling, rejection and policy checks. ## Publish a version [Section titled “Publish a version”](#publish-a-version) 
```sh
caplo tool publish                      # the latest succeeded build
caplo tool publish orders --build <BUILD_ID>
```
 In Tool Studio, select **Publish** in the **Versions** view. List and inspect versions with `caplo tool version list orders` and `caplo tool version get orders@3`, or see the latest one on the console’s **Tools** page. A new version does not reach agents by itself. Each agent release pins the version that was current when the agent was published, and keeps it until you publish the agent again. See [Publish an agent](/agents/publishing/). ## Revoke a version [Section titled “Revoke a version”](#revoke-a-version) Revoke a version when it must stop running at once, for example because it has a bug that harms customers or leaks data. 
```sh
caplo tool version revoke orders@3 --reason "Refunds the wrong amount for partial orders"
```
 The reason is required and is recorded with the version. In Tool Studio, select **Revoke Version** on the version in the **Versions** view. Caution Revoking cannot be undone. Agent releases that pin the version stop calling its tools immediately, so those agents lose the tools until you publish a fixed version and publish the agents again. ## Archive, restore and delete [Section titled “Archive, restore and delete”](#archive-restore-and-delete) * `caplo tool archive orders` stops all pushes, builds and publishes. Published versions keep running until you revoke them. `caplo tool list --archived` includes archived packages. * `caplo tool restore orders` makes an archived package editable again. * `caplo tool delete orders` deletes a package that never published a version, with its draft and builds. A published package can only be archived. ## Pull source [Section titled “Pull source”](#pull-source) `caplo tool pull` downloads a package into a new or empty directory: 
```sh
caplo tool pull orders                  # the current draft
caplo tool pull orders@3 --dir ./orders-v3   # the exact source version 3 was built from
```
 Every file is checked against its recorded SHA-256 before anything is written. Use it to debug what a pinned version really runs, or to start working on a package someone else created in Tool Studio. Then run `npm install` and `caplo tool dev --no-inspector` to run it locally. ## Read invocation logs [Section titled “Read invocation logs”](#read-invocation-logs) `caplo tool logs` shows the package’s recent live calls with their outcome, timing, errors, refused hosts and log lines: 
```sh
caplo tool logs orders -n 50
caplo tool logs orders -f
```
 
```txt
14:02:11 get_order succeeded 182.4 ms cold 7d4c2a9e-...
  log info {"message":"order found","invocationId":"7d4c2a9e-...","orderId":"SO-1002"}
14:03:40 get_order failed tool_timeout 5001.2 ms 0b81f6d3-...
  The tool did not finish before its timeout
  egress denied api.partner.example: host not in the egress list of a loaded package version
```
 * `-n` sets how many recent invocations come first (default 20, at most 200); `-f` keeps streaming new ones. * Logs cover live calls from the last hour only. Test runs show their logs in the test output instead. * Each invocation keeps up to 200 log lines, from `log` in the [call context](/tools/define-tool-and-context/#the-call-context); a line over 8 KiB is cut short. `console.log` output is not included. * Never log secrets, tokens or customer details you do not need.

# Write tools safely

> Mark tools that change data as writes, require customer confirmation, use the idempotency key, report outcomes and answer status checks.

A write tool changes something outside the conversation: it cancels an order, books a slot, sends an email, issues a refund. Caplo treats writes differently from reads, so mark every such tool as one and give Caplo the information it needs to know whether the change happened. ## Choose the effect [Section titled “Choose the effect”](#choose-the-effect) Set `effect` in `caplo.yaml` for every tool: * `read`: only looks things up. Running it twice has no side effects. * `write`: changes data or triggers an action, including anything that sends a message or costs money. The effect is yours to set; Caplo does not infer it from the code or from MCP annotations. For writes, Caplo asks the customer to confirm (unless you turn that off), passes an idempotency key, tracks the outcome, and never runs the tool in places where nobody can approve it: fixture runs on Caplo skip write tools, and evaluations do not run writes. ## Customer confirmation [Section titled “Customer confirmation”](#customer-confirmation) `confirmation: required` is the default for write tools. The agent proposes the call with exact arguments, the customer reviews it, and the tool runs only after the customer confirms, with exactly those arguments. 
```yaml
tools:
  - name: cancel_order
    effect: write
    timeoutSeconds: 15
    confirmation: required
```
 * In the website widget, the customer sees a review card. Add a [`presentation`](/tools/present-results/#confirmation-and-receipt) to choose which arguments it shows, how they are labelled, and which ones the customer may change before confirming. * In WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram, the customer gets a message with **Confirm** and **Cancel** buttons. Fields cannot be edited there. * If the customer declines, or a different customer verifies in the same conversation, the write does not run. * While an earlier write in the conversation still waits for a decision, or its outcome is unknown, the agent cannot start another write. `confirmation: none` runs the write as soon as the agent calls it. Use it only for low-risk actions where a confirmation step adds nothing, such as adding a note to a support ticket. ## Use the idempotency key [Section titled “Use the idempotency key”](#use-the-idempotency-key) Every write call carries `idempotencyKey` in its context. The key is the same for every attempt and status check of one confirmed action, and different for every other action. Send it to your API so that a repeated request applies the change only once: 
```ts
async handler({ orderId }, { secrets, signal, idempotencyKey }) {
  const response = await fetch(`https://api.shop.example/orders/${encodeURIComponent(orderId)}/cancel`, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: {
      authorization: `Bearer ${secrets.get("SHOP_API_KEY")}`,
      "idempotency-key": idempotencyKey ?? "",
    },
    signal,
  });
  // ...
}
```
 If your API has no idempotency support, store the key with the change on your side and check it before applying the change again. ## Report the outcome [Section titled “Report the outcome”](#report-the-outcome) When a write ends, Caplo records one of these outcomes and the agent and customer are told accordingly: | Your handler | Outcome | Meaning | | ----------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Returns a value | Applied | The change happened. The value is the result. | | Returns `rejected(message)` | Not applied | The provider definitely did not make the change, for example it refused the request. | | Returns `pending(operationRef, message?)` | Pending | The provider accepted the change but has not finished it. | | Throws | Unknown | The change may or may not have happened. | Return `rejected` only when you are certain nothing changed. When in doubt, throw: an unknown outcome is safer than a false “not applied”, which could lead the customer to try again and, for example, pay twice. 
```ts
import { defineTool, pending, rejected } from "@caplo/tools";


export default defineTool({
  name: "cancel_order",
  // description, inputSchema, outputSchema...
  async handler({ orderId }, { secrets, signal, idempotencyKey }) {
    const response = await fetch(`https://api.shop.example/orders/${encodeURIComponent(orderId)}/cancel`, {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${secrets.get("SHOP_API_KEY")}`, "idempotency-key": idempotencyKey ?? "" },
      signal,
    });
    const body = await response.json();
    if (response.status === 200) return { orderId, status: body.status };
    if (response.status === 202) return pending(body.operationId, "The cancellation is being processed.");
    if (response.status === 409) return rejected(body.reason ?? "This order can no longer be cancelled.");
    throw new Error(`cancel failed with ${response.status}`);
  },
});
```
 `operationRef` is your reference for the pending operation: 1 to 256 printable ASCII characters. Caplo keeps it for status checks and never shows it to the model. Typed outcomes need `@caplo/tools` 0.1.6 or later, which the templates already use. ### Outcomes in MCP packages [Section titled “Outcomes in MCP packages”](#outcomes-in-mcp-packages) An MCP package reports the same outcomes in the result metadata. Advertise the capability when you create the server, and add `_meta` to each write result: 
```ts
import { Server } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/index.js";
import { EFFECT_META_KEY, EFFECT_OUTCOME_CAPABILITY } from "@caplo/tools";


const server = new Server(
  { name: "orders", version: "1.0.0" },
  { capabilities: { tools: {}, logging: {}, experimental: { ...EFFECT_OUTCOME_CAPABILITY } } },
);


// Applied:
// { content, structuredContent, _meta: { [EFFECT_META_KEY]: { version: 1, outcome: "applied" } } }
// Not applied:
// { isError: true, content, _meta: { [EFFECT_META_KEY]: { version: 1, outcome: "not_applied" } } }
// Pending:
// { content, _meta: { [EFFECT_META_KEY]: { version: 1, outcome: "pending", operationRef: "op_123" } } }
```
 Without the capability or the metadata, an error result from a write counts as unknown. ## Answer status checks [Section titled “Answer status checks”](#answer-status-checks) When a write’s outcome is unknown or pending, Caplo can ask your package what happened. Add a `status` handler to the `defineTool`: 
```ts
import { applied, defineTool, pending, rejected } from "@caplo/tools";


export default defineTool({
  name: "cancel_order",
  // ...handler as above
  async status({ operationRef }, { secrets, signal, idempotencyKey }) {
    const query = new URLSearchParams({ key: idempotencyKey ?? "", ...(operationRef && { operation: operationRef }) });
    const response = await fetch(`https://api.shop.example/operations?${query}`, {
      headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${secrets.get("SHOP_API_KEY")}` },
      signal,
    });
    if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`status lookup failed with ${response.status}`);
    const body = await response.json();
    if (body.status === "applied") return applied({ orderId: body.orderId, status: "cancelled" });
    if (body.status === "not_applied") return rejected("The order was not cancelled.");
    if (body.status === "pending") return pending(body.operationId);
    throw new Error(`unexpected status ${body.status}`);
  },
});
```
 * `ctx.idempotencyKey` is the original write’s key, and `operationRef` is the reference your `pending` returned, if any. * Return `applied(result)`, `rejected(message)` or `pending(operationRef)`. Throw when the provider cannot tell. Any other return value leaves the write unknown. * Caplo asks one check at a time per write, up to 8 times with growing gaps between checks. An MCP package answers the `caplo/status` request (`STATUS_METHOD`) with params `name` and `operationRef`, and advertises `OPERATION_STATUS_CAPABILITY` beside `EFFECT_OUTCOME_CAPABILITY`. ## Ask the customer to verify again [Section titled “Ask the customer to verify again”](#ask-the-customer-to-verify-again) For tools that act for a [verified customer](/tools/customer-aware-tools/), your API may need a stronger or more recent sign-in before it allows an operation. Return `stepUpRequired` instead of a result: 
```ts
import { stepUpRequired, stepUpFromChallenge } from "@caplo/tools";


// Explicitly: the customer must have verified at level high within the last 5 minutes.
return stepUpRequired({ level: "high", maxAuthAgeSeconds: 300 });


// Or from your API's RFC 9470 challenge (WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="insufficient_user_authentication").
const stepUp = stepUpFromChallenge(response.headers.get("www-authenticate"));
if (stepUp) return stepUp;
```
 `level` is `low`, `standard` or `high`; `maxAuthAgeSeconds` is 0 to 86400. The call applies nothing. For a write, Caplo asks the customer to verify again and, once they have, runs the write again with the same arguments. For a read, the customer is told they need to verify. ## Before you publish a write tool [Section titled “Before you publish a write tool”](#before-you-publish-a-write-tool) * `effect: write` is set, and `confirmation` is `required` unless you have a reason. * The handler sends `idempotencyKey` to your API. * Errors you are unsure about are thrown, not returned as `rejected`. * A `presentation` shows the customer what they are confirming. See [Show results to customers](/tools/present-results/). * Local fixtures for the tool point at a test environment: `caplo tool test` runs write fixtures for real on your machine. See [Test tools with fixtures](/tools/testing/).

# How Caplo charges

> What Caplo charges for, what is free, how the monthly allowance and your own provider keys change the bill, and where to find your prices.

Caplo charges for production conversations: the ones your customers have with your agents on live channels. Building and testing agents is free. Your prices come from your workspace’s price plan, which the console shows on the **Billing** page under **Your rates**. This page explains what each charge is for; it does not list prices. ## What is charged [Section titled “What is charged”](#what-is-charged) Charges come in two kinds. **Platform fees** apply to every production conversation, whichever key paid for the models: | Charge | What counts | | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | **Voice minutes** | Connected time on voice calls, by phone or in the website widget, billed by the second. | | **AI replies** | Each completed agent reply in a text channel, such as the website widget chat or WhatsApp. | **AI usage** applies only to providers set to **Caplo Managed**. It is the provider’s list price for what your agents used, plus your plan’s markup percentage, if it has one: | Charge | What counts | | ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Language models** | Input, cached input and output tokens, including audio tokens of realtime voice models. | | **Speech to text** | Audio transcribed on voice calls. | | **Text to speech** | Characters spoken on voice calls. | The **Your rates** card lists the platform fee rates and the list rates of the Caplo-managed models your workspace used in the selected period. ## What is free [Section titled “What is free”](#what-is-free) These are never charged: * Live tests from the agent builder. * Evaluation runs. * Agent building assistance and other platform work in the background. They still appear on the **Usage** page, under the matching **Source** filter, so you can see how much they use. ## Monthly allowance [Section titled “Monthly allowance”](#monthly-allowance) Your price plan can include an amount each month (**Included each month**). It is credited against all of that month’s charges, platform fees and AI usage alike. The **Billing** page shows how much of it is used, and **Estimated amount due** is the month’s charges after the allowance. Months are UTC calendar months, and days on the usage and billing pages are UTC days. ## Using your own keys [Section titled “Using your own keys”](#using-your-own-keys) When a provider is set to **Your key**, its calls run on your provider account: * They add no AI usage charge. The provider bills you directly at your rates with them. * Platform fees still apply to those conversations. * The **Billing** page counts the requests that used your keys, so you can see why a conversation has a platform fee but no AI usage. See [Bring your own key](/providers/bring-your-own-key/). ## When no price plan applies [Section titled “When no price plan applies”](#when-no-price-plan-applies) If no price plan covers a period, the **Billing** page shows “No price plan applies to this period yet, so its usage is recorded but not charged.” Usage is still recorded on the **Usage** page. If a plan started partway through the period, the page notes that usage before the plan started is not charged. A new price plan is announced on the **Billing** page before it takes effect, with its date and new rates. Prices change only with notice, and each charge uses the plan in effect when the usage happened. ## Estimates, invoices and payment [Section titled “Estimates, invoices and payment”](#estimates-invoices-and-payment) Charges on the **Billing** page are estimates until the month closes. The console does not show subscriptions, taxes, invoices or payment collection; under **Settings** then **Billing & limits**, the **Invoices** section reads “No invoices yet”. ## Who can see charges [Section titled “Who can see charges”](#who-can-see-charges) Owners, Admins, Billing members and Auditors can see the **Usage** and **Billing** pages and spend budgets. Other roles see a message that their workspace role does not allow viewing usage or billing. Only Owners and Billing members can change budgets. See [Spend budgets](/usage/spend-budgets/). ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [Usage and statements](/usage/usage-and-statements/) * [Spend budgets](/usage/spend-budgets/) * [Cost reports](/usage/cost-reports/) * [Models and providers](/providers/)

# Cost reports

> Understand the different cost figures in the console, from model cost estimates in Monitor to billed charges, and how Caplo-managed and your-key costs are separated.

The console shows cost in more than one place, and the figures answer different questions. Use this page to pick the right one and to understand why two figures can differ. | Figure | Where | Answers | Is it what you pay Caplo? | | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- | | **Estimated cost** | **Overview**, and **Conversations** under **Monitor** | Roughly what did the model calls in these conversations cost? | No. | | **AI usage** and **Platform** | **Usage & billing** then **Billing** | What will Caplo charge, by charge type, agent, channel and model? | Yes, as an estimate until the month closes. | | Quantities | **Usage & billing** then **Usage** | How many tokens, audio minutes, characters and voice minutes did we use? | No prices are shown. | ## Monitor cost estimates [Section titled “Monitor cost estimates”](#monitor-cost-estimates) The **Overview** page has an **Estimated cost** tile, and **Conversations** shows an estimated cost for each conversation and each agent turn, next to its model calls and token counts. These are quick estimates for spotting expensive conversations and agents: * They estimate model calls only, priced from a rate card whose version the tile shows (for example “rate card 2026-10”). * They price every model call the same way, whether it ran on **Caplo Managed** or **Your key**. Each model call in a turn shows which key source it used. * They do not include platform fees, speech, your plan’s markup or the monthly allowance. * **Partial estimate, some models have no price** means some calls used a model the rate card has no price for. The real cost is higher than shown. * The **Overview** tile is not estimated per channel. Clear the channel filter to see it. The tile says “not billing” for a reason: never reconcile an invoice against these figures. ## Billed cost [Section titled “Billed cost”](#billed-cost) The **Billing** page is the figure that matters for your bill. See [Usage and statements](/usage/usage-and-statements/). It separates: * **Platform**: voice minutes and AI replies, charged whichever key was used. * **AI usage**: Caplo-managed language models and speech, at list price plus your plan’s markup. Calls on your own keys add no AI usage charge. The page counts how many requests used your keys and which providers, and those providers bill you directly. Group the **Charge breakdown** by **Model** or **Provider** to see where AI usage comes from. ## Caplo-managed and your-key usage [Section titled “Caplo-managed and your-key usage”](#caplo-managed-and-your-key-usage) To compare what runs on Caplo’s accounts with what runs on yours, use the **Usage** page and set **Keys** to **Caplo-managed** or **Your keys**. Quantities come from what each provider reports, so you can compare your-key usage with the provider’s own usage dashboard. Caplo does not show prices for usage on your keys: your provider’s invoice is the source of truth for that cost. ## Known cost is a lower bound [Section titled “Known cost is a lower bound”](#known-cost-is-a-lower-bound) Caplo counts cost from what each provider reports for each request. Sometimes a report is incomplete: * On the **Usage** page, a note says how many requests have partial or missing usage from the provider. Their known quantities are included, so totals can rise as reports arrive. * On the **Billing** page, a note says when Caplo-managed requests used a meter that has no list price yet. Those requests are charged for their priced part only. * In Monitor, a partial estimate means some models have no price. In each case the figure shown is the cost Caplo knows, not more. Treat it as a minimum, and select **Refresh** later for updated numbers. ## Why the figures differ [Section titled “Why the figures differ”](#why-the-figures-differ) If **Estimated cost** in Monitor and **AI usage** on the Billing page disagree, the usual reasons are: * Monitor includes calls on your own keys; Billing charges only Caplo-managed calls. * Billing adds platform fees, speech and your plan’s markup; Monitor does not. * The two use different price sources: a rate card for Monitor, your price plan and list prices for Billing. * Usage before your price plan started is recorded but not charged. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [How Caplo charges](/usage/) * [Spend budgets](/usage/spend-budgets/) * [Bring your own key](/providers/bring-your-own-key/)

# Spend budgets

> Set monthly budgets for the workspace, an agent or a channel, get email alerts at thresholds, and optionally pause new conversations at the limit.

A spend budget is a monthly limit on what Caplo charges for production conversations, for the whole workspace, one agent or one channel. When charges reach a threshold, Caplo emails your Owners and Billing members. You can also choose to pause new conversations when a budget is reached. Budgets live on the **Spend controls** page, under **Usage & billing** in the console. ## What a budget counts [Section titled “What a budget counts”](#what-a-budget-counts) * Caplo’s charges for production conversations in the current UTC calendar month: platform fees and Caplo-managed AI usage. See [How Caplo charges](/usage/). * Charges before the monthly allowance is subtracted, so a budget fills from the first charge of the month. * Not live tests, evaluations or agent building, which are free. * Not what your providers bill you for usage on your own keys. Budgets reset on the first day of each UTC month. ## Who can manage budgets [Section titled “Who can manage budgets”](#who-can-manage-budgets) Owners and Billing members can create, change and remove budgets. Admins and Auditors can view them. Every change is recorded in the audit log. ## Create a budget [Section titled “Create a budget”](#create-a-budget) 1. Open **Usage & billing** then **Spend controls** and select **New budget**. 2. Enter a **Name**, for example “Support team”. 3. Under **Applies to**, choose **Whole workspace**, **An agent** or **A channel**. For an agent or channel, choose which one. You can’t change the scope later, and each scope can have only one budget. 4. Enter the **Monthly amount (USD)**. 5. Under **Alert at**, choose one or more thresholds: 50%, 80%, 90% or 100%. 6. Under **When the budget is reached**, choose **Alert only** or **Pause new conversations**. 7. Select **Create budget**. A new budget shows **Not checked yet** until Caplo has compared it with the month’s charges, which takes about a minute. ## Alerts [Section titled “Alerts”](#alerts) Each threshold sends one email per budget per month, to every active Owner and Billing member of the workspace. The **Alert delivery** card lists who receives them, and **Recent alerts** shows the 50 most recent alerts with the amount spent when each was sent. ## Pause new conversations [Section titled “Pause new conversations”](#pause-new-conversations) With **Pause new conversations**, when charges in the budget’s scope reach the monthly amount: * New conversations in that scope are refused. For a workspace budget, that is every channel; for an agent or channel budget, only that agent or channel. * Conversations already in progress continue and finish normally. * The pause lasts until the next month starts, or until you raise the amount, switch the budget to **Alert only** or remove it. * Owners and Billing members get an email saying new conversations are paused, even if you did not choose the 100% threshold. Caution Pausing affects your customers. Charges are checked about once a minute, so the total can go slightly over the amount before new conversations stop, and conversations in progress keep adding charges. Use a lower threshold alert to act before a pause is needed. ## Read the page [Section titled “Read the page”](#read-the-page) The tiles at the top show **Charges this month** (production only), **Forecast at month end** at the month’s pace so far (shown once 10% of the month has passed), **Budgets at risk** and **Alerts this month**. Each budget’s row shows what it has spent this month, its forecast, its thresholds, what happens at the limit and its status: | Status | Meaning | | ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **On track** | Below the amount and not forecast to reach it. | | **Forecast over** | Below the amount, but forecast to go over by month end. | | **Over budget** | At or over the amount, with **Alert only**. | | **Paused** | At or over the amount, and new conversations in its scope are paused. | | **Not checked yet** | No charges have been compared with this budget yet. | ## Resume or change a budget [Section titled “Resume or change a budget”](#resume-or-change-a-budget) To resume conversations a budget paused, open it with **Edit** and raise the **Monthly amount (USD)** above the month’s charges, or switch it to **Alert only**, then select **Save budget**. New conversations are accepted again once Caplo applies the change, usually within a minute. To remove a budget, open it with **Edit** and select **Remove budget**. Its alerts stay in the history, and any conversations it paused are allowed again.

# Usage and statements

> Read the Usage and Billing pages, follow daily charges, check the month's estimated amount due, and export the data as CSV.

Two console pages, both under **Usage & billing**, show what your agents use and what you are charged: * **Usage**: raw quantities from the models and speech services your agents call, with no prices. * **Billing**: what you pay Caplo, by day, with the month’s statement. Both need a role that can view financial data: Owner, Admin, Billing or Auditor. Days and months are UTC. ## Usage page [Section titled “Usage page”](#usage-page) The **Usage** page answers “how much did our agents use, and where?”. **Filters** at the top apply to everything on the page: | Filter | Options | | ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Source** | **Production** (the default), **Live tests**, **Evals**, **Background**, **Authoring** or **All sources**. | | **Keys** | **All keys**, **Caplo-managed** or **Your keys**. | | **Agent** and **Channel** | One agent or channel, or all. | | Dates | Last 7 or 30 days, or any range within the last 400 days. | **Tiles** show the totals for the range: **Language model tokens**, **Speech to text** (audio minutes), **Text to speech** (characters), **Voice minutes** and **Conversations**. Voice minutes and conversations are not paid with a provider key, so they show no value when you filter by keys. **Usage by channel** shows how language model tokens or voice minutes split across your channels. **Language models** charts tokens over time and lists them by **Model**, **Provider**, **Agent** or **Channel**, with **Input**, **Cached input**, **Output**, **Audio** (realtime models) and **Requests** columns. **Voice** charts **Speech to text**, **Text to speech** or **Voice minutes**, grouped the same way. Usage on your own keys is included on this page. Those providers bill you, not Caplo. If a provider has not reported full usage for some requests yet, a note under the table says how many; their known quantities are included. Quantities update as providers report them, and each request counts once at its latest report. ## Billing page [Section titled “Billing page”](#billing-page) The **Billing** page answers “what will Caplo charge us?”. **Filters**: **Charges** (**All charges**, **Voice**, **Text** or **Language models**), **Agent**, **Channel** and **Billing dates** (**This month**, **Last month**, **Last 30 days** or a custom range). **Tiles**: | Tile | What it shows | | ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | **Estimated amount due** | The statement for the month that contains the end of your date range: that month’s charges to date, after the included allowance. It always covers the whole workspace, whatever the filters. | | **Platform** | Platform fees in the range, with the number of voice minutes and AI replies. | | **AI usage** | Caplo-managed models and speech, at list price plus your plan’s markup. | | **Included allowance** | How much of the month’s allowance is used, and how much is left. | **Charges over time** shows daily or weekly charges by type. **Charges by** splits them by channel, agent or charge type. **Charge breakdown** lists every charge in the range grouped by **Charge**, **Model**, **Provider**, **Agent** or **Channel**, with quantity, rate, amount and share. Notes under the breakdown explain two cases: * How many requests used your own keys. Only the platform fee for those conversations appears. * Caplo-managed requests that used a meter without a list price yet. They are charged for their priced part only. ### Daily charges and the monthly statement [Section titled “Daily charges and the monthly statement”](#daily-charges-and-the-monthly-statement) Each charge belongs to the UTC day the usage happened and is priced with the price plan in effect at that time. The monthly statement adds up the calendar month’s charges and subtracts the allowance. It is an estimate until the month closes. To follow a month as it goes, choose **This month**. To review a finished month, choose **Last month**. **Settings** then **Billing & limits** shows the same month-to-date summary: **Estimated amount due**, **Charges to date** and **Included each month**. ## Export [Section titled “Export”](#export) Both pages have **Export CSV**: * **Usage** exports exact quantities per UTC day, service, provider and model, with request counts and any usage Caplo cannot classify yet. * **Billing** exports every charge per UTC day, with its charge type, provider, model, agent, channel, quantity, unit, amount in USD and whether a price plan rated it. ## Consistent numbers [Section titled “Consistent numbers”](#consistent-numbers) Each page reads all its charts and tables from one snapshot, shown at the bottom of the page with its time. Select **Refresh** to load a newer one. Exports use the same snapshot as the page, so a CSV matches what you see. ## Related [Section titled “Related”](#related) * [How Caplo charges](/usage/) * [Spend budgets](/usage/spend-budgets/) * [Cost reports](/usage/cost-reports/)

# Agent YAML

> Every field of an agent file (apiVersion v2.0.0), generated from the schema the CLI and console validate against.

For validation and completion in your editor, add this first line to the file (YAML language server, used by VS Code and others): 
```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.caplo.si/schemas/agent-2.0.0.json
```
 The schema is also published at [/schemas/agent-2.0.0.json](/schemas/agent-2.0.0.json). ## Agent file [Section titled “Agent file”](#agent-file) A file with `kind: Agent` edits or creates one agent. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------------------ | ------------------------------ | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `apiVersion` | string | Yes | Pattern `^v2(?:\.0(?:\.0)?)?$`. | | `kind` | `"Agent"` | Yes | | | `evals` | object | | See [`evals`](#evals). | | `name` | string | Yes | Agent name shown throughout the workspace. At most 80 characters. | | `description` | string | | Short explanation of what the agent helps with. At most 240 characters. | | `instructions` | string | Yes | Reusable behavior, tone, and boundaries. No voice or channel settings. | | `greeting` | string | Yes | Opening message when this agent serves a channel. | | `model` | object | Yes | Language model and response limits for this agent. See [`model`](#model). | | `tools` | array of string | | Stable IDs of workspace tools. | | `knowledgeBases` | array of string | | IDs of ready workspace knowledge bases referenced by this agent. | | `actions` | array of `"transfer"`, `"end"` | | Agent-level conversation actions. Channel-specific workflows stay on the entry point. | | `subAgents` | array of object | | Links to reusable agents. A linked agent may have sub-agents of its own. See [`subAgents`](#subagents). | | `routing` | object | | How this agent picks a sub-agent. Omitted means model. See [`routing`](#routing). | | `policy` | object | | How the agent loop runs. Omitted fields take the platform defaults; an empty guidance or style turns that block off. See [`policy`](#policy). | | `suggestedReplies` | object | | Short next messages offered under the replies this agent writes itself, on channels that can show them. Omit to turn them off. See [`suggestedReplies`](#suggestedreplies). | | `visitorFeedback` | boolean | | Let website visitors rate this agent’s replies with a thumbs up or down and an optional reason; operators see the ratings in Conversations and the agent never does. Omit to turn it off. | Other fields are refused. ### `evals` [Section titled “evals”](#evals) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------- | --------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | | `cases` | array of object | Yes | At most 100 items. See [`evals.cases`](#evalscases). | Other fields are refused. #### `evals.cases` [Section titled “evals.cases”](#evalscases) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------------------ | ---------------------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `id` | string | Yes | Pattern `^[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12}$`. | | `name` | string | Yes | At most 80 characters. | | `input` | string | Yes | At most 8000 characters. | | `desiredOutcome` | string | Yes | At most 4000 characters. | | `uncertainty` | string | | | | `prohibitedAction` | string | | | | `toolFailure` | string | | | | `answerContains` | string | | | | `answerExcludes` | string | | | | `subAgent` | string | | | | `avoidSubAgent` | string | | | | `tool` | string | | | | `routedBy` | `"fast"`, `"model"`, `"any"` | | | Other fields are refused. ### `model` [Section titled “model”](#model) Language model and response limits for this agent. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | --------------- | ------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `provider` | string | Yes | Provider configured in this workspace. Example: OpenAI. | | `name` | string | Yes | Model ID or display name from this workspace’s catalog. | | `temperature` | number | | Lower values produce more consistent responses. From 0 to 1. Default `0.4`. | | `responseLimit` | integer | | Maximum response tokens; the model’s tested maximum output bounds it when the agent is tested or published. From 64 to 128000. Default `1024`. | Other fields are refused. ### `subAgents` [Section titled “subAgents”](#subagents) A reusable agent this agent may call. Use agentId for an existing agent or local for another agent in this bundle. The linked agent runs with its release’s own tools and knowledge. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | -------------- | -------------------------------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `agentId` | string | | Stable ID of an existing workspace agent. | | `local` | string | | Key of an agent defined in this bundle. | | `release` | string | | ‘latest’ resolves to a fixed child release when the parent publishes; or choose a fixed semantic version now. Default `"latest"`. | | `useWhen` | string | Yes | When the parent should call this agent. | | `doNotUseWhen` | string | | Requests that should stay with the parent or another agent. | | `mode` | `"task_tool"`, `"active_conversation"` | | task_tool: this agent asks it in plain words and hears back. active_conversation: it takes over the conversation. Default `"task_tool"`. | Other fields are refused. ### `routing` [Section titled “routing”](#routing) How this agent picks a sub-agent. Omitted means model. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | --------------- | ------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `mode` | `"model"`, `"fast"` | Yes | model: the agent’s model decides. fast: a decision service picks a sub-agent in about 0.3 s and the model decides when unsure. fast needs doNotUseWhen on every sub-agent. | | `minConfidence` | number | | Lowest decision confidence fast routing accepts before the model decides. From 0.6 to 0.99. Default `0.85`. | Other fields are refused. ### `policy` [Section titled “policy”](#policy) How the agent loop runs. Omitted fields take the platform defaults; an empty guidance or style turns that block off. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------------------- | ------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `guidance` | string | | Platform guidance that follows the instructions. At most 4000 characters. | | `chatStyle` | string | | Reply style for chat turns. At most 2000 characters. | | `voiceStyle` | string | | Reply style for voice calls. At most 2000 characters. | | `history` | object | | See [`policy.history`](#policyhistory). | | `knowledgePassages` | integer | | Passages one knowledge search returns. Default 6. From 1 to 8. | | `maxModelCalls` | integer | | Model calls of the whole agent graph per turn. Default 16. From 1 to 48. | | `maxReads` | integer | | Tool lookups per turn. Default 8. From 1 to 32. | | `maxSearches` | integer | | Knowledge searches per turn. Default 4. From 1 to 16. | | `maxDepth` | integer | | Linked agent depth. Default 3. From 1 to 4. | | `maxParallelReads` | integer | | Lookups run at once. Default 4. From 1024 to 32000. | Other fields are refused. ### `suggestedReplies` [Section titled “suggestedReplies”](#suggestedreplies) Short next messages offered under the replies this agent writes itself, on channels that can show them. # caplo.yaml (tool package)

> Every field of a tool package caplo.yaml, generated from the schema the CLI and builder validate against.

For validation and completion in your editor, add this first line to the file (YAML language server, used by VS Code and others): 
```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.caplo.si/schemas/caplo.schema.json
```
 The schema is also published at [/schemas/caplo.schema.json](/schemas/caplo.schema.json). ## Fields [Section titled “Fields”](#fields) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ---------------- | ---------------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `apiVersion` | string | Yes | v1, v1.0, and v1.0.0 resolve to the latest matching schema. Pattern `^v1(\.0(\.0)?)?$`. | | `kind` | `"ToolPackage"` | Yes | | | `name` | string | Yes | Unique per workspace. Pattern `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,39}$`. | | `type` | `"function"`, `"mcp"` | Yes | | | `description` | string | | At most 500 characters. | | `entry` | string | Yes | Module that exports createServer() (mcp) or a default defineTool() (function). Pattern `^(?!/)(?!.*(^\|/)\.\.(/\|$))[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+\.(ts\|mts\|js\|mjs)$`. | | `runtime` | `"node24"` | Yes | | | `tools` | array of object | | Effect and timeout for every tool the server lists (mcp only). At least 1 items. At most 64 items. See [`tools`](#tools). | | `effect` | `"read"`, `"write"` | | Function packages only. | | `timeoutSeconds` | integer | | Function packages only. From 1 to 30. | | `confirmation` | `"required"`, `"none"` | | Function packages only. | | `egress` | array of string | | Exact hosts or \*.suffix wildcards. Empty or absent means no network. At most 50 items. | | `secrets` | array of string | | Secret names; values are configured per workspace and passed per call. At most 50 items. | | `tests` | string | | Glob of fixture files relative to the package root. Default `"tests/*.json"`. | | `subject` | object | | Function packages only. Pattern `^(?![\s\S]*(?:https?:\|www\.))[^\s&lt;&gt;{}\u0000-\u001f\u007f][^&lt;&gt;{}\u0000-\u001f\u007f]{1,87}\.$`. | Other fields are refused. ### `elicitation` [Section titled “elicitation”](#elicitation) Function packages only. | Field | Type | Required | Description | | -------- | ----------------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `style` | `"form"`, `"questions"` | | form (default) shows each request as a form on the website widget. questions asks one tapped question per request, on the website and in WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram: each request names one field as a required single choice of 2 to 9 options with labels of at most 20 characters, and the tool may ask as many questions per call as it declares fields, up to 8. | | `fields` | array of object | Yes | At least 1 items. At most 12 items. See [`elicitation.fields`](#elicitationfields). | Other fields are refused. #### `elicitation.fields` [Section titled “elicitation.fields”](#elicitationfields) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------ | ------ | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `name` | string | Yes | A property name the tool’s requestedSchema may use. Pattern `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}$`. | Other fields are refused.

# CLI reference

> Every caplo command, flag, environment variable and exit code, generated from the released CLI.

Install the CLI with the [installation guide](/cli/install/). Task guides are under [CLI](/cli/workflow/). 
```sh
caplo [flags]
```
 ## Commands [Section titled “Commands”](#commands) ### Project [Section titled “Project”](#project) | Command | Description | | ---------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | [`caplo diff`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-diff) | Show how the project differs from the workspace drafts | | [`caplo eval`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-eval) | Push the selection, then run each selected agent’s eval cases against its draft | | [`caplo init`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-init) | Create a project: caplo.yaml, agent files, and tool packages | | [`caplo publish`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-publish) | Validate, push, test, evaluate, and publish bottom up: tools, then child agents, then parents | | [`caplo pull`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-pull) | Write a workspace agent, its sub-agents, and their packages into a project | | [`caplo push`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-push) | Create or update the workspace drafts, children first, so you can live test them | | [`caplo test`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-test) | Run the selected tool packages’ fixtures locally, like caplo tool test | | [`caplo validate`](/reference/cli/project/#caplo-validate) | Check the project offline: schemas, references, link cycles, and the depth limit | ### Workspace resources [Section titled “Workspace resources”](#workspace-resources) | Command | Description | | ---------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | | [`caplo completion`](/reference/cli/#caplo-completion) | Generate the autocompletion script for the specified shell | | [`caplo version`](/reference/cli/#caplo-version) | Show the caplo version | ## Global flags [Section titled “Global flags”](#global-flags) These flags work with every command. | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `--workspace`, `-w` | string | | workspace ID or name (default: $CAPLO_WORKSPACE, else the one selected with caplo workspace use) | ## Environment variables [Section titled “Environment variables”](#environment-variables) | Variable | Description | | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `CAPLO_API_KEY` | Authenticate as an API client instead of the login session, for CI and automation. Create one with caplo workspace api-client. | | `CAPLO_WORKSPACE` | Workspace ID or name for every command, unless –workspace is given. | | `CAPLO_ENV` | Project environment from caplo.yaml, unless –env is given. | | `CAPLO_API_URL` | API base URL used by caplo auth login, unless –api-url is given. | | `CAPLO_ISSUER` | OpenID issuer used by caplo auth login, unless –issuer is given. | | `CAPLO_CONFIG_DIR` | Directory for the CLI configuration and session, instead of \~/.config/caplo ($XDG_CONFIG_HOME/caplo), or %AppData%\caplo on Windows. | | `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` | Base configuration directory when CAPLO_CONFIG_DIR is not set. | ## Exit codes [Section titled “Exit codes”](#exit-codes) | Code | Meaning | | ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `0` | Success. | | `1` | An error: the API refused the request, a file could not be read, or the command failed. | | `2` | A usage error: an unknown command or flag, a wrong argument, or a publish that needs –yes. | | `3` | A failed gate: validation, tool fixtures, or eval cases did not pass. | | `130` | Interrupted with Ctrl-C (SIGINT). A second Ctrl-C ends the process at once. | | `143` | Terminated with SIGTERM. | ## caplo completion [Section titled “caplo completion”](#caplo-completion) Generate the autocompletion script for the specified shell. 
```sh
caplo completion
```
 Generate the autocompletion script for caplo for the specified shell. See each sub-command’s help for details on how to use the generated script. **Commands** * [`caplo completion bash`](#caplo-completion-bash) * [`caplo completion fish`](#caplo-completion-fish) * [`caplo completion powershell`](#caplo-completion-powershell) * [`caplo completion zsh`](#caplo-completion-zsh) ## caplo completion bash [Section titled “caplo completion bash”](#caplo-completion-bash) Generate the autocompletion script for bash. 
```sh
caplo completion bash
```
 Generate the autocompletion script for the bash shell. This script depends on the ‘bash-completion’ package. If it is not installed already, you can install it via your OS’s package manager. To load completions in your current shell session: 
```sh
source <(caplo completion bash)
```
 To load completions for every new session, execute once: #### Linux: [Section titled “Linux:”](#linux) 
```sh
caplo completion bash > /etc/bash_completion.d/caplo
```
 #### macOS: [Section titled “macOS:”](#macos) 
```sh
caplo completion bash > $(brew --prefix)/etc/bash_completion.d/caplo
```
 You will need to start a new shell for this setup to take effect. **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ----- | ------- | ------------------------------- | | `--no-descriptions` | | | disable completion descriptions | ## caplo completion fish [Section titled “caplo completion fish”](#caplo-completion-fish) Generate the autocompletion script for fish. 
```sh
caplo completion fish [flags]
```
 Generate the autocompletion script for the fish shell. To load completions in your current shell session: 
```sh
caplo completion fish | source
```
 To load completions for every new session, execute once: 
```sh
caplo completion fish > ~/.config/fish/completions/caplo.fish
```
 You will need to start a new shell for this setup to take effect. **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ----- | ------- | ------------------------------- | | `--no-descriptions` | | | disable completion descriptions | ## caplo completion powershell [Section titled “caplo completion powershell”](#caplo-completion-powershell) Generate the autocompletion script for powershell. 
```sh
caplo completion powershell [flags]
```
 Generate the autocompletion script for powershell. To load completions in your current shell session: 
```sh
caplo completion powershell | Out-String | Invoke-Expression
```
 To load completions for every new session, add the output of the above command to your powershell profile. **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ----- | ------- | ------------------------------- | | `--no-descriptions` | | | disable completion descriptions | ## caplo completion zsh [Section titled “caplo completion zsh”](#caplo-completion-zsh) Generate the autocompletion script for zsh. 
```sh
caplo completion zsh [flags]
```
 Generate the autocompletion script for the zsh shell. If shell completion is not already enabled in your environment you will need to enable it. You can execute the following once: 
```sh
echo "autoload -U compinit; compinit" >> ~/.zshrc
```
 To load completions in your current shell session: 
```sh
source <(caplo completion zsh)
```
 To load completions for every new session, execute once: #### Linux: [Section titled “Linux:”](#linux-1) 
```sh
caplo completion zsh > "${fpath[1]}/_caplo"
```
 #### macOS: [Section titled “macOS:”](#macos-1) 
```sh
caplo completion zsh > $(brew --prefix)/share/zsh/site-functions/_caplo
```
 You will need to start a new shell for this setup to take effect. **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ----- | ------- | ------------------------------- | | `--no-descriptions` | | | disable completion descriptions | ## caplo version [Section titled “caplo version”](#caplo-version) Show the caplo version.

 Shows the CLI version, the commit it was built from, and the Go version and platform. caplo –version prints the version alone. **Examples**
