# caplo agent

> Build, test, evaluate, and publish one agent.

Build, test, evaluate, and publish one agent. 
```sh
caplo agent
```
 Commands for a single agent file or workspace agent. A workspace agent is named by its name or ID. To push and publish the agents of a project together, children first, use caplo push and caplo publish. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent init -f support.yaml
caplo agent push -f support.yaml
caplo agent chat "Support"
caplo agent publish "Support"
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo agent chat`](#caplo-agent-chat) * [`caplo agent eval`](#caplo-agent-eval) * [`caplo agent get`](#caplo-agent-get) * [`caplo agent init`](#caplo-agent-init) * [`caplo agent list`](#caplo-agent-list) * [`caplo agent publish`](#caplo-agent-publish) * [`caplo agent pull`](#caplo-agent-pull) * [`caplo agent push`](#caplo-agent-push) * [`caplo agent release`](#caplo-agent-release) * [`caplo agent test`](#caplo-agent-test) * [`caplo agent validate`](#caplo-agent-validate) ## caplo agent chat [Section titled “caplo agent chat”](#caplo-agent-chat) Chat with the agent’s saved draft, or a release, in a private test conversation. 
```sh
caplo agent chat <agent> [flags]
```
 Starts a private test conversation with the agent’s saved draft, or with a release named by –release, and reads one message per line from standard input. Each reply is printed with the sub-agents and tools the turn used. End with Ctrl-D, /exit, or /quit; the test session is closed when the chat ends. A write awaiting a decision is shown as its review, as a review of the whole plan, or as a write that can only be declined: one with no review here, or one waiting for the customer to sign in. /confirm or /cancel decides the write shown last, as the widget’s buttons do; ordinary text never approves a write. If the write changed meanwhile, the current one is shown to answer again, and a refused (409) message or decision is reported without ending the chat. A message or decision that was not sent makes the command exit 1 at its end; without a terminal, a failed turn makes it exit 3. Replies arrive on the agent-test event stream. It resumes after the last event when it reconnects, reloads the history on a reset, reconnects with a fresh token when asked to reauthenticate, and retries network failures, 429, and 5xx, but not an unknown host, a rejected TLS certificate, or another 4xx. When no test session is free, caplo agent test list and caplo agent test close free one. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent chat "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent chat "Shop Concierge" --release 2.1.0
printf 'Where is order 1042?\n/confirm\n' | caplo agent chat "Shop Concierge"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `--release` | string | | chat with this release, by number or version, instead of the draft | ## caplo agent eval [Section titled “caplo agent eval”](#caplo-agent-eval) Run and inspect an agent’s evaluations. 
```sh
caplo agent eval
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent eval run "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent eval list "Shop Concierge"
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo agent eval get`](#caplo-agent-eval-get) * [`caplo agent eval list`](#caplo-agent-eval-list) * [`caplo agent eval run`](#caplo-agent-eval-run) ## caplo agent eval get [Section titled “caplo agent eval get”](#caplo-agent-eval-get) Show an evaluation run and its cases. 
```sh
caplo agent eval get <agent> <run> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent eval get "Shop Concierge" 0c9e7a52-1d4b-4e8f-a3c6-5b2d9f0e1a47
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent eval list [Section titled “caplo agent eval list”](#caplo-agent-eval-list) List the agent’s evaluation runs, newest first. 
```sh
caplo agent eval list <agent> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent eval list "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent eval list "Shop Concierge" -n 5 -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--limit`, `-n` | int | `20` | most runs to show (at most 100) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent eval run [Section titled “caplo agent eval run”](#caplo-agent-eval-run) Run the agent’s eval cases against its saved draft. 
```sh
caplo agent eval run <agent>
```
 Runs every case of the agent’s eval suite against its saved draft in isolated test conversations, as publishing does, and prints each case with its failed checks and the grader’s rationale. A passing run for the same draft and suite is reused. It waits up to 12 minutes for the run and exits 3 when a case fails. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent eval run "Shop Concierge"
```
 ## caplo agent get [Section titled “caplo agent get”](#caplo-agent-get) Show an agent: draft, model, tools, sub-agents, and latest release. 
```sh
caplo agent get <agent> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent get "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent get 6f1d2c3b-8a4e-4f0a-9b7c-2d1e0f3a4b5c -o yaml
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent init [Section titled “caplo agent init”](#caplo-agent-init) Write a new agent file to edit, validate, and push. 
```sh
caplo agent init [flags]
```
 Writes a starter agent file with one eval case. The file must not exist. Without –name, the agent is named after the file: its first letter capitalized and dashes as spaces. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent init
caplo agent init -f agents/help-desk.yaml
caplo agent init -f support.yaml --name "Support Desk"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------------- | ------ | ------------ | ---------------------------------------- | | `--file`, `-f` | string | `agent.yaml` | agent file to write | | `--name` | string | | agent name (default: from the file name) | ## caplo agent list [Section titled “caplo agent list”](#caplo-agent-list) List the workspace’s agents. 
```sh
caplo agent list [flags]
```
 Lists the workspace’s agents with their latest release, draft revision, and status. Archived agents are left out unless –archived. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent list
caplo agent list --archived -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--archived` | | | include archived agents | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent publish [Section titled “caplo agent publish”](#caplo-agent-publish) Pin tools, run the evals, and publish the agent’s saved draft as a release. 
```sh
caplo agent publish <agent> [flags]
```
 Publishes the saved draft of a workspace agent: it pins the draft’s tool versions, checks that the draft is ready, runs its eval cases, and publishes it. The agent needs at least one eval case, and every case must pass. Its sub-agents must have releases already; caplo publish releases a project’s agents together. A released draft is published again only when its packages have newer versions. When its eval cases changed since its release, they run against it and the release is kept. Without –bump the version follows the console: a first release, or a change of sub-agents, provider, or model, or a removed tool, action, or knowledge base, is major; an added one is minor; anything else is a patch. It asks before publishing; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent publish "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent publish "Shop Concierge" --bump minor --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--bump` | string | | version bump: major, minor, or patch (default: suggested from the changes) | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | publish without asking | ## caplo agent pull [Section titled “caplo agent pull”](#caplo-agent-pull) Write an agent’s draft as the console’s Export YAML, to –file or standard output. 
```sh
caplo agent pull <agent> [flags]
```
 Writes the agent’s draft byte for byte as the console’s Export YAML. Sub-agents are linked by agentId, and the eval cases are not included; caplo pull writes an agent with its sub-agents and eval cases into a project. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent pull "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent pull "Shop Concierge" -f concierge.yaml
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------------- | ------ | ------- | ---------------------------------------- | | `--file`, `-f` | string | | file to write (default: standard output) | ## caplo agent push [Section titled “caplo agent push”](#caplo-agent-push) Save an agent file as the agent’s draft, creating the agent if needed, and its evals as the eval suite. 
```sh
caplo agent push [flags]
```
 Saves the file as the draft of the workspace agent with its name, creating the agent when there is none. The file’s evals replace the agent’s eval suite; without an evals key the suite is left alone. Cases in the console’s evals: {cases: \[…]} form keep their IDs, and a plain list of cases matches the suite’s cases by name. Inside a project, local references resolve, and the sub-agents and packages they name must exist in the workspace already. –dry-run prints the draft diff without saving. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent push -f agent.yaml
caplo agent push -f agent.yaml --dry-run
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------------- | ------ | ------- | ---------------------------------- | | `--dry-run` | | | show the draft diff without saving | | `--file`, `-f` | string | | agent file | ## caplo agent release [Section titled “caplo agent release”](#caplo-agent-release) List, show, and revoke an agent’s releases. 
```sh
caplo agent release
```
 A release is a published draft revision with its tool versions and sub-agent releases pinned. Channels serve releases, and parents link them. A release is named by its number, its version with or without a leading v, or its ID. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent release list "Shop Concierge"
caplo agent release revoke "Shop Concierge" 2.1.0 --reason "wrong refund policy"
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo agent release get`](#caplo-agent-release-get) * [`caplo agent release list`](#caplo-agent-release-list) * [`caplo agent release revoke`](#caplo-agent-release-revoke) ## caplo agent release get [Section titled “caplo agent release get”](#caplo-agent-release-get) Show a release: version, tools, sub-agent releases, and eval gate. 
```sh
caplo agent release get <agent> <release> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent release get "Shop Concierge" 2.1.0
caplo agent release get "Shop Concierge" 7 -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent release list [Section titled “caplo agent release list”](#caplo-agent-release-list) List the agent’s releases, newest first. 
```sh
caplo agent release list <agent> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent release list "Shop Concierge"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo agent release revoke [Section titled “caplo agent release revoke”](#caplo-agent-release-revoke) Permanently revoke a release, so channels and parents can no longer run it. 
```sh
caplo agent release revoke <agent> <release> [flags]
```
 Revokes a release permanently; it cannot be undone. Channels that serve it and parents that link it can no longer run it, and links at release: latest and channel deploys skip it. It needs –reason and asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent release revoke "Shop Concierge" 2.1.0 --reason "wrong refund policy"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------- | | `--reason` | string | | why the release is revoked (required) | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | do not ask for confirmation | ## caplo agent test [Section titled “caplo agent test”](#caplo-agent-test) Manage your private test sessions. 
```sh
caplo agent test
```
 Each caplo agent chat runs in a private test session, which holds test capacity until it is closed. The chat closes its session when it ends; list and close find and free sessions left open. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent test list
caplo agent test close 4d2f8b1e-6c3a-4f7e-9d05-1a2b3c4d5e6f
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo agent test close`](#caplo-agent-test-close) * [`caplo agent test list`](#caplo-agent-test-list) ## caplo agent test close [Section titled “caplo agent test close”](#caplo-agent-test-close) Close one of your private test sessions and release its capacity. 
```sh
caplo agent test close <session-id>
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent test close 4d2f8b1e-6c3a-4f7e-9d05-1a2b3c4d5e6f
```
 ## caplo agent test list [Section titled “caplo agent test list”](#caplo-agent-test-list) List your open test sessions across agents in this workspace. 
```sh
caplo agent test list
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent test list
```
 ## caplo agent validate [Section titled “caplo agent validate”](#caplo-agent-validate) Check an agent file offline: schema, references, and link policies. 
```sh
caplo agent validate [flags]
```
 Checks the file against the agent schema and its references without calling the workspace. When the file is an agent of a project, by the project’s agents glob, local references resolve and the project’s problems with the file are reported too. Outside a project, sub-agents are linked by agentId and tools name a package ID or a remote MCP server. Problems are printed and the command exits 3. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo agent validate -f agent.yaml
caplo agent validate -f agents/orders.yaml
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------- | | `--file`, `-f` | string | | agent file |

# caplo auth

> Log in, log out, and show the session.

Log in, log out, and show the session. 
```sh
caplo auth
```
 A login session authenticates a person. CI and automation use an API client’s key in CAPLO_API_KEY instead; see caplo workspace api-client. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo auth login
caplo auth status
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo auth login`](#caplo-auth-login) * [`caplo auth logout`](#caplo-auth-logout) * [`caplo auth status`](#caplo-auth-status) ## caplo auth login [Section titled “caplo auth login”](#caplo-auth-login) Log in through the browser, or with –device on a machine without one. 
```sh
caplo auth login [flags]
```
 Logs in with the caplo-cli OpenID client: in the browser with the authorization code flow and PKCE, answered on a 127.0.0.1 port, or with –device through the device authorization grant. The refresh token is stored in the OS keychain, or with –insecure-storage (or credential-storage file) in a 0600 file in the configuration directory; access tokens stay in memory. Refresh tokens rotate on every use, and caplo processes share a lock so that none reuses a token another has used. The API and issuer are –api-url and –issuer, else CAPLO_API_URL and CAPLO_ISSUER, else the saved settings, else production (<https://api.caplo.si> and <https://auth.caplo.si/realms/caplo>). A successful login saves them, so the variables affect login only and never redirect an existing session. Login keeps the selected workspace while you still belong to it, and otherwise selects your only workspace. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo auth login
caplo auth login --device
caplo auth login --api-url https://api.staging.example.com --issuer https://auth.staging.example.com/realms/caplo
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------------------- | ------ | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--api-url` | string | | Caplo API URL, for example <https://api.caplo.si> | | `--device` | | | use the device authorization grant | | `--insecure-storage` | | | keep the refresh token in a 0600 file instead of the OS keychain (default: the saved credential-storage) | | `--issuer` | string | | OpenID issuer URL, for example <https://auth.caplo.si/realms/caplo> | | `--no-browser` | | | print the login URL without opening a browser | ## caplo auth logout [Section titled “caplo auth logout”](#caplo-auth-logout) Revoke the session and delete the stored refresh token. 
```sh
caplo auth logout
```
 Deletes the stored refresh token, then revokes the session at the issuer. When the revocation fails, the local token is deleted all the same and the command reports the failure. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo auth logout
```
 ## caplo auth status [Section titled “caplo auth status”](#caplo-auth-status) Show the logged-in user, API, and selected workspace. 
```sh
caplo auth status [flags]
```
 Shows the logged-in user, the API and issuer, where the credentials are stored, and the selected workspace. With CAPLO_API_KEY it shows the API client, its workspace, and its scopes instead. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo auth status
caplo auth status -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml |

# caplo channel

> List channels and deploy agent releases to them.

List channels and deploy agent releases to them. 
```sh
caplo channel
```
 A channel is a website widget, WhatsApp number, Messenger or Instagram account, Outlook mailbox, or phone number, and serves one agent release. Channels are created in the console; deploy and rollback change the release they serve. New conversations get the new release, and open ones keep the release they started with. A channel is named by its ID, its name, or \<kind>/\<name>. Every other setting of a channel stays as it is, and its revision guards a deploy against a concurrent change in the console. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo channel list
caplo channel deploy Website --agent "Shop Concierge"
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo channel deploy`](#caplo-channel-deploy) * [`caplo channel get`](#caplo-channel-get) * [`caplo channel list`](#caplo-channel-list) * [`caplo channel rollback`](#caplo-channel-rollback) ## caplo channel deploy [Section titled “caplo channel deploy”](#caplo-channel-deploy) Point channels at an agent release, by default their agent’s newest release. 
```sh
caplo channel deploy <channel>... [--agent <agent>[@<version>]] [flags]
```
 Points each channel at the release: –agent names the agent and, after @, its version (1.4.0) or release number; without a version it is the agent’s newest release that is not revoked. Without –agent each channel keeps its agent. A revoked release is never deployed. It asks for confirmation; –yes skips it. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo channel deploy Website
caplo channel deploy Website "twilio/Support line" --agent "Shop Concierge@2.1.0" --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | | `--agent` | string | | agent name or ID, with @version or @number to pick a release | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | deploy without asking | ## caplo channel get [Section titled “caplo channel get”](#caplo-channel-get) Show a channel and the release it serves. 
```sh
caplo channel get <channel> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo channel get Website
caplo channel get "twilio/Support line"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo channel list [Section titled “caplo channel list”](#caplo-channel-list) List the workspace’s channels and the release each serves. 
```sh
caplo channel list [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo channel list
caplo channel list --agent "Shop Concierge" -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------- | | `--agent` | string | | only the channels serving this agent (name or ID) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo channel rollback [Section titled “caplo channel rollback”](#caplo-channel-rollback) Point channels back at their agent’s release before the one they serve. 
```sh
caplo channel rollback <channel>... [flags]
```
 Points each channel at the newest release of its agent, older than the one it serves, that is not revoked. The release is served as it was published. Publishing never moves a channel; caplo channel deploy points it at a newer release. It asks for confirmation; –yes skips it. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo channel rollback Website --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ----- | ------- | ------------------------ | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | roll back without asking |

# caplo config

> Show and change CLI settings.

Show and change CLI settings. 
```sh
caplo config
```
 Settings are saved in config.yaml in the configuration directory: CAPLO_CONFIG_DIR, else \~/.config/caplo ($XDG_CONFIG_HOME/caplo), or %AppData%\caplo on Windows. caplo workspace use saves the selected workspace there too. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo config list
caplo config set credential-storage file
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo config get`](#caplo-config-get) * [`caplo config list`](#caplo-config-list) * [`caplo config set`](#caplo-config-set) ## caplo config get [Section titled “caplo config get”](#caplo-config-get) Show one setting. 
```sh
caplo config get <key>
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo config get api-url
```
 ## caplo config list [Section titled “caplo config list”](#caplo-config-list) Show every setting. 
```sh
caplo config list [flags]
```
 Shows the saved settings, the production defaults for those not saved, and the selected workspace. Defaults are not saved. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo config list
caplo config list -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo config set [Section titled “caplo config set”](#caplo-config-set) Change one setting: api-url, issuer, client-id, credential-storage. 
```sh
caplo config set <key> <value>
```
 Changes a saved setting: api-url, issuer, client-id (the OAuth client, caplo-cli by default), or credential-storage (keychain or file). Changing api-url clears the selected workspace. Run caplo auth login afterwards to start a session with the new setting. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo config set credential-storage file
caplo config set api-url https://api.staging.example.com
```

# caplo identity

> Generate keys and sign or check host sign-in assertions locally.

Generate keys and sign or check host sign-in assertions locally. 
```sh
caplo identity
```
 Local helpers for a host that signs its customers in to Caplo with signed assertions, through a customer identity connection of type signed_assertion. Nothing is sent to Caplo. Only verify-assertion makes a request, to fetch a JWKS from an https URL as Identity does: within 3 seconds, at most 64 KiB, and without redirects. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo identity keygen --out keys
caplo identity sign-assertion --key keys/<kid>.private.jwk.json --iss https://shop.example.com \
  --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> --sub cust-1042 --nonce <nonce> > assertion.jwt
caplo identity verify-assertion --jwks keys/<kid>.public.jwk.json --iss https://shop.example.com \
  --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> - < assertion.jwt
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo identity keygen`](#caplo-identity-keygen) * [`caplo identity sign-assertion`](#caplo-identity-sign-assertion) * [`caplo identity verify-assertion`](#caplo-identity-verify-assertion) ## caplo identity keygen [Section titled “caplo identity keygen”](#caplo-identity-keygen) Write a new signing key: private JWK and PEM, and the public JWK to give the connection; nothing is sent. 
```sh
caplo identity keygen [flags]
```
 Writes \<kid>.private.jwk.json and \<kid>.private.pem, readable only by you, and \<kid>.public.jwk.json, with kid, alg, and use sig, into –out. Add the public JWK to the connection’s keys or your JWKS, and keep the private key on your server. RSA keys have 3072 bits, and the key ID defaults to the key’s RFC 7638 thumbprint. Existing files are never overwritten. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo identity keygen
caplo identity keygen --alg EdDSA --kid shop-2026 --out keys
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------- | ------ | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--alg` | string | `ES256` | algorithm: ES256, ES384, PS256, RS256, EdDSA; RSA keys have 3072 bits | | `--kid` | string | | key ID (default: the RFC 7638 thumbprint) | | `--out` | string | `.` | directory to write the key files in | ## caplo identity sign-assertion [Section titled “caplo identity sign-assertion”](#caplo-identity-sign-assertion) Print a signed caplo-customer+jwt assertion, as a host backend would make one, for local tests. 
```sh
caplo identity sign-assertion [flags]
```
 Signs a caplo-customer+jwt assertion with a private JWK or PEM key and prints it as a compact JWS, as a host’s backend would for a challenge nonce. –key, –iss, –aud, –sub, and –nonce are required. The assertion lives –ttl seconds, at most 300. An –attr value that is a JSON number, boolean, or quoted string keeps its type; any other value is a string. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo identity sign-assertion --key keys/shop-2026.private.jwk.json --iss https://shop.example.com \
  --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> --sub cust-1042 --nonce <nonce>
caplo identity sign-assertion --key key.pem --alg PS256 --kid shop-rsa --iss https://shop.example.com \
  --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> --sub cust-1042 --nonce <nonce> --amr pwd,otp --auth-time now \
  --attr tier=gold --attr orders=12 --act-for cust-7:guardian
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ----------- | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--acr` | string | | authentication context class, mapped to a level by the connection | | `--act-for` | string | | subject the customer acts for, as sub:relationship | | `--alg` | string | | algorithm (default: the JWK’s alg, or the one an EC or Ed25519 PEM key fits) | | `--amr` | stringSlice | | authentication methods, comma separated, e.g. pwd,otp | | `--attr` | stringArray | | verified attribute k=v, repeatable; a JSON number, boolean or “quoted string” keeps its type | | `--aud` | string | | audience: the connection’s urn:caplo:customer-identity:\<id> | | `--auth-time` | string | | when the customer last authenticated: Unix time or now | | `--iss` | string | | issuer: the connection’s issuer | | `--key` | string | | private key file: JWK or PEM | | `--kid` | string | | key ID (default: the JWK’s kid) | | `--name` | string | | display name | | `--nonce` | string | | the challenge nonce | | `--sid` | string | | the host’s session ID, for back-channel logout | | `--sub` | string | | the customer’s stable, opaque ID | | `--ttl` | int64 | `60` | lifetime in seconds, at most 300 | ## caplo identity verify-assertion [Section titled “caplo identity verify-assertion”](#caplo-identity-verify-assertion) Run Identity’s assertion checks locally and print each; challenge state and connection policy are skipped. 
```sh
caplo identity verify-assertion <token|-> [flags]
```
 Runs Identity’s checks on an assertion, given as an argument or read from standard input with -, and prints each as pass, fail, or skip: format, typ, key, signature, issuer and audience, nonce, times, subject and the claim allow-list, and claim values such as amr, sid, jti, profile claims, attributes, and act_for. The nonce is compared only with –nonce. Challenge state and the connection’s policy (acr mapping, allowed profile claims, declared attributes and their types, and delegation relationships) are Identity’s and are skipped. Exits 3 when a check fails. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo identity verify-assertion --jwks keys/shop-2026.public.jwk.json --iss https://shop.example.com \
  --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> "$ASSERTION"
caplo identity sign-assertion ... | caplo identity verify-assertion --jwks https://shop.example.com/jwks.json \
  --iss https://shop.example.com --aud urn:caplo:customer-identity:<id> --nonce <nonce> -
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ----------- | --------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | | `--alg` | stringSlice | `[ES256,ES384,PS256,RS256,EdDSA]` | the connection’s pinned algorithms | | `--aud` | string | | the connection’s audience | | `--iss` | string | | the connection’s issuer | | `--jwks` | string | | JWKS or public JWK: a file or an https URL | | `--nonce` | string | | the challenge nonce (default: skip the comparison) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml |

# caplo knowledge

> Manage knowledge bases and sync their documents from a directory.

Manage knowledge bases and sync their documents from a directory. 
```sh
caplo knowledge
```
 Aliases: `kb` A knowledge base holds documents an agent answers from. Each upload is extracted and waits for review; a person approves it in the console, or caplo knowledge approve does. Publishing snapshots the approved documents into a version, and an agent release pins the newest ready version. Agent files name knowledge bases by name or ID, and so do these commands. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge create "Returns policy" --owner support
caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns --approve --publish --yes
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo knowledge approve`](#caplo-knowledge-approve) * [`caplo knowledge create`](#caplo-knowledge-create) * [`caplo knowledge get`](#caplo-knowledge-get) * [`caplo knowledge list`](#caplo-knowledge-list) * [`caplo knowledge publish`](#caplo-knowledge-publish) * [`caplo knowledge sync`](#caplo-knowledge-sync) ## caplo knowledge approve [Section titled “caplo knowledge approve”](#caplo-knowledge-approve) Wait for extraction and approve documents for the next version, by default every one awaiting review. 
```sh
caplo knowledge approve <kb> [document]... [flags]
```
 Approving a document skips reviewing its extracted text in the console. It waits for documents still being extracted and fails when one fails to extract. It needs agents:publish. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge approve "Returns policy"
caplo knowledge approve "Returns policy" refunds.md exchanges.pdf
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------- | -------- | ------- | ------------------------------- | | `--timeout` | duration | `15m0s` | how long to wait for extraction | ## caplo knowledge create [Section titled “caplo knowledge create”](#caplo-knowledge-create) Create a knowledge base. 
```sh
caplo knowledge create <name> --owner <team> [flags]
```
 Creates a knowledge base. –owner names the team that owns its content. –audience verified_customer limits its content to customers who signed in; the default is public. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge create "Returns policy" --owner support --description "Return and refund rules"
caplo knowledge create "Account help" --owner support --audience verified_customer
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | --------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--audience` | string | | public (default) or verified_customer, for content only signed-in customers may get | | `--description` | string | | what the knowledge base covers | | `--owner` | string | | team that owns the content | ## caplo knowledge get [Section titled “caplo knowledge get”](#caplo-knowledge-get) Show a knowledge base, its documents, and its versions. 
```sh
caplo knowledge get <kb> [flags]
```
 Shows the knowledge base, its newest version, and its documents with their extraction and review state. -o json or yaml includes every version. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge get "Returns policy"
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo knowledge list [Section titled “caplo knowledge list”](#caplo-knowledge-list) List the workspace’s knowledge bases. 
```sh
caplo knowledge list [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo kb list
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo knowledge publish [Section titled “caplo knowledge publish”](#caplo-knowledge-publish) Publish a version from the approved documents and wait for it to index. 
```sh
caplo knowledge publish <kb> [flags]
```
 Publishes a version from the approved documents, unless the newest version holds exactly them, and waits until it is ready. Agent releases published from then on use it. Exits 3 when there are no approved documents or the version does not become ready. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge publish "Returns policy"
caplo knowledge publish "Returns policy" --timeout 30m
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------- | -------- | ------- | ----------------------------- | | `--timeout` | duration | `15m0s` | how long to wait for indexing | ## caplo knowledge sync [Section titled “caplo knowledge sync”](#caplo-knowledge-sync) Make a knowledge base’s documents match a directory, and optionally approve and publish them. 
```sh
caplo knowledge sync <kb> <dir> [flags]
```
 Uploads the directory’s new and changed documents and removes the ones whose file is gone. The older copy of a changed file is removed once the new copy is approved, so a version never lacks the document; a document a published version holds stays in that version. Files are matched by name and content, so an unchanged file is not uploaded again. Documents are named by file name, so two files in the tree may not share one. Supported types, up to 25 MiB each: .txt .md .markdown .html .pdf .docx .png .jpg .jpeg .webp. Other files are skipped, and so are hidden files and directories. –approve waits for extraction and approves the directory’s documents, which skips reviewing their extracted text in the console; it needs agents:publish. –publish then publishes a version from the approved documents and waits for it to index. It asks for confirmation before removing documents; –yes skips it. –dry-run prints the changes only. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns --dry-run
caplo knowledge sync "Returns policy" ./docs/returns --approve --publish --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | -------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--approve` | | | wait for extraction and approve the directory’s documents | | `--dry-run` | | | show the changes without making them | | `--publish` | | | publish a version from the approved documents and wait for it to index | | `--timeout` | duration | `15m0s` | how long –approve and –publish wait for extraction and indexing | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | remove documents without asking |

# caplo mcp

> Connect remote MCP servers and publish their reviewed tools.

Connect remote MCP servers and publish their reviewed tools. 
```sh
caplo mcp
```
 A remote MCP server is an MCP server reachable over HTTPS whose tools agents can call. Add it with caplo mcp add, authorize it with caplo mcp connect when it uses OAuth, review its discovered tools with caplo mcp review, and publish them with caplo mcp publish. Agent files then name them \<server>/\<tool>. A server is named by its name or ID. Secrets are read only from standard input or an environment variable, so they stay out of shell history and process listings, and are never shown again. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp check https://mcp.example.com/mcp
caplo mcp add crm --url https://mcp.example.com/mcp --auth oauth
caplo mcp connect crm --open
caplo mcp review crm --enable search_contacts=read && caplo mcp publish crm
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo mcp add`](#caplo-mcp-add) * [`caplo mcp check`](#caplo-mcp-check) * [`caplo mcp connect`](#caplo-mcp-connect) * [`caplo mcp get`](#caplo-mcp-get) * [`caplo mcp list`](#caplo-mcp-list) * [`caplo mcp publish`](#caplo-mcp-publish) * [`caplo mcp refresh`](#caplo-mcp-refresh) * [`caplo mcp remove`](#caplo-mcp-remove) * [`caplo mcp review`](#caplo-mcp-review) * [`caplo mcp tools`](#caplo-mcp-tools) * [`caplo mcp update`](#caplo-mcp-update) ## caplo mcp add [Section titled “caplo mcp add”](#caplo-mcp-add) Add a remote MCP server; its name prefixes every tool’s model name. 
```sh
caplo mcp add <name> [flags]
```
 Adds a remote MCP server. Its name matches \[a-z]\[a-z0-9-]{1,39}, must differ from every package and server in the workspace, and prefixes every tool’s model name. –auth is none, bearer, header (with –header-name), or oauth. A bearer token or header value comes from –secret-stdin or –secret-env. With –auth oauth the client is registered through a Client ID Metadata Document or dynamic registration, unless –client-id names a pre-registered client; its secret comes from –client-secret-stdin or –client-secret-env, and it must allow the callback URL that caplo mcp get shows. –oauth-grant is authorization_code, authorized with caplo mcp connect, or client_credentials. The command prints the server’s status and what to do next. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp add crm --url https://mcp.example.com/mcp --auth oauth
CRM_TOKEN=... caplo mcp add crm --url https://mcp.example.com/mcp --auth bearer --secret-env CRM_TOKEN
caplo mcp add erp --url https://erp.example.com/mcp --auth header --header-name X-Api-Key --secret-stdin
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------- | ----------- | -------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--auth` | string | | authentication: none, bearer, header, or oauth | | `--client-id` | string | | pre-registered OAuth client ID (default: a Client ID Metadata Document or dynamic registration) | | `--client-secret-env` | string | | read the pre-registered client’s secret from this environment variable | | `--client-secret-stdin` | | | read the pre-registered client’s secret from stdin | | `--description` | string | | what the server is for | | `--display-name` | string | | name shown in the console (default: the name) | | `--header-name` | string | | credential header of –auth header | | `--oauth-grant` | string | `authorization_code` | OAuth grant: authorization_code or client_credentials; update keeps the current one unless set | | `--scope` | stringArray | | OAuth scope to request, repeatable (default: the server’s) | | `--secret-env` | string | | read the bearer token or header value from this environment variable | | `--secret-stdin` | | | read the bearer token or header value from stdin (a hidden prompt on a terminal) | | `--token-auth-method` | string | | token endpoint authentication: none, client_secret_basic, or client_secret_post | | `--transport` | string | | auto, streamable_http, or sse (default auto) | | `--url` | string | | HTTPS endpoint of the server | ## caplo mcp check [Section titled “caplo mcp check”](#caplo-mcp-check) Check a remote MCP server without saving it: transport, protocol, tools, and OAuth discovery. 
```sh
caplo mcp check <url> [flags]
```
 Has Caplo probe a server URL without saving anything: whether it is reachable, its transport, protocol, and tool count, and, when it needs authorization, what OAuth discovery found. A bearer token or header value for the check is read from standard input and not stored. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp check https://mcp.example.com/mcp
printf %s "$CRM_TOKEN" | caplo mcp check https://mcp.example.com/mcp --bearer-stdin
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `--bearer-stdin` | | | check with a bearer token read from stdin; it is not stored | | `--header` | string | | check with a credential in this header, its value read with –value-stdin | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | | `--transport` | string | | auto, streamable_http, or sse (default auto) | | `--value-stdin` | | | read the –header value from stdin; it is not stored | ## caplo mcp connect [Section titled “caplo mcp connect”](#caplo-mcp-connect) Authorize a remote MCP server that uses OAuth. 
```sh
caplo mcp connect <name|id> [flags]
```
 Authorize a remote MCP server that uses OAuth. For the authorization code grant, open the printed URL in a browser signed in to the Caplo console: the authorization server redirects to the console, which completes the authorization. The command waits for it unless –wait is 0, retrying network and 5xx failures while it checks. The client credentials grant completes at once. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp connect crm --open
caplo mcp connect crm --wait 0
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | -------- | -------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | | `--open` | | | open the authorization URL in the default browser | | `--wait` | duration | `10m0s` | how long to wait for the authorization; 0 returns at once | ## caplo mcp get [Section titled “caplo mcp get”](#caplo-mcp-get) Show a remote MCP server: status, authentication, last error, and its discovered tools. 
```sh
caplo mcp get <name|id> [flags]
```
 Shows the server’s status, transport and protocol, authentication without secrets, last error, published version, and its discovered tools with their review, change, and published state. For OAuth it shows the callback URL a pre-registered client must allow. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp get crm
caplo mcp get crm -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo mcp list [Section titled “caplo mcp list”](#caplo-mcp-list) List the workspace’s remote MCP servers. 
```sh
caplo mcp list [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp list
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo mcp publish [Section titled “caplo mcp publish”](#caplo-mcp-publish) Pin the enabled tools of the latest tool list as a new version that agents can use. 
```sh
caplo mcp publish <name|id>
```
 Pins the enabled tools of the latest tool list, as reviewed, as a new version. It fails when the tool list changed since the review; check it with caplo mcp tools, review it again, and publish. Agent files name the tools \<server>/\<tool>. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp publish crm
```
 ## caplo mcp refresh [Section titled “caplo mcp refresh”](#caplo-mcp-refresh) Test a remote MCP server and refresh its tool list against the published version. 
```sh
caplo mcp refresh <name|id>
```
 Connects to the server, lists its tools again, and marks each new, changed, or removed against the published version. Published tools that changed on the server are blocked until they are reviewed and published again. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp refresh crm
```
 ## caplo mcp remove [Section titled “caplo mcp remove”](#caplo-mcp-remove) Remove a remote MCP server: its published tools stop working and its credentials are deleted. 
```sh
caplo mcp remove <name|id> [flags]
```
 Removes a remote MCP server. Its published tools stop working for every agent that uses them, its stored credentials are deleted, and its name stays reserved. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp remove crm --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ----- | ------- | --------------------------- | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | do not ask for confirmation | ## caplo mcp review [Section titled “caplo mcp review”](#caplo-mcp-review) Enable, classify, and configure discovered tools; nothing reaches agents until caplo mcp publish. 
```sh
caplo mcp review <name|id> [flags]
```
 Enable, classify, and configure discovered tools by their remote name. –enable without an effect keeps the tool’s effect, or takes the one the server’s hints suggest. Write tools ask the customer to confirm unless –confirmation \<tool>=none. –timeout takes 1 to 30 seconds. Tools not named keep their review. Nothing reaches agents until caplo mcp publish. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp review crm --enable search_contacts=read --enable create_note=write
caplo mcp review crm --confirmation create_note=none --timeout create_note=20
caplo mcp review crm --disable delete_contact
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ----------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | | `--confirmation` | stringArray | | for a write tool, \<remote tool>=required\|none, repeatable | | `--disable` | stringArray | | disable a tool by its remote name, repeatable | | `--enable` | stringArray | | enable a tool, as \<remote tool>\[=read\|write], repeatable | | `--timeout` | stringArray | | \<remote tool>=\<seconds from 1 to 30>, repeatable | ## caplo mcp tools [Section titled “caplo mcp tools”](#caplo-mcp-tools) List a remote MCP server’s discovered tools with their review and publish state. 
```sh
caplo mcp tools <name|id> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp tools crm
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo mcp update [Section titled “caplo mcp update”](#caplo-mcp-update) Change a remote MCP server’s endpoint, transport, or authentication. 
```sh
caplo mcp update <name|id> [flags]
```
 Change a remote MCP server’s endpoint, transport, or authentication. Authentication flags change the current setup; a stored secret is kept unless a new one is given. A new URL or authentication setup discards stored OAuth tokens and clients, so the server must be authorized again with caplo mcp connect. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo mcp update crm --url https://mcp.example.com/v2/mcp
caplo mcp update crm --scope contacts.read --scope contacts.write
printf %s "$NEW_TOKEN" | caplo mcp update erp --secret-stdin
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------- | ----------- | -------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--auth` | string | | authentication: none, bearer, header, or oauth | | `--client-id` | string | | pre-registered OAuth client ID (default: a Client ID Metadata Document or dynamic registration) | | `--client-secret-env` | string | | read the pre-registered client’s secret from this environment variable | | `--client-secret-stdin` | | | read the pre-registered client’s secret from stdin | | `--description` | string | | what the server is for | | `--display-name` | string | | name shown in the console | | `--header-name` | string | | credential header of –auth header | | `--oauth-grant` | string | `authorization_code` | OAuth grant: authorization_code or client_credentials; update keeps the current one unless set | | `--scope` | stringArray | | OAuth scope to request, repeatable (default: the server’s) | | `--secret-env` | string | | read the bearer token or header value from this environment variable | | `--secret-stdin` | | | read the bearer token or header value from stdin (a hidden prompt on a terminal) | | `--token-auth-method` | string | | token endpoint authentication: none, client_secret_basic, or client_secret_post | | `--transport` | string | | auto, streamable_http, or sse | | `--url` | string | | HTTPS endpoint of the server |

# Project commands

> caplo init, validate, diff, push, test, eval, publish and pull: commands that work on a project folder.

These commands work on the project around the current directory, or the one `-C` names. See [Projects](/cli/projects/). ## caplo diff [Section titled “caplo diff”](#caplo-diff) Show how the project differs from the workspace drafts. 
```sh
caplo diff [agent/<name> | tool/<name>]... [flags]
```
 Compares each selected package’s files with its workspace draft, and each agent file and its evals with the agent’s draft and eval suite. It prints the plan, one line per resource, and a unified diff per changed agent; a new agent diffs against nothing. It changes nothing. Selectors are agent/\<file key or name>, where a file key wins over a name, and tool/\<package>. An agent brings its project sub-agents and the project packages they use. Without selectors the command acts on the whole project. With -o json or yaml the plan is one document: the project, workspace, environment, whether anything changes, and per resource its steps (create, update, save-evals, build, test, evaluate, publish, archive, deploy; none when unchanged), summary, changed files, reasons, current and next versions, and diff. A pipeline can post it on a pull request or check it with a policy. push and publish print it only with –dry-run. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo diff
caplo diff agent/concierge tool/order-desk
caplo diff --env prod -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--env`, `-e` | string | | environment from caplo.yaml: its workspace, agent fields, channels, and promotion (default: $CAPLO_ENV) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo eval [Section titled “caplo eval”](#caplo-eval) Push the selection, then run each selected agent’s eval cases against its draft. 
```sh
caplo eval [agent/<name> | tool/<name>]... [flags]
```
 Pushes the selection like caplo push, then runs each selected agent’s eval cases against its saved draft in isolated test conversations and prints every case. A passing run for the same draft and suite is reused. Exits 3 when a case fails or an agent has no eval cases. An agent file’s evals list cases with the console’s fields: name, input, desiredOutcome, uncertainty, prohibitedAction, answerContains, answerExcludes, subAgent, avoidSubAgent, routedBy, and tool. A push matches them to the suite’s cases by name and replaces the agent’s eval suite with them; without an evals key the suite is left alone. In every run a grader model also judges each case’s desiredOutcome. A failed case prints the grader’s rationale, and a case the grader could not judge reads desired outcome not graded and does not pass. routedBy (fast, model, or any) checks how an agent with fast routing routed the case. Selectors are agent/\<file key or name>, where a file key wins over a name, and tool/\<package>. An agent brings its project sub-agents and the project packages they use. Without selectors the command acts on the whole project. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo eval
caplo eval agent/concierge
caplo eval --env staging
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--env`, `-e` | string | | environment from caplo.yaml: its workspace, agent fields, channels, and promotion (default: $CAPLO_ENV) | ## caplo init [Section titled “caplo init”](#caplo-init) Create a project: caplo.yaml, agent files, and tool packages. 
```sh
caplo init [dir] [flags]
```
 Creates a project in dir (default: the current directory), which must be missing or empty. The blank template has one agent; summit is a sample shop with a concierge agent, three sub-agents, and two tool packages. Each tool package gets package.json and tsconfig.json from the @caplo/tools templates; run npm install in it before testing it. A project is a folder with caplo.yaml: apiVersion v1, kind Project, a name, and the agents and tools globs (default agents/*.yaml and tools/*). Project commands find it by walking up from the current directory or –dir. Each agent file is an agent YAML document with apiVersion v2.0.0, the format of the console’s Export YAML; v1 files are refused. The file name without .yaml is the agent’s key, which links and selectors use. Each tool package is a directory with its own caplo.yaml, as caplo tool init makes it. In an agent file: 
```sh
subAgents link a project agent with local: <file key>, or any workspace agent with agentId. With release:
latest, or no release, the link pins the child's newest release that is not revoked whenever the parent is
pushed or published, so a child's new release publishes its parents again. A version pins that release. The
child runs with its own release's tools and knowledge.
```
 
```sh
tools name <package>/<tool> for a project package, <server>/<tool> for a published tool of a workspace remote
MCP server (caplo mcp), or <package ID>/<tool>; a package alone selects all its tools. A name that is no
project package is looked up as a remote MCP server when the project is diffed, pushed, or published.
```
 
```sh
knowledgeBases name a workspace knowledge base by name or ID. A name resolves in the workspace the command acts
on, so one file works in every environment.
```
 
```sh
evals are the agent's eval cases; see caplo eval.
```
 Workspace agents and packages are matched by name: a missing one is created and an existing one updated. Nothing is deleted; caplo publish –prune archives agents the project no longer defines. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo init shop --template summit
cd shop && (cd tools/order-desk && npm install) && (cd tools/store-stock && npm install)
caplo init --name support
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------ | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------ | | `--name` | string | | project name (default: the directory name) | | `--template`, `-t` | string | `blank` | project template: blank or summit | ## caplo publish [Section titled “caplo publish”](#caplo-publish) Validate, push, test, evaluate, and publish bottom up: tools, then child agents, then parents. 
```sh
caplo publish [agent/<name> | tool/<name>]... [flags]
```
 Publishes the selection, or the whole project, bottom up. Changed packages are pushed, built, tested with their fixtures on the workspace’s test runner, and published first. Then agents publish with children before parents: each draft is saved pinned to its children’s new releases, its eval cases run, and it is published. An agent publishes when its file changed, its saved draft is unpublished, or a package or release: latest sub-agent it uses publishes. A release does not pin eval cases, so an agent whose cases alone changed since its release is evaluated and keeps the release. A published agent needs at least one eval case, and every case must pass. It validates the project, prints the plan and diffs, and asks before publishing; –yes skips the question. Without a terminal, and on a project’s first publish to a workspace, it needs –yes. –dry-run prints the plan only. –prune archives the agents an earlier publish of this project created that it no longer defines; it takes no selectors. Selectors are agent/\<file key or name>, where a file key wins over a name, and tool/\<package>. An agent brings its project sub-agents and the project packages they use. Without selectors the command acts on the whole project. Selecting an agent also selects the project agents that link it at release: latest, since its new release publishes them again. Environments. caplo.yaml may define environments, each a workspace the project publishes to: 
```sh
environments:
  staging:
    workspace: 5b1e9c2a-...        # an ID, or a name when you log in rather than use an API key
    apiKeyEnv: CAPLO_STAGING_KEY   # optional: the variable with this environment's API key
  prod:
    workspace: 0d6a3c87-...
    apiKeyEnv: CAPLO_PROD_KEY
    promoteFrom: staging           # publish only what staging released from the same source
    agents:                        # agent fields by file key, merged over the agent file
      front: {model: {name: claude-sonnet-4-5}}
    channels:                      # channels a publish points at the agent's newest release
      front: [Website, twilio/Support line]
```
 –env, or CAPLO_ENV, selects one for validate, diff, push, eval, and publish: its workspace (a different –workspace or CAPLO_WORKSPACE is refused), its API key when its apiKeyEnv variable is set (used instead of CAPLO_API_KEY), and its agent fields, which merge like a JSON merge patch: mappings merge, null removes a key, and anything else replaces. Each release’s note names the project and the agent’s source digest: its file as written, the files of the project packages it uses, and its project sub-agents. With promoteFrom, the publish exits 3 unless every agent it publishes has a release in that environment, not revoked, with the same digest. That environment is read with its own apiKeyEnv key, else the login session; a publish that uses an API key needs the other environment’s key, with agents:read. After publishing, it deploys the environment’s channels that do not serve their agent’s newest release, which needs channels:write; the plan lists them as channel/\<kind>/\<name>. With -o json or yaml the plan is one document: the project, workspace, environment, whether anything changes, and per resource its steps (create, update, save-evals, build, test, evaluate, publish, archive, deploy; none when unchanged), summary, changed files, reasons, current and next versions, and diff. A pipeline can post it on a pull request or check it with a policy. push and publish print it only with –dry-run. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo publish
caplo publish agent/orders --dry-run
caplo publish --env prod --yes
caplo publish --prune
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--dry-run` | | | show the plan and diffs without publishing | | `--env`, `-e` | string | | environment from caplo.yaml: its workspace, agent fields, channels, and promotion (default: $CAPLO_ENV) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | | `--prune` | | | archive agents an earlier publish of this project created that it no longer defines | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | publish without asking | ## caplo pull [Section titled “caplo pull”](#caplo-pull) Write a workspace agent, its sub-agents, and their packages into a project. 
```sh
caplo pull agent/<name> [dir] [flags]
```
 Writes a workspace agent, named by name or ID, and every sub-agent it links, with their drafts and eval cases, as agent files of a project. The project is the one at dir; a dir without caplo.yaml becomes a new project unless it is inside another one. Without dir it is the project above –dir, or a new one in –dir. Agents the project defines are written to their own files, others to new files in the directory the agents glob names. Packages the agents use that the project lacks are pulled from their workspace drafts into the directory the tools glob names; remote MCP server tools stay \<server>/\<tool>. Links name the release the draft pins, since a draft does not record release: latest. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo pull "agent/Shop Concierge"
caplo pull agent/concierge ./shop
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | ## caplo push [Section titled “caplo push”](#caplo-push) Create or update the workspace drafts, children first, so you can live test them. 
```sh
caplo push [agent/<name> | tool/<name>]... [flags]
```
 Prints the plan, then uploads and builds each changed package as its workspace draft, and saves each agent file as its agent’s draft and its evals as the agent’s eval suite, children before parents. A missing package or agent is created. Nothing is published. A live test runs linked sub-agents from their releases, so push names the project sub-agents that have no release yet and the caplo publish that releases them. –dry-run prints the plan and diffs without pushing. Selectors are agent/\<file key or name>, where a file key wins over a name, and tool/\<package>. An agent brings its project sub-agents and the project packages they use. Without selectors the command acts on the whole project. With -o json or yaml the plan is one document: the project, workspace, environment, whether anything changes, and per resource its steps (create, update, save-evals, build, test, evaluate, publish, archive, deploy; none when unchanged), summary, changed files, reasons, current and next versions, and diff. A pipeline can post it on a pull request or check it with a policy. push and publish print it only with –dry-run. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo push
caplo push agent/concierge
caplo push --dry-run -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--dry-run` | | | show the changes and diffs without pushing | | `--env`, `-e` | string | | environment from caplo.yaml: its workspace, agent fields, channels, and promotion (default: $CAPLO_ENV) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo test [Section titled “caplo test”](#caplo-test) Run the selected tool packages’ fixtures locally, like caplo tool test. 
```sh
caplo test [agent/<name> | tool/<name>]... [flags]
```
 Runs caplo tool test in each selected package: it bundles the package like the builder and runs its fixtures in local Node 24. It calls no workspace. Exits 3 when a fixture fails. Selectors are agent/\<file key or name>, where a file key wins over a name, and tool/\<package>. An agent brings its project sub-agents and the project packages they use. Without selectors the command acts on the whole project. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo test
caplo test tool/order-desk -v
caplo test agent/orders
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--verbose`, `-v` | | | print log lines for passing fixtures too | ## caplo validate [Section titled “caplo validate”](#caplo-validate) Check the project offline: schemas, references, link cycles, and the depth limit. 
```sh
caplo validate [flags]
```
 Checks the project without calling the workspace: caplo.yaml and each package’s caplo.yaml, every agent file against the agent schema, unique agent names and file keys, references to project agents and packages, the tools an mcp package declares, sub-agent link cycles, and each agent’s depth limit: at most policy.maxDepth (1 to 4, default 3) sub-agent links below it. Tools of remote MCP servers are checked when the project is diffed, pushed, or published. An agent with routing mode fast needs at least one sub-agent and doNotUseWhen on every link. When its file lists evals, each sub-agent also needs two cases that set routedBy and name it in subAgent. Every environment in caplo.yaml is checked: the agent keys it names and its agent fields merged over each file. With –env, the agents are checked as that environment publishes them. Problems are printed and the command exits 3. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo validate
caplo validate --env prod
caplo validate -C ~/src/shop
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | start looking for the project’s caplo.yaml here | | `--env`, `-e` | string | | environment from caplo.yaml: its workspace, agent fields, channels, and promotion (default: $CAPLO_ENV) |

# caplo secret

> Manage workspace secrets that package secrets bind to.

Manage workspace secrets that package secrets bind to. 
```sh
caplo secret
```
 A workspace secret holds one value for many packages: each package secret bound to it with caplo tool secret bind receives its value. Rotating it with caplo secret set changes the value for every bound package within a minute. Values are never shown again. Names match \[a-z]\[a-z0-9-]{1,63}. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo secret set orders-api-key
caplo secret usage orders-api-key
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo secret delete`](#caplo-secret-delete) * [`caplo secret list`](#caplo-secret-list) * [`caplo secret set`](#caplo-secret-set) * [`caplo secret usage`](#caplo-secret-usage) ## caplo secret delete [Section titled “caplo secret delete”](#caplo-secret-delete) Delete a workspace secret that no package secret binds. 
```sh
caplo secret delete <name> [flags]
```
 Deletes a workspace secret. A secret that package secrets are bound to is not deleted; the command lists the bindings to remove first with caplo tool secret unbind. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo secret delete orders-api-key --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ----- | ------- | --------------------------- | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | do not ask for confirmation | ## caplo secret list [Section titled “caplo secret list”](#caplo-secret-list) List the workspace secrets and how many package secrets bind each. 
```sh
caplo secret list [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo secret list
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo secret set [Section titled “caplo secret set”](#caplo-secret-set) Create a workspace secret or rotate its value, from a hidden prompt or from stdin when it is not a terminal. 
```sh
caplo secret set <name> [flags]
```
 Creates a workspace secret, or rotates the value of an existing one for every package secret bound to it. The value comes from a hidden prompt, or from standard input without its final newline when that is not a terminal, and is 1 to 8192 bytes. –description sets what it is for; without it, a rotation keeps the current description. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo secret set orders-api-key --description "Orders API key"
printf %s "$ORDERS_API_KEY" | caplo secret set orders-api-key
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | --------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | | `--description` | string | | what the secret is for (default: keep the current one) | ## caplo secret usage [Section titled “caplo secret usage”](#caplo-secret-usage) List the package secrets bound to a workspace secret. 
```sh
caplo secret usage <name> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo secret usage orders-api-key
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml |

# caplo tool

> Build, test, publish, and run tool packages.

Build, test, publish, and run tool packages. 
```sh
caplo tool
```
 A tool package is a TypeScript package of tools that agents call, of type function or mcp. Its caplo.yaml names it and declares its tools’ policy, egress, secrets, and fixtures. caplo tool init creates one, caplo tool test runs its fixtures locally, caplo tool push uploads and builds its workspace draft, and caplo tool publish makes a build a version that agents can use. Writing tools is covered at <https://docs.caplo.si/tools/>. Commands that act on a workspace package take its name, or read caplo.yaml in –dir (default: the current directory). Inside a project, caplo push and caplo publish handle its packages. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool init orders --type mcp
cd orders && npm install
caplo tool test
caplo tool push && caplo tool publish
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo tool archive`](#caplo-tool-archive) * [`caplo tool build`](#caplo-tool-build) * [`caplo tool delete`](#caplo-tool-delete) * [`caplo tool dev`](#caplo-tool-dev) * [`caplo tool get`](#caplo-tool-get) * [`caplo tool init`](#caplo-tool-init) * [`caplo tool list`](#caplo-tool-list) * [`caplo tool logs`](#caplo-tool-logs) * [`caplo tool publish`](#caplo-tool-publish) * [`caplo tool pull`](#caplo-tool-pull) * [`caplo tool push`](#caplo-tool-push) * [`caplo tool restore`](#caplo-tool-restore) * [`caplo tool secret`](#caplo-tool-secret) * [`caplo tool test`](#caplo-tool-test) * [`caplo tool version`](#caplo-tool-version) ## caplo tool archive [Section titled “caplo tool archive”](#caplo-tool-archive) Archive a package: no more pushes, builds, or publishes; its versions stay live until revoked. 
```sh
caplo tool archive [package] [flags]
```
 Archives a package: it can no longer be pushed, built, or published. Its published versions stay live for the agents that use them until they are revoked with caplo tool version revoke. caplo tool restore undoes it. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool archive orders --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | --------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | do not ask for confirmation | ## caplo tool build [Section titled “caplo tool build”](#caplo-tool-build) Show, follow, and cancel builds. 
```sh
caplo tool build
```
 A build is named by its ID; \[package] defaults to the one caplo.yaml in –dir names. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool build list orders
caplo tool build logs orders 5e0c8a3d-7b21-4f6e-8c90-3a1d2b4c5e6f
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo tool build cancel`](#caplo-tool-build-cancel) * [`caplo tool build get`](#caplo-tool-build-get) * [`caplo tool build list`](#caplo-tool-build-list) * [`caplo tool build logs`](#caplo-tool-build-logs) ## caplo tool build cancel [Section titled “caplo tool build cancel”](#caplo-tool-build-cancel) Cancel a queued or running build. 
```sh
caplo tool build cancel [package] <build> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool build cancel orders 5e0c8a3d-7b21-4f6e-8c90-3a1d2b4c5e6f
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | ## caplo tool build get [Section titled “caplo tool build get”](#caplo-tool-build-get) Show a build: status, bundle, tools, and diagnostics. 
```sh
caplo tool build get [package] <build> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool build get orders 5e0c8a3d-7b21-4f6e-8c90-3a1d2b4c5e6f
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo tool build list [Section titled “caplo tool build list”](#caplo-tool-build-list) List a package’s builds, newest first. 
```sh
caplo tool build list [package] [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool build list orders
caplo tool build list orders --status failed -n 5
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------ | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | | `--limit`, `-n` | int | `20` | most builds to show (at most 100) | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | | `--status` | string | | only builds with this status, for example failed | ## caplo tool build logs [Section titled “caplo tool build logs”](#caplo-tool-build-logs) Print a build’s log, following it until the build finishes. 
```sh
caplo tool build logs [package] <build> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool build logs orders 5e0c8a3d-7b21-4f6e-8c90-3a1d2b4c5e6f
```

 Deletes a package that has never published a version, with its draft and builds. A package with versions can only be archived. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples**

```sh
caplo tool dev [flags]
```
 Runs the package’s src/stdio.ts with Node 24 in MCP Inspector, started with npx, to call its tools by hand. The secrets caplo.yaml declares are read from environment variables of the same name. With –no-inspector the server speaks MCP on this process’s standard input and output, so another MCP client can start it. **Examples**

 Creates a package in directory, which must be missing or empty, from the function or mcp template of the @caplo/tools SDK, which is embedded in the CLI at build time. –type is required.
```sh
caplo tool logs [package] [flags]
```
 Prints the package’s recent live invocations with each tool’s status, timing, error, egress denials, and log lines. -n sets how many come first, and -f keeps streaming new ones. **Examples**

```sh
caplo tool publish [package] [flags]
```
 Publishes the package’s latest succeeded build, or the build –build names, as its next version. An agent pins its tools’ versions when it is published, so agents use the new version from their next release. **Examples**

 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------------- | | `--build` | string | | build ID to publish | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | ## caplo tool pull [Section titled “caplo tool pull”](#caplo-tool-pull) Download the draft, or the exact source of a published version, to run it locally. 
```sh
caplo tool pull <package>[@version] [flags]
```
 Writes the package’s draft, or with @version the exact source of the build that version was published from, into an empty or new directory (default: ./\<package>). Every file is downloaded and checked against its listed SHA-256 before anything is written, so all files or none are written. Listed paths outside the package are refused, and files under .git or node_modules are left out. Run the package locally with caplo tool dev –no-inspector. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool pull orders
caplo tool pull orders@3 --dir /tmp/orders-v3
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | | directory to write, which must be empty or missing (default: ./\<package>) | ## caplo tool push [Section titled “caplo tool push”](#caplo-tool-push) Upload the package as the workspace draft, build it, and stream the build log. 
```sh
caplo tool push [flags]
```
 Uploads the package directory as its workspace draft, creating the package on its first push, then builds the draft and streams the build log. node_modules, .git, and what git ignores are left out, by the .gitignore files from the enclosing git repository, else the project, down through the package; symlinks and other special files are refused. The builder installs dependencies from the lockfile. Publish the build with caplo tool publish. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool push
caplo tool push --dir tools/orders
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | ## caplo tool restore [Section titled “caplo tool restore”](#caplo-tool-restore) Restore an archived package. 
```sh

```
 Restores an archived package, so it can be pushed, built, and published again. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool restore orders
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | ## caplo tool secret [Section titled “caplo tool secret”](#caplo-tool-secret) Manage the secret values live calls receive. 
```sh
caplo tool secret
```
 A package’s live calls receive the secrets its caplo.yaml declares, by name. Each one is the package’s own value, set with caplo tool secret set, or is bound to a workspace secret that several packages can share (caplo secret). Values are never shown again, and live calls use a change within a minute. The commands act on the package -p names, else the one caplo.yaml in –dir names. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool secret list orders
caplo tool secret bind ORDERS_API_KEY orders-api-key -p orders
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo tool secret bind`](#caplo-tool-secret-bind) * [`caplo tool secret list`](#caplo-tool-secret-list) * [`caplo tool secret set`](#caplo-tool-secret-set) * [`caplo tool secret unbind`](#caplo-tool-secret-unbind) * [`caplo tool secret unset`](#caplo-tool-secret-unset) ## caplo tool secret bind [Section titled “caplo tool secret bind”](#caplo-tool-secret-bind) Fill a secret from a workspace secret, replacing the package’s own value. 
```sh
caplo tool secret bind <NAME> <workspace-secret> [flags]
```
 **Examples** 

```sh
caplo tool secret list [package] [flags]
```
 Lists the names the package’s latest active version declares, and any other name that is set or bound, with the source of each value: workspace: \<secret>, package, or none. **Examples**

 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo tool secret set [Section titled “caplo tool secret set”](#caplo-tool-secret-set) Set a secret from a hidden prompt, or from stdin when it is not a terminal. 
```sh
caplo tool secret set <NAME> [flags]
```
 Sets the package’s own value of a secret for live calls. The value comes from a hidden prompt, or from standard input without its final newline when that is not a terminal, and is 1 to 8192 bytes. Names match \[A-Z]\[A-Z0-9\_]{0,63}. A name bound to a workspace secret must be unbound first. **Examples**
