 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------- | ------ | ------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | | `--dir`, `-C` | string | `.` | package directory | | `--package`, `-p` | string | | package name (default: the one caplo.yaml in –dir names) | ## caplo tool secret unbind [Section titled “caplo tool secret unbind”](#caplo-tool-secret-unbind) Remove a secret’s workspace secret binding; live calls that need it fail until it is bound or set.

```sh
caplo tool secret unbind <NAME> [flags]
```
 Removes the binding and leaves the secret unset. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples**

 Removes the package’s own value of a secret. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples**

```sh
caplo tool test [flags]
```
 Bundles the package with the builder’s esbuild version, options, and rejection rules, loads it through the @caplo/tools host harness from an empty directory with no environment, checks the tool policy in caplo.yaml, and runs the fixtures (tests/\*.json unless caplo.yaml sets tests) in local Node 24. It prints the capabilities the builder records from the installed @caplo/tools release beside the ones the running server advertises; the runner requires both. Run npm install first. Local tests run on macOS and Linux; on Windows, use WSL. The server build stays authoritative, since local dependencies may differ from the lockfile. –remote instead pushes and builds the draft like caplo tool push and runs the fixtures on the workspace’s test runner. It exits 3 when a fixture fails. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo tool test
caplo tool test -v
caplo tool test --remote
```

# caplo workspace

> List, show, create, and select workspaces.

List, show, create, and select workspaces. 
```sh
caplo workspace
```
 Commands act on the workspace that –workspace or CAPLO_WORKSPACE names, by ID or exact name, else on the one selected with caplo workspace use. With an API key the workspace must be named by ID, since a key cannot list workspaces. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace list
caplo workspace use "Shop staging"
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo workspace api-client`](#caplo-workspace-api-client) * [`caplo workspace create`](#caplo-workspace-create) * [`caplo workspace get`](#caplo-workspace-get) * [`caplo workspace list`](#caplo-workspace-list) * [`caplo workspace use`](#caplo-workspace-use) ## caplo workspace api-client [Section titled “caplo workspace api-client”](#caplo-workspace-api-client) Manage API clients and their keys for CI and automation. 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client
```
 An API client authenticates CI and automation without a person’s login. Set CAPLO_API_KEY to one of its keys and CAPLO_WORKSPACE (or –workspace, or a project environment’s workspace) to its workspace ID, and caplo commands act as the client with its scopes, at CAPLO_API_URL, else the configured API, else production. caplo auth status shows the client and its scopes. A key cannot list workspaces, so a workspace named by name is refused. Scopes: agents:read, agents:write, agents:publish tools:read, tools:write, tools:publish channels:read, channels:write conversations:read, usage:read endpoints:read, endpoints:write A write or publish scope includes its read scope, and a member can grant only the actions their own role holds. A CI publish needs agents:write and agents:publish, and tools:write and tools:publish when the project has packages. An environment that deploys channels needs channels:write, which a publish checks before it starts. Channel commands also read agents, and knowledge commands use the agent scopes. A project environment can name its own key variable with apiKeyEnv; see caplo publish. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client create ci --scope agents:write,agents:publish,tools:write,tools:publish
CAPLO_API_KEY=... CAPLO_WORKSPACE=5b1e9c2a-... caplo publish --yes
```
 **Commands** * [`caplo workspace api-client create`](#caplo-workspace-api-client-create) * [`caplo workspace api-client list`](#caplo-workspace-api-client-list) * [`caplo workspace api-client revoke`](#caplo-workspace-api-client-revoke) * [`caplo workspace api-client rotate`](#caplo-workspace-api-client-rotate) ## caplo workspace api-client create [Section titled “caplo workspace api-client create”](#caplo-workspace-api-client-create) Create an API client and print its first key. 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client create <name> --scope <scope>... [flags]
```
 Creates an API client with the scopes and prints its first key on standard output, once; store it at once, for example as the CI secret CAPLO_API_KEY. Scopes: agents:read, agents:write, agents:publish tools:read, tools:write, tools:publish channels:read, channels:write conversations:read, usage:read endpoints:read, endpoints:write **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client create ci --scope agents:write --scope agents:publish
caplo workspace api-client create reports --scope conversations:read,usage:read
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ----------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | | `--scope` | stringSlice | | scope to grant; repeat or separate with commas | ## caplo workspace api-client list [Section titled “caplo workspace api-client list”](#caplo-workspace-api-client-list) List the workspace’s API clients and their keys. 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client list [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client list
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo workspace api-client revoke [Section titled “caplo workspace api-client revoke”](#caplo-workspace-api-client-revoke) Revoke an API client’s active keys, or only the ones –key names. 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client revoke <client> [flags]
```
 Revokes the API client’s active keys: every one, the ones –key names, or all but the one –except names. Keys are named by ID or prefix. Anything using a revoked key stops working. It asks first; –yes skips the question. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client revoke ci --except 9d3e1f20-4b5a-4c6d-8e7f-0a1b2c3d4e5f
caplo workspace api-client revoke ci --key caplo_sk_3f9a6b1c --yes
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ------------- | ----------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--except` | string | | revoke every active key but this one (ID or prefix), after a rotation | | `--key` | stringSlice | | key ID or prefix to revoke (default: every active key) | | `--yes`, `-y` | | | revoke without asking | ## caplo workspace api-client rotate [Section titled “caplo workspace api-client rotate”](#caplo-workspace-api-client-rotate) Add a new key to an API client and print it; its other keys stay active until revoked. 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client rotate <client> [flags]
```
 Adds a key to the API client and prints it on standard output, once. The client’s other keys stay active, so CI keeps working while you replace the key; then revoke the old ones with caplo workspace api-client revoke \<client> –except \<new key>. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace api-client rotate ci
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo workspace create [Section titled “caplo workspace create”](#caplo-workspace-create) Create a workspace you own and select it. 
```sh
caplo workspace create <name>
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace create "Shop staging"
```
 ## caplo workspace get [Section titled “caplo workspace get”](#caplo-workspace-get) Show a workspace, by default the selected one. 
```sh
caplo workspace get [name or ID] [flags]
```
 **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace get
caplo workspace get "Shop staging" -o json
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo workspace list [Section titled “caplo workspace list”](#caplo-workspace-list) List your workspaces. 
```sh
caplo workspace list [flags]
```
 Lists the workspaces you belong to with your role; \* marks the selected one. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace list
```
 **Flags** | Flag | Value | Default | Description | | ---------------- | ------ | ------- | ----------------------------------- | | `--output`, `-o` | string | `table` | output format: table, json, or yaml | ## caplo workspace use [Section titled “caplo workspace use”](#caplo-workspace-use) Select the workspace for later commands. 
```sh
caplo workspace use <name or ID>
```
 Saves the workspace, by ID or exact name, as the one later commands act on. –workspace or CAPLO_WORKSPACE overrides it for one command. **Examples** 
```sh
caplo workspace use "Shop staging"
```

# Tool fixtures

> Every field of a tool test fixture, generated from the schema caplo tool test validates against.

For validation and completion in your editor, add this first line to the file (YAML language server, used by VS Code and others): 
```yaml
# yaml-language-server: $schema=https://docs.caplo.si/schemas/fixture.schema.json
```
 The schema is also published at [/schemas/fixture.schema.json](/schemas/fixture.schema.json). ## Fields [Section titled “Fields”](#fields) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ----------- | ------ | -------- | --------------------------------- | | `name` | string | | At most 200 characters. | | `tool` | string | Yes | Pattern `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,47}$`. | | `arguments` | object | Yes | | | `secrets` | object | | Test values for declared secrets. | | `expect` | object | Yes | See [`expect`](#expect). | Other fields are refused. ### `expect` [Section titled “expect”](#expect) | Field | Type | Required | Description | | ------------------- | ------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `isError` | boolean | | | | `structuredContent` | object | | Every listed field must equal the result’s field; extra result fields are allowed. | | `textIncludes` | string | | Substring of the joined text content. | Other fields are refused.

# @caplo/tools

> API reference for @caplo/tools, generated from the published package.

## Functions [Section titled “Functions”](#functions) ### applied() [Section titled “applied()”](#applied) 
```ts
function applied(result): Applied;
```
 Reports that the provider applied the change, with its result. A status handler must return it to confirm a change; a tool handler may return the result directly. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters) | Parameter | Type | | --------- | --------- | | `result` | `unknown` | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns) [`Applied`](#applied) *** ### context() [Section titled “context()”](#context) 
```ts
function context(extra): ToolContext;
```
 #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-1) | Parameter | Type | | --------- | ------------------------------- | | `extra` | [`HandlerExtra`](#handlerextra) | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-1) [`ToolContext`](#toolcontext) *** ### defineTool() [Section titled “defineTool()”](#definetool) 
```ts
function defineTool<Args, Result>(definition): FunctionTool<Args, Result>;
```
 #### Type Parameters [Section titled “Type Parameters”](#type-parameters) | Type Parameter | Default type | | -------------- | ------------ | | []()`Args` | `any` | | []()`Result` | `unknown` | #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-2) | Parameter | Type | | ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `definition` | [`FunctionToolDefinition`](#functiontooldefinition)<`Args`, `Result`> | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-2) [`FunctionTool`](#functiontool)<`Args`, `Result`> *** ### pending() [Section titled “pending()”](#pending) 
```ts
function pending(operationRef, message?): Pending;
```
 Reports that the provider accepted the change but has not finished it. The runner later asks `status` about it with this `operationRef`; it is never shown to the model. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-3) | Parameter | Type | | -------------- | -------- | | `operationRef` | `string` | | `message?` | `string` | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-3) [`Pending`](#pending) *** ### rejected() [Section titled “rejected()”](#rejected) 
```ts
function rejected(message): Rejected;
```
 Reports that the provider definitively did not apply the change, for example because it refused the request. Return it only when that is certain; throw when unsure, and the runner treats the change as unknown. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-4) | Parameter | Type | | --------- | -------- | | `message` | `string` | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-4) [`Rejected`](#rejected) *** ### serveStdio() [Section titled “serveStdio()”](#servestdio) 
```ts
function serveStdio(createServer, options?): Promise<void>;
```
 Runs a package over stdio so local MCP clients and MCP Inspector can use it. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-5) | Parameter | Type | | -------------- | --------------------------------------- | | `createServer` | () => [`McpServerLike`](#mcpserverlike) | | `options?` | [`StdioOptions`](#stdiooptions) | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-5) `Promise`<`void`> *** ### stepUpFromChallenge() [Section titled “stepUpFromChallenge()”](#stepupfromchallenge) 
```ts
function stepUpFromChallenge(header): StepUpRequired | undefined;
```
 Maps a client API’s RFC 9470 step-up challenge (`WWW-Authenticate: Bearer error="insufficient_user_authentication"`) to the result a tool returns; undefined for any other header. `acr_values` lists acceptable Caplo levels and the weakest one listed is required; `max_age` is the freshness in seconds. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-6) | Parameter | Type | | --------- | --------------------------------- | | `header` | `string` \| `null` \| `undefined` | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-6) [`StepUpRequired`](#stepuprequired) | `undefined` *** ### stepUpRequired() [Section titled “stepUpRequired()”](#stepuprequired) 
```ts
function stepUpRequired(requirement?): StepUpRequired;
```
 Reports that the provider refused the call until the customer authenticates again, at `level` or within `maxAuthAgeSeconds`, and applied nothing. A write waits for the customer to confirm on screen and then runs again. #### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-7) | Parameter | Type | | -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | | `requirement?` | [`AuthenticationRequirement`](#authenticationrequirement) | #### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-7) [`StepUpRequired`](#stepuprequired) ## Interfaces [Section titled “Interfaces”](#interfaces) ### Applied [Section titled “Applied”](#applied-1) #### Properties [Section titled “Properties”](#properties) | Property | Modifier | Type | | --------------- | ---------- | ----------- | | []()`[OUTCOME]` | `readonly` | `"applied"` | | []()`result` | `readonly` | `unknown` | *** ### AuthenticationRequirement [Section titled “AuthenticationRequirement”](#authenticationrequirement) #### Properties [Section titled “Properties”](#properties-1) | Property | Type | Description | | ------------------------ | ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | | []()`level?` | `"low"` \| `"standard"` \| `"high"` | - | | []()`maxAuthAgeSeconds?` | `number` | How recently the customer must have authenticated, in seconds. | *** ### CallContext [Section titled “CallContext”](#callcontext) Per-call context the runner places in the `tools/call` request `_meta`. #### Properties [Section titled “Properties”](#properties-2) | Property | Type | Description | | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | []()`conversationId?` | `string` | - | | []()`deadline?` | `number` | Epoch milliseconds. | | []()`idempotencyKey?` | `string` | - | | []()`invocationId?` | `string` | - | | []()`packageVersionId?` | `string` | - | | []()`secrets?` | `Record`<`string`, `string`> | - | | []()`subject?` | [`Subject`](#subject) | The owner-verified customer; [Section titled “status()?”](#status) 
```ts
status?(input, ctx):
  | StatusAnswer
| Promise<StatusAnswer>;
```
 Answers whether an earlier write was applied, for a write whose outcome is unknown or that returned `pending`. `ctx.idempotencyKey` is that write’s key and `operationRef` the reference its `pending` named. Return `applied(result)` when it was applied, `rejected` when it definitively was not, and `pending` while it is still in progress; throw when the provider cannot tell. Any other return leaves the write unknown. ###### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-9) | Parameter | Type | | --------------------- | ------------------------------ | | `input` | { `operationRef?`: `string`; ## Functions [Section titled “Functions”](#functions) ### verifyCustomerContext() [Section titled “verifyCustomerContext()”](#verifycustomercontext)

 ###### Parameters [Section titled “Parameters”](#parameters-1) | Parameter | Type | | ----------- | -------- | | `jti` | `string` | | `expiresAt` | `Date` | ###### Returns [Section titled “Returns”](#returns-1) `boolean` | `Promise`<`boolean`> *** ### VerifyOptions [Section titled “VerifyOptions”](#verifyoptions) #### Properties [Section titled “Properties”](#properties-1) | Property | Type | Description | | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | []()`audience` | `string` | This API’s audience, one of the connection’s API audiences. | | []()`clockToleranceSeconds?` | `number` | Allowed clock skew; default 0. | | []()`issuer` | `string` | The exact issuer shown in the Caplo Console, `https://…/identity/workspaces/<workspaceId>`. | | []()`replayStore?` | [`ReplayStore`](#replaystore) | Rejects a reused token; recommended for write APIs. | | []()`requiredScope?` | `string` | A scope the token must carry. | ## Type Aliases [Section titled “Type Aliases”](#type-aliases) ### CustomerContextLevel [Section titled “CustomerContextLevel”](#customercontextlevel)

# Changelog

> Notable changes to Caplo that affect how you build agents, tools and widgets, and to the caplo CLI.

Release notes for the CLI, with downloads and checksums, are on [GitHub](https://github.com/getcaplo/homebrew-tap/releases). Update with `brew upgrade getcaplo/tap/caplo` or rerun the [installer](/cli/install/). ## November 2026 [Section titled “November 2026”](#november-2026) * **Documentation.** docs.caplo.si launched, with guides for agents, tools, the website widget, provider keys, usage and the CLI. The [CLI reference](/reference/cli/) and the [agent](/reference/agent-yaml/), [caplo.yaml](/reference/caplo-yaml/) and [fixture](/reference/fixtures/) schemas are generated from each CLI release. Every page has a Markdown version and AI prompts for common tasks. * **CLI 0.4.0.** Every command’s help now explains its behavior and shows examples; the same text is the [CLI reference](/reference/cli/).

# Get help

> Where to ask questions, report a problem, or suggest a change to these docs.

* **Questions and problems:** email <ask@caplo.si>. Include your workspace name, the agent or channel involved, and what you expected to happen. For the CLI, add the output of `caplo version` and the full command you ran. * **Talk to Caplo Guide:** the assistant on [caplo.si](https://caplo.si) answers product questions and can book a call with the team. * **Docs fixes:** every page has an **Edit page** link at the bottom, and you can open an issue in [getcaplo/docs](https://github.com/getcaplo/docs/issues). Never send API keys, provider keys or secret values by email or in an issue. If you think a key leaked, rotate it first: see [Bring your own key](/providers/bring-your-own-key/) and [Authentication](/cli/authentication/).
